PREFACE
I, the Chairman of the Department-related Parliamentary Standing Committee on Health and
Family Welfare, having been authorized by the Committee to present the Report on its behalf, do
hereby present this Thirty-fifth Report of the Committee on Action Taken by Government on the
Recommendations contained in the Twenty-seventh Report on Demands for Grants (Demand
No.46) of the Department of Health and Family Welfare, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare for
the year 2008-09.
2. The Twenty-seventh Report of the Department-related Parliamentary Standing Committee on
Health and Family Welfare was presented to Rajya Sabha and laid on the Table of Lok Sabha on
30th April, 2008. Replies of the Government on the recommendations contained in the Report,
received from the Department of Health and Family Welfare were considered by the Committee at
its meeting held on the 26th October, 2009.
3. The Committee considered the draft Report and adopted the same at its meeting held on
the 26th October, 2009.
AMAR SINGH
N
EW D ELHI; Chairman,
October 26 , 2009 Department-related Parliamentary
Kartika 4, 1931 (Saka) Standing Committee on Health and Family Welfare.

1
Part-A
(HEALTH SECTOR)
REPORT
The Report of the Committee deals with the Action Taken by the Government on the
recommendations contained in its Twenty-seventh Report on Demands for Grants (Demand No. 46)
of the Department of Health and Family Welfare for the year 2008-09.
2. Action Taken Notes have been received from the Government in respect of the
recommendations contained in the Report. They have been categorized as follows:
(i) Recommendations/Observations which have been accepted by the Government: 2.4, 2.7,
3.3, 4.4, 6.2, 6.6, 6.9, 7.3, 11.6, 11.7, 12.4, 12.5, 12.6, 12.7, 15.4, 17.9, 18.4, 18.5, 20.4,
20.5, 21.4, 21.5, 21.9, 22.5, 24.1, Total–25 (Chapter-I)
(ii) Recommendations/Observations which Committee does not desire to pursue in view of the
Government’s replies: 2.6, 2.8, 2.9, 2.13, 3.2, 3.4, 3.10, 3.11, 4.2, 4.5, 4.7, 4.8, 5.2, 5.3,
6.4, 6.5, 6.7, 6.8, 6.10, 7.4, 7.6, 8.5, 8.6, 9.2, 10.2, 12.2, 12.3, 12.9, 16.6, 16.7, 16.8,
16.10, 16.12, 17.3, 17.5, 17.7, 19.4, 19.6, 19.8, 22.3, 23.2, 24.2, Tota1–42 (Chapter-II)
(iii) Recommendations/Observations in respect of which replies of the Government have not
been accepted by the Committee: 2.3, 2.11, 2.12, 3.5, 3.6, 3.7, 3.8, 3.9, 3.12, 3.13, 3.14,
4.3, 6.11, 6.12, 6.13, 7.5, 9.3, 10.3, 11.8, 13.2, 14.3, 14.4, 16.5, 16.13, 17.6, 17.8, 21.6,
21.7, 22.2, Total–29 (Chapter-III)
(iv) Recommendations/observations in respect of which final replies of the Government are still
awaited: Nil,  Total–Nil  (Chapter-IV)

2
CHAPTER-I
RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERV ATIONS WHICH
HA VE BEEN ACCEPTED BY THE GOVERNMENT
II. BUDGETARY ALLOCATION
Recommendations/Observations
2.4 The Committee notes that the overall allocation for the health sector (outside NRHM) has
been on the higher side. Still, the Committee is constrained to note that the Department has failed
to project its budgetary requirements in an effective way for the simple reason that the utilization
of funds during the Tenth Plan is far from satisfactory which is evident from the fact that the
sanctioned BE for 2005-06 and 2006-07 was Rs.4253.84 crores and Rs.3564.95 crores,
respectively, which was reduced to Rs.4096.25 crores and Rs.3433.02 crores, respectively at the
RE stage. The actual expenditure figures of Rs.2907.96 crores and Rs.3377.34 crores were even
less than the budgeted allocations at the RE stage. The Committee notes that this trend has
continued during 2007-08 also. This goes to illustrate that the Department’s record in utilization of
the allocated funds is below par. The Committee would, therefore, recommend the Department to
first ensure optimum and judicious utilization of the funds allocated instead of highlighting the
reduced budgetary allocation. The Committee also recommends to the Department to take
substantive steps so that the stumbling blocks in the utilization of sanctioned funds for 2008-09
for a crucial sector like health, are removed.
Action Taken
Noted for compliance.
Recommendations/Observations
2.7 Committee observes that in spite of its raising concerns over this trend of under utilization
of funds year after year, there does not seem to be any noticeable improvement in this regard. Time
and again the Committee has been advocating for higher allocation of funds for this sector and this
time the Committee is happy to note that the approved outlay has seen a 32% hike over the last
year’s Revised Estimate. The Committee reiterates its concern and recommends to the Department
to play a more pro-active role in identifying the problems afflicting the utilization of funds in the
Health Sector and initiate remedial measures from the very beginning of the current financial year so
that the old story of revising estimates downwards and subsequent under utilization does not recur.
Action Taken
Noted for compliance.
III. CENTRAL GOVERNMENT HEALTH SCHEME (CGHS)
Recommendations/Observations
3.3 The Committee, therefore, recommends that allocation of funds under Non-Plan be
enhanced adequately so that the services available to the CGHS beneficiaries are not affected.
2

3
Action Taken
The requirement of additional funds for Non-Plan expenditure by CGHS will be projected to the
Ministry of Finance for discussions in the Pre-Budget meeting to be taken by Secretary (Expenditure).
IV. SAFDARJUNG HOSPITAL AND V ARDHMAN MAHAVEER MEDICAL COLLEGE, NEW
DELHI
Recommendations/Observations
4.4 The Committee observes that procedures and rules are made to streamline, strengthen and
facilitate a process and not create hurdles and bottlenecks. The Committee, therefore, recommends
that effective coordination between the Department and the Hospital authorities is required in this
regard. If required, suitable steps for improvement in the procedural aspect may be initiated so as
to provide better health care delivery system.
Action Taken
In view of enhancement of powers of Additional D.G. and Medical Superintendent for
procurement of equipments to Rs.l.00 crore, the hospital is not finding difficulty in procurement
of equipments as most of the hospital equipments are below Rs.l.00 crore.
VI. ALL INDIA INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL SCIENCES (AIIMS)
Recommendations/Observations
6.2 The Committee was informed that the allocated funds would not be sufficient for
maintaining the infrastructure and patient care services of the Institute. The Committee feels that
the decrease in Non-Plan from Rs.3l0.00 crores in 2007-08 to Rs.290.00 crores is surely going
to affect the patient care services offered by the Institute. The Committee apprehends return of
the ‘user charges regime’ in AIIMS because consumables are the only negotiable component of the
Non-Plan budget as salary and bills have to be paid any way.
Action Taken
A letter has already been written to Director, AIIMS in the month of June, 2008 asking
them to workout their requirement additional funds, if any, which will be considered during
Supplementary demands.
Recommendations/Observations
6.6 The Committee would emphasize that procedural formalities for the centre should be
expedited so as to complete it by the targeted date.
Action Taken
The observation of the Committee has been noted.
Recommendations/Observations
6.9 The Committee expresses deep concern that in the premier institute of the country like
AIIMS so many posts are lying vacant. In the opinion of the Committee, this is definitely bound
to affect the quality of services rendered by the Institute. The Committee, therefore, recommends
immediate action to fill up the vacant posts.
Action Taken
All categories of vacant posts are in process of recruitment.

4
VII. POST GRADUATE INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL EDUCATION AND RESEARCH
(PGIMER), CHANDIGARH
Recommendations/Observations
7.3 The Committee is of the opinion that PGIMER is amongst India’s premier Institutes and its
developmental plans should not be affected in any way due to scarcity of funds. While
recommending that adequate funds should be made available to the PGIMER, Chandigarh to meet
its requirements for developmental works, the Committee also recommends judicious and optimum
utilization of the allocated funds.
Action Taken
The Recommendations of the Committee were/are being complied with strictly.
XI. PRADHAN MANTRI SWASTHYA SURAKSHA YOJANA (PMSSY)
Recommendations/Observations
11.6 The Committee in its 22nd Report on the Demands for Grants (2007-08) presented on
3rd May, 2007 had expressed its apprehension that it would take at least another plan period before
the six AIIMS like Institutions would start functioning in the real sense. The Committee is
constrained to modify its assessment. From the details made available to the Committee,
construction work on the Six AIIMS like Institutions is expected to start by August-
September, 2008 and would be completed in 24 months. Thus, it is more than clear now that only
buildings would be ready by the end of Eleventh Plan. The real target of making available best
health care services to public through these institutions seems to be a distant dream even now. The
Committee finds that tendering process for procurement of medical equipments is at an initial stage.
Process for other allied infrastructure facilities and manpower – both highly qualified/technical as
well as non-technical is yet so start. With regard to up gradation of 13 Medical College Institutions,
the Committee can only hope that work would be completed within the targeted date of 20 months.
The Committee is, however, worried about the fate of upgradation of Grants Medical College and
JJ hospital, Mumbai due to a court case pending in Nagpur High Court in the matter. The
Committee is of the view that the Department should take up other linked activities like arranging
for the faculty and requisite infrastructure simultaneously so that no delay takes place in
operationalization of the Institutions once the construction activity is completed.
Action Taken
Noted. The civil construction work is likely to be completed by the end of 2010. However,
parallel activities for procurement of medical equipment, manpower planning and recruitment,
finalization of governance and management structure as also preparation of curriculum design have
been initiated in 2008. The effort will be to make the hospital functional by the end of 2010 and
Medical College operational by academic year 2011.
Recommendations/Observations
11.7 The Committee also recommends that funds as and when required should be made available
to the Department. The Committee would, however, like to point out that though funds have never
been a problem for this ambitious project of national importance, utilization thereof has been far
from satisfactory level. The Committee, accordingly, recommends setting up of a joint mechanism
of Centre and the concerned States so that greater coordination is achieved and effective
monitoring of the ongoing projects is done. The Committee also calls for greater emphasis on
adhering to the time lines so that the overall cost of the project is not escalated further.

5
Action Taken
All out efforts are being made to fully utilize the funds allocated for the Project. A
Committee under the Chairmanship of Chief Secretaries of the concerned States is being set up to
monitor the progress of setting up of each of the six AIIMS-like institutions.
XII. IMPROVEMENT OF NURSING SERVICES
Recommendations/Observations
12.4 The Committee is not aware about the existence of any Monitoring Committee for such
projects. The Committee is of the view that such a mechanism will prove to be very useful,
especially in view of Rs.198.00 crores allocated for strengthening/upgradation of Nursing Services.
The Committee welcomes the enhancement in the assistance to be provided to Nursing
Institutions from Rs.10.00 lakhs to Rs.25.00 lakhs. Such a move was overdue. The Committee,
however, wonders whether any exercise has been initiated for identification of locations of all the
proposed Centres/Schools/Colleges so far. The Committee would appreciate if implementation of all
these projects is carried out in a time-bound manner. The Committee is not aware about any
assessment/review made recently about the (training both pre and in-service) programmes for
Nurses and also functioning of Training Institutions for Nurses. The Committee is of the view that
such an exercise is necessary for strengthening the Nursing Services which is a very crucial
component of health care.
Action Taken
Noted for compliance. The establishment of Monitoring and Evaluation Cell has been
proposed in the EFC Memo. Although no assessment has been made recently for pre and in-service
training and that of Training Institutions for Nurses but it is envisaged that the Monitoring and
Evaluation Cell when set up would take care of these aspects.
The enhanced grant of Rs.25.00 lakhs is meant for procurement of A.V. AIDS, laboratory
equipment, furniture, library, transport etc. and is need based and would be released to the nursing
institutions on the basis of proposals to be received from the states. Locations for establishing
Centres/Schools/Colleges have been identified. Details are at Annexure-XI.
Recommendations/Observations
12.5 The Committee is happy to note that the National Awards for Nurses, given as a mark of
highest recognition for the meritorious services of the nurses and nursing profession in the
Country, that continued up to 1989, have been revived and named as the National Florence
Nightingale Award from 2007 with the consent of the President of India. 17 Nursing personnel
have been awarded with this prestigious award during the year 2007.
Action Taken
The National Florence Nightingale Awards for the year 2008 have also been distributed by
the President of India to 26 meritorious nursing personnel in the award ceremony held on
12.5.2008 at Rashtrapati Bhavan.
Recommendations/Observations
12.6 The Committee recommends that a suitable mechanism should be evolved for maintaining
a live register of nurses so as to enable the Department for formulating appropriate policy
pertaining to nurses.

6
Action Taken
The Indian Nursing Council is already seized of the matter and will make efforts to devise
suitable mechanism to overcome the problem.
Recommendations/Observations
12.7 The Committee recommends that the Department should make all out efforts including
effective co-ordination and close monitoring so that all the proposals for strengthening of nursing
education are implemented properly and desired results are achieved for getting adequate number
of qualified and trained nursing personnel.
Action Taken
As regards effective coordination and monitoring of the project the Monitoring and
Evaluation Cell as explained in para 12.4 would take care of this aspect.
XV. NATIONAL URBAN HEALTH MISSION (NUHM)
Recommendations/Observations
15.4 The Committee observes that NUHM is one of the most ambitious Projects after National
Rural Health Mission (NRHM). Objectives of the Mission are laudable and are according to the
requirements of the present scenario. The Committee cautions the Department to take all necessary
steps so that the problems afflicting the implementation of NRHM like those related to ground level
healthcare functionaries, availability of doctors, paramedics and infrastructure do not recur and
interfere in the implementation of this parallel scheme meant for urban areas. For achieving the
targets proposed under mission, the Department should do proper homework and work out
modalities for their implementation. The Department should start coordinating with the States right
from the beginning of the project. The Committee would like to be apprised about the progress
made in this regard.
Action Taken
Based on the document on NUHM approved by Minister of Health and Family Welfare, a
draft EFC Memo for NUHM has been circulated to the concerned Ministries for seeking their view/
comments.
The EFC proposal envisages to achieve the objectives of NUHM by strengthening the
extents primary public health systems, rationalising the available manpower and resources, filling
the gaps in service delivery through private partnership through a regulatory frame work and also
through a communitised risk pooling/insurance mechanism with IT enablement, capacity building of
key stake holders and by making special provision for inclusion of the most vulnerable amongst
the poor.
All the State/UT Governments have been requested to start the work of GIS mapping of
the selected cities in TOP PRIORITY basis.
The teams of this Ministry has visited the States of Maharashtra, Madhya Pradesh
Karnataka, Andhra Pradesh, Jharkhand, Chandigarh UT, Haryana, Punjab, Tamil Nadu, West Bengal,
Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand. A presentation on proposed NUHM and the GIS mapping was arranged
and the discussion was also held on various issues pertaining to the NUHM. The officials of
Puducherry were requested to come at Chennai and the officials of A&N Islands were requested
to come at Kolkata to participate in the meeting.

7
The teams would visit other States/UT Governments soon.
XVII. NATIONAL CANCER CONTROL PROGRAMME (NCCP)
Recommendations/Observations
17.9 The Committee recommends to the Department to give utmost priority to evaluation of the
National Cancer Control Programme by a premier agency having experience of work in such
Disease Control Programmes. Proper evaluation would help in identifying weak links and result in
effective implementation of various components of the programme.
Action Taken
This Ministry is inserting an advertisement through DA VP in leading dailies relating to
evaluation of the NCCP (Enquiry for Expression of Interest). This will attract more organizations
in evaluation of the scheme. Efforts will be taken to complete the exercise of the evaluation within
the stipulated time-frame.
XVIII. NATIONAL TOBACCO CONTROL PROGRAMME (NTCP)
Recommendations/Observations
18.4 The Committee was informed last year that the ‘in principle’ approval of the Planning
Commission for setting up of the National Regulatory Authority and Referral Labs for testing Tar
and Nicotine content in various tobacco products has been obtained. Process for seeking approval
of the competent authority had already begun at that time. The Committee, however, notes that no
progress seems to have been made in this regard thereafter. Keeping in view the prolonged process
of getting the approval of the competent authority, the Committee does not have much hope about
the same being obtained by June, 2008. The Department has to make concerted efforts so that
approval of the competent authority for setting up of the National Regulatory Authority, backbone
of the entire programme, is obtained at the earliest.
Action Taken
The Ministry has noted the recommendation for compliance and further necessary action.
Recommendations/Observations
18.5 More than one million people are dying due to consumption of tobacco in the country. The
Committee, therefore, strongly feels that the Ministry needs to bring up sustained Mass Media/IEC
campaign regarding the adverse and harmful effects of tobacco consumption immediately, as also
when NTCP is launched. Sufficient funds should be made available for the same.
Action Taken
The Ministry has noted the recommendation for compliance and further necessary action.
XX. NATIONAL PROGRAMME FOR PREVENTION AND CONTROL OF DIABETES,
CARDIOV ASCULAR DISEASES STROKE (NPDCS)
Recommendations/Observations
20.4 The Committee is happy to note that the pilot phase of the programme has finally taken off
during 2007-08.

8
Action Taken
The budget allocation for 2008-09 is only for Rs.30 crores. In view of this provision the
new districts to be covered in 2008-09 will be 25. State NCD Cells are to be set up in 25 States
and 1 dedicated unit of Cardiovascular Disease/diabetes/stroke Prevention is proposed to be set up.
Recommendations/Observations
20.5 The Committee notes that out of an approved outlay of Rs.1660.50 crores for the Eleventh
Five Year Plan, only Rs.30.00 crores have been earmarked for 2008-09. This, when taken into
account along with last year’s utilization of Rs.4.42 crores, leaves behind a whooping amount of
Rs.1625.50 crores for the last three years of the plan. The Committee is given to understand that
a steering Committee has been established to review and expedite the Programme. A meeting of the
States participating in the pilot project was held on 17th October, 2007. Appreciating this initiative,
the Committee recommends to the Department to carry forward the programme vigorously during
the remaining period of the Plan so that the set targets are achieved with the optimum utilization
of funds available for the programme.
Action Taken
The recommendations of the Committee have been noted and all out efforts would be made
to comply with the same.
XXI. MEDICAL EDUCATION
Recommendations/Observations
21.4 The Committee is aware that keeping in view the shortage of doctors and existing medical
institutions not being adequate enough, MCI norms for opening of colleges/increase of seats need
to be reviewed. The Committee, however, feels that this exercise should be undertaken with
extreme caution so that objective of imparting quality medical education remains intact. Relaxing
norms by allowing private promoters to have land in two pieces within 10 Kms. of each other and
allowing corporate entities to setup medical colleges are initiatives which should be implemented
after examining all pros and cons. The Committee is of the opinion that in the exercise of relaxation
of existing norms, assessment of those experts having the experience of this field should be given
due weightage.
Action Taken
The observations of the Committee will be kept in view while finalizing the regulations of
MCI.
Recommendations/Observations
21.5 The Committee also takes note of the suggestion of the Department to MCI for relaxing
the age of retirement of staff of Private Medical Educational Institutes from 65 to 70 years, subject
to their physical ability, in order to fill-up the acute shortage of teaching staff in that sector.
Secretary, Department of Health and Family Welfare has also informed the Committee that a
Cabinet Note has been prepared for increasing the age of retirement to 65 years in Government
Medical Educational Institutes. Committee is also given to understand that PG degrees of USA, UK,
Australia, New Zealand and Canada have been recognized and for other countries like Germany and
France the matter is under consideration. The idea is that they can come and settle here or they
can come as visiting faculties. The Committee welcomes these steps.

9
Action Taken
Noted.
Recommendations/Observations
21.9 The Committee opines that if implemented, the recommendations of the Commission would
be a milestone in improving the standards and quality of medical education in India. The Committee
recommends that action may be initiated in this regard and Committee apprised accordingly.
Action Taken
Noted.
XXII. V ACANCIES IN CHS CADRE
Recommendations/Observations
22.5 The Committee views with serious concern the grave injustice done to the GDMO cadre
for no fault of theirs. Inspite of their being eligible for specialist post, continuance of such doctors
in GDMO cadre is indeed against all principles of natural justice. Non-implementation of Fifth Pay
Commission recommendation of SAG level positions in All India Service to be 17% of total cadre
has created another hurdle in their career progression. The Committee finds that Sixth Pay
Commission’s recommendations of filling up the additional posts to be created for fulfilling any gap,
by direct recruitment, will make the position of GDMOs very vulnerable. The Committee is of the
firm opinion that the Department should take up this issue immediately with the Ministry of
Finance. Every attempt should be made to remove the grave injustice done to the GDMO cadre
so that this sub-cadre of professionals gets due recognition and morale boost like others in All India
Services.
Action Taken
(i) In the new proposed CHS Rules, provision is being proposed for lateral entry of
General Duty Medical Officers with Post-graduate qualification in the Specialist
sub-cadre.
(ii) A proposal is under reference to COS for creating/upgrading 17% of total Senior Duty
posts in CHS for SAG level.
XXIV. MISCELLANEOUS
Recommendations/Observations
24.1 The Committee recommends the Department to take care of the eco-friendly part of the
whole exercise.
Action Taken
Pradhan Mantri Swasthya Suraksha Yojana (PMSSY) envisages setting up of six AIIMS-like
institutions and upgradation of the existing 13 medical college institutions.
Insofar as setting up of AIIMS-like institutions is concerned, the designs prepared by the
Design-DPR Consultants were got evaluated by faculties of PGIMER/AIIMS for functional
efficiency. The Energy Research Institute (TERI) has appraised the project for incorporating green
building concepts and best practices at the design stage. This will ensure a functionally efficient,

10
eco-friendly building. The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare proposes to take up Green Rates
for Integrated Habitat Assessment (GRIHA), as advised by the Ministry of New and Renewable
Energy. Bureau of Energy Efficiency (BEE) have also evaluated the designs for ECBC (Energy
Conservation Building Codes) compliance, to ensure that the energy saving measures have been
incorporated in the buildings.
W APCOS, Government of India Undertaking had conducted Environment Impact
Assessment (EIA) study for setting up of AIIMS-like institutions. Ministry of Environment and
Forests is examining the proposals for granting environmental clearance. The recommendations of
the MoEF to recycle water and use energy saving features have been taken into consideration by
the Design-DPR Consultants. All efforts are being made to ensure that the eco-system of the place
is not adversely impacted by the setting up of the new AIIMS-like institutions

11
CHAPTER-II
RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERV ATIONS WHICH THE COMMITTEE DOES
NOT DESIRE TO PURSUE IN VIEW OF THE GOVERNMENT'S REPLIES
II. BUDGETARY ALLOCATION
Recommendations/Observations
2.6 The Committee would like to point out that expenditure reported upto 29th February, 2008
was only Rs.1608.59 crores and within a span of three weeks it has reached to Rs.2121.24 crores.
This clearly indicates that major utilization is taking place in the last quarter of the year.
Action Taken
While this Ministry shares the views of the Committee, efforts are being made to streamline
the pace of expenditure as per Cash Management Plan.
Recommendations/Observations
2.8 The Committee views with serious concern the under utilization of funds on account of
slow pace of expenditure on implementation of schemes like Pradhan Mantri Swasthya Suraksha
Yojna (PMSSY), Cancer, Mental Health, Capacity Building Project for Food and Drugs, non-
implementation of the recommendations of the Oversight Committee, New Initiatives, and also non-
finalizations of proposals relating to procurement of various equipments, non-filling up of vacant
posts, non-receipt of proposals from State Government/Implementing Agencies etc. The Committee
recommends the Department to keep the activities of these schemes/institutions under constant
monitoring so as to arrest this trend of under utilization.
Action Taken
The concern of the Committee is fully shared by the Ministry. Implementation of the
schemes mentioned is being accordingly monitored on a regular basis by Secretary (H&FW) and
in the context of the observations of the Committee will be examined for such action as considered
necessary to ensure proper utilization of funds.
Recommendations/Observations
2.9 The pendency level of Utilization Certificates has been engaging the attention of the
Committee for the last few years. According to latest figures as on 1st March, 2008, there were
as many as 1318 Utilization Certificates in respect of Health Sector amounting to Rs.677.61 crores
for the grants released upto 2005-06. On a specific query regarding any plans of the Department
to reach a justifiable level of pendency, the Committee is given to understand that a communication
was sent by the Chief Controller of Accounts (CCA) to all the States through Resident
Commissioners on this issue giving full details of pending UCs in respect of the Department of
Health and Family Welfare. The Committee is dismayed to note that in the name of efforts; only
a letter has been issued. The Committee feels that spirited efforts, which are apparently lacking,
need to be made on priority basis to clear the UCs which are pending for the last three decades
11

12
or so. The Committee also recommends to the Department to take up the case with concerned
State Governments at the highest level so as to assess whether the funds have been utilized
properly or not.
Action Taken
The Department is taking Monthly review of outstanding Utilisation Certificates besides
issuing periodical reminders at the appropriate level for early clearance. In this regard a DO was
issued by Secretary (H&FW) to all Principal Secretaries of the States for submission of pending
utilization certificates on priority basis. The fresh grants were not released during the year 2007-08
to the Grantees having outstanding utilization certificates against them. By such efforts, the 1024
Utilisation Certificates amounting to Rs.1176.04 crores were liquidated during the year 2007-08
in respect of Health Section. As on 1st May, 2008, 973 utilisation certificates amounting to
Rs.550.77 crores were outstanding in respect of Department of Health and Family Welfare for the
Grants released up to 2006-07.
Recommendations/Observations
2.13 The Committee’s attention has been drawn by high level of pendency of Audit paras
contained in CAG Report and Internal Audit on matters relating to the Department. The Committee
is surprised to note that there are 62 pending Audit paras dating back from CAG Report No. 2 of
1998 to the latest CAG Report No. 20 of 2007 on the Performance Audit of procurement of
medicines and medical equipment. The Committee fails to understand the reasons for such high
level of pendency. The very fact that such a large number of Audit paras remain to be reconciled
indicates that internal financial management of the Ministry is weak. The Committee would like to
be apprised about the corrective measures taken in the light of the pending Audit paras.
Action Taken
Fresh proposal will be submitted to take up the audit of all centrally sponsored programmes,
PSUs under the department and autonomous bodies. As regards P.G.I. Chandigarh, the audit was
conducted by the Internal Audit Party in the Financial Year 2007-08 but the audit of AIIMS could
not be conducted and postponed until further order during the month of July, 2007. It is further
stated that the Scheme Audit of National Vector Borne Disease Programme and Central Government
Health Scheme was also conducted in the Financial Year 2007-08. To undertake the audit of more
centrally sponsored health programmes and PSUs and autonomous bodies during the Financial
Year 2008-09 the planning of the same is included in the proposal.
The following steps have been taken for strengthening of Internal Audit Wing of this
Ministry:
1. Training on Internal Audit at INGAF: One Accounts Officer and two Assistant
Accounts Officer and one Senior Accountant have been nominated for the proposed
specialized training at INGAF.
2. IT and System Audit: The instructions regarding outsourcing the IT and system audit
through INGAF wherein a pool of trained manpower are to be developed to conduct
special audit are noted for implementation in this Ministry.
3. Diversity in Audit Techniques: Internal Audit Wing adopted diversity in audit techniques
such as physical inspection, performance evaluation, process review, risk analysis etc.
4. Follow-up of Audit Report: Instructions contained in audit manual is strictly followed
to follow-up the audit reports. The Internal Audit peruse the Inspection Report and

13
ensure the settlement of objection including in the reports, reminders are also issued
towards the settlement of outstanding objections.
5. Annual Review also submitted each year to higher authorities
III. CENTRAL GOVERNMENT HEALTH SCHEME (CGHS)
Recommendations/Observations
3.2 The Committee notes that Plan allocation for 2008-09 is at an enhanced level when
compared with provision of Rs.40.00 crores made for 2007-08. As per the Outcome Budget (2008-09),
against a Plan allocation of Rs.40.00 crores for 2007-08 under CGHS; expenditure reported upto
29.02.08 is only Rs.19.08 crores. The Committee is, however, surprised to note that in response
to a specific query made by it, utilisation of Rs.32.16 crores was reported upto February, 2008.
The Committee is unhappy with the conflicting figures of utilization of Plan funds. Either the
information published in the Outcome Budget is incorrect or inaccurate status report has been
furnished to the Committee, that too on a specific query made by it. Both the positions compel
the Committee to conclude that there is a need for better management at the Department level. The
Committee apprehends that chances are there that major utilization under CGHS has taken place
during the last quarter of the year. If it is true, this indicates improper handling of the funds. The
trend of utilization of major chunk of funds during the last quarter is not a healthy sign. The
Committee would like to be apprised about utilization of Plan and Non-Plan funds (quarter-wise)
during 2007-08. In the light of last year’s experience of utilization of funds, the Committee
recommends that concerted efforts need to be made for optimum and judicious utilization of
enhanced allocation made for 2008-09.
Action Taken
V ariations between the two sets of figures for the expenditure upto 29.2.2008 is attributed
to the fact that the Departmental figures communicated comprise expenditure in the pipeline also
whereas those reported in the Outcome Budget were the figures actually booked by the Accounting
Wing.

3.4 The computerization of CGHS dispensaries and offices in Delhi was taken up at an
expenditure of Rs.8.74 crores. The Committee was informed that as on 26.3.2008, 83 dispensaries

14
in Delhi have been fully computerized and the process of computerization of remaining 4
dispensaries was under way. The Committee would like to point out that computerization process
of CGHS dispensaries in Delhi could not be completed as scheduled. Originally targeted to be
completed by 2006-07, revised date of 17 August, 2007 could also not be adhered to. By the end
of financial year, 2007-08, 4 dispensaries in Delhi still remained to be computerized.
Computerization of CGHS dispensaries located in Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, Hyderabad, Nagpur
and Pune to be taken up in 2007-08 as scheduled, is yet to start. The Committee can imagine the
status of third phase slated to be completed in 2008-09. The Committee, keeping in view the past
experience in respect of Delhi dispensaries, can only emphasize that all steps need to be taken to
complete the computerization of dispensaries outside Delhi at the earliest. Corrective measures
based on the problem areas noticed in the computerization of dispensaries in Delhi need to be taken.

STATUS OF COMPUTERIZATION PROJECT IN DELHI

connection is provided, operations shall start.
At Ghaziabad and Vivek Vihar, Dispensaries are to be shifted to new premises.
At Shakurbasti, renovation of building is nearly complete and ready for starting operations
any time after that.

3.10 The Committee understands that third party administrators for processing of claims and
medical audit are also proposed to be introduced. The Committee has been given to understand that

The SIU in their report have recommended that the posts of Safaiwala and Chowkidar only

D in the CGHS has been recommended for outsourcing.
Recommendations/Observations
3.11 The Committee views with serious concern the growing number of vacancies in different
categories of posts in CGHS dispensaries. The Committee understands that all surplus posts
identified in cities outside Delhi have been abolished. It has also been informed that the staff
strength in the dispensaries is decided as per the workload. The very fact that at the present
juncture, no CGHS city can be stated to have full complement of the staff strength as per the SIU
norms establishes that the existing dispensaries are not functioning as envisaged. The Committee
also strongly feels that outsourcing /hiring of staff on contract-basis on a big scale is a remedy
worse than the disease. The concept of public private partnership cannot be applied across the

15
board specially in a set-up meant for providing health care. The Committee would like to have a

dispensaries.

IV. SAFDARJUNG HOSPITAL AND V ARDHMAN MAHAVEER MEDICAL COLLEGE, NEW
DELHI
Recommendations/Observations
4.2 The Committee, however, notes that upto 29.02.2008 expenditure of only Rs.53.77 crores
could be reported.
Action Taken
Allocation under PLAN during the Financial Year 2007-08 was raised from Rs.51.00 crore
(B.E.) to Rs.73.50 crore (R.E.). The expenditure of Rs.53.77 could be reported mainly due to
following reasons:
• Rs.19.00 crore was allotted (Under Major Head–4210 PLAN SJH), SJH only during the
month of August/September, 2007 in 1st Batch of Supplementary Grant. This resulted
in delay in starting the process of procurement of Machinery and Equipment.
Moreover, debits of Rs.3.00 crore (approx.) against LCs opened were still awaited.
Similarly, placement of funds under Major Works was delayed due to allotment funds
in 1st Batch of Supplementary grants instead of B.E. 2007-08.
• Rs.5.00 crore (Under Major Head–4210 PLAN VMMC) was unutilized upto
29th February, 2008 as sanction of placement of funds to HSCC was received in the
month of March, 2008 from Ministry of Health and Family Welfare. Leaving aside the
above, hospital expenditure was about 88% against the rest of the allocation under the
PLAN budget upto February, 2008. In this respect, it is further stated that against
allocated F. E. 2007-08 of Rs.73.72 crore, Rs.70.64 crore was utilized. The balance
of Rs.3.00 crore (approx.) could not be utilized due to non receipt of debits upto
31.03.2008 against LCs opened in the year 2007-08 for procurement of Machinery and
Equipment.

Recommendations/Observations
4.5 The Committee is happy to note that scheme of redevelopments of hospitals including
redevelopment of Safdarjung Hospital with the proposal to increase its bed strength from 1531 to
3000 has finally been included in the approved schemes for the Eleventh Plan. HSCC has submitted
a final report on ‘Redevelopment Plan of Safdarjung Hospital’ to the Department for consideration.
The Department has started work for engaging consultants for preparation of DPR for the detailed
redevelopment plan. The Committee would like to point out that ‘in-principle’ approval by the
Planning Commission has already taken considerable time. The Committee therefore, urges the
Department to make wholehearted efforts to expedite the procedural formalities at the earliest. The
redevelopment plan is required to be started without any further delay, keeping in view the ever-
increasing patient load on the Safdarjung Hospital. The Committee recommends that scarcity of
funds and lack of coordination should not come in the way of its timely completion and the
Department should make every conceivable effort in this regard.
Action Taken
Planning Commission has already conveyed ‘in principle’ approval to the project which has
been included in the Eleventh Five Year Plan. The feasibility report including the issues pertaining
to public private partnership in the proposed redevelopment of the Hospital has already been
prepared with an estimated expenditure of Rs.4150 crores including recurring cost to be completed
within a period of 6 years. The Ministry has also initiated action to appoint a Transaction Advisor
to select a suitable Design Consultant for working out the detailed drawing and plans for the
redevelopment of the Safdarjung Hospital. The draft EFC Memo is under finalization. The same has
been considered by the Integrated Finance Division of the Ministry and has been circulated to
MOF, Planning commission, M/o Urban Development, Government of NCT of Delhi and others for
their comments. Once comments from these bodies are received, Secretary (Expenditure) will be
requested to convene meeting of the EFC.
Recommendations/Observations
4.7 The Committee expresses its serious concern on the slow progress made with regard to
setting up of Renal Transplantation Unit. Status of the project for IVF unit is more discouraging as
‘in-principle’ approval is yet to come. The Committee can only emphasize that the Renal
Transplantation Unit should be made functional by the year end by closely monitoring the progress
of the same. Procedural formalities with regard to IVF unit also need to be completed at the earliest.
Action Taken
The progress in setting up the Renal Transplantation unit and for IVF unit is in full swing.
Some of the Machinery and Equipments have already been procured and rest is likely to be
procured within 2-3 months. However, it is to be mentioned here that to start Renal Transplant
Unit, the hospital needs licence from Government. In this regard, an inspection has been carried
out on 16.07.2008 on the available infrastructural facilities as well as and Specialists. The
inspection Committee has pointed out that the post of functional Nephrologist i.e. D.M. in
Nephrology is a must for the functioning of the Unit which is not presently available. Necessary
action would be taken to fulfill this requirement for starting the Unit.
Recommendations/Observations
4.8 The Committee’s attention has been drawn by a large number of vacancies in different
categories of posts in Safdarjung Hospital. Out of the total sanctioned staff strength of 4781, there

17
were 1054 vacant posts in different categories as on July, 2007. The Committee has been given
to understand that workload of the Hospital has progressively increased manifold in the past few
years. The doctor-patient ratio has inversely grown, there is an acute need of more doctors per
patient and related infrastructure including sub-staff. In such a scenario, with the redevelopment
project of Safdarjung Hospital likely to take off at the earliest, additional staff strength of all the
categories would be required. The Committee, accordingly, recommends that the Department in co-
ordination with hospital authorities should chalk out an action-plan for acquiring the required
manpower.
Action Taken
Action has already been initiated for the revival/creation/filling up of the vacant posts in SJH
and VMMC. Recently, 214 posts of Sr. Residents and 120 posts of Jr. Residents in various
departments of SJH and VMCC has already been created with approval of MOF.
V. DR. RAM MANOHAR LOHIA HOSPITAL, NEW DELHI
Recommendations/Observations
5.2 The Committee is constrained to note that inspite of an assurance that each Central Institute
will have a separate monitoring Committee, given by the Secretary, H&FW before the Committee
last year, no action seems to have been taken. The Committee, therefore, reiterates that a proper
monitoring mechanism needs to be put in place so as to arrest this trend of sub-optimal utilization
of the funds.
Action Taken
It may be clarified that during 2007-08 the actual expenditure upto 29.02.2008
was Rs.64.38 crores and not Rs.56.60 crore as reported by the Committee against RE of
Rs.71.95 crore. A Project Monitoring Committee has also been constituted to oversee utilization of
funds along with other things for PGIMER.
Recommendations/Observations
5.3 The Committee has been observing that a number of projects – big and small, envisaged
for strengthening and augmenting the existing services in the premier Hospital, continue to remain
in the implementation status with very little progress being made. The Committee has been
constrained to point out that the year 2007-08 was also no exception. Funds allocated remained
under-utilised with the following three major projects running behind schedule:—
• Strengthening of Accidents and Emergency Department and setting up of Trauma Care
Centre
• Setting up of PGIMER at Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia Hospital
• Upgradation of School of Nursing into College of Nursing
All these projects were to be completed in 2007-08 only. The Committee notes that
academic session for PG Courses in Medicine, Paediatrics and Anesthesia is to begin in 2008-09.
While welcoming the starting of PGIMER at Dr. RML Hospital, the Committee would appreciate
if the PGI becomes fully operational running all the courses with the required infrastructure and
regular staff in place. The Committee apprehends that there is little chance of Trauma Care Centre
becoming operational in 2008-09 with appointment of 178 additional staff yet to be made and
process of purchase of equipment and consumable going on. The Committee also finds that not

18
much progress seems to have been made in the upgradation of School of Nursing into College of
Nursing. The target remains the same in 2008-09 also as in the previous year.
Action Taken
The status report of the major three projects as mentioned above is given under:
(i) Strengthening of Accidents and Emergency Department and setting up of Trauma
Centre – It may be informed that the aforesaid scheme has since been executed during
2007-08 itself and the Trauma Care Centre has been inaugurated by the Chief Minister
of Delhi in the presence of Hon’ble Health Minister and other dignitaries on 19.3.08.
The Trauma Care Centre has since started functioning. The recruitment of most of the
staff has since been done on regular/contract basis, as per the statement enclosed at
Annexure-V. The proposal for creation of 418 additional staffs for the Trauma Centre
is under process.
(ii) Setting up of PGIMER at Dr. RML Hospital – The Committee has noted that the
academic session for PG Courses in Medicine, Paediatrics and Anesthesia is to begin
in 2008-09. It may be informed that the aforesaid PG Courses, including the above
disciplines, have since been started w.e.f. June this year, as per number of seats and
their allocation/affiliation given at Annexure-VI. The committee has also commented
that it would appreciate if the PGIMER becomes fully operational running all the
courses with the required infrastructure and regular staff in place. It may be noted that
efforts are being made to ensure that the new building, infrastructural facilities along
with required specialists/staffs in PGIMER are made fully functional and put in place
on priority.
(iii) Upgradation of School of Nursing into College of Nursing – The Upgradation of
School of Nursing to College of Nursing in the hospital is an approved plan activity
of Eleventh Five Year Plan. It has been decided by the Ministry/DGHS to start B. Sc.
Degree Course in Nursing in place of existing Diploma in General Nursing and
Midwifery being provided by the School of Nursing, with 50 annual intake capacity in
Central Government Hospitals including Dr. RML Hospital from the current Academic
Session 2008-09. The hospital has already approached the GGSIP University
for affiliation of B.Sc. Degree Course in Nursing from the current academic
session 2008-09. The “In-Principle” approval of the Indian Nursing Council (INC) has
also been received on 27.04.2008 to upgrade the School of Nursing to College of
Nursing from the academic year 2008-09 with 50 students intake. The ongoing
construction/renovation of building for the College of Nursing started by the HSCC in
March, 2008 is likely to be completed by October, 2008. The hospital is planning to
take admission from this Academic Session (2008-09) for B.Sc. Hons (Nursing).
VI. ALL INDIA INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL SCIENCES (AIIMS)
Recommendations/Observations
6.4 The Committee is constrained to observe that enhanced outlay for the Eleventh Plan period
does not seem to have any visible impact. Plan provision of Rs.200.00 crores for 2007-08 not only
remains at the same level as in the previous Plan period but has also been brought down to
Rs.160.00 crores at the RE stage with actual expenditure reported up to 29.02.2008 being only
Rs.198.00 crores. The Committee is not able to comprehend the reasons for under-utilization of
funds duly allocated for specific purposes. Failure on the part of a premier institute like AIIMS to
utilize the funds as targeted indicates that problem areas continue to persist both in the co-
ordination between the administrative Ministry and the Institute and its functioning.

19
Action Taken
The Institute has received grant of Rs.160.00 crores under Plan for the year 2007-08 and
the same have been fully utilized.
Minister of Health and Family Welfare, as President of the Institute, has taken a meeting
with all the Head of Departments of All India Institute of Medical Sciences on 17.03.2008 and there
is no lack of co-ordination between the Ministry and the Institute.
Recommendations/Observations
6.5 The Committee hopes that position would improve now.
Action Taken
The Ministry is committed to extend all the cooperation and help to the Institute for
improving its quality of patient care services, research and education.
Recommendations/Observations
6.7 The Committee also takes note of major new initiatives for which plans were under
preparation during 2007-08 as listed below:—
➢ Construction of new PC Block
➢ Advance facilities for Paediatrics (Medical) and (Surgery)
➢ Advance facilities for Geriatrics
➢ Facility for Family Medicine and Public Health
Action Taken
• Construction of new PC Block – A proposal for construction of new PC and Teaching
Block had been perceived to augment the existing space and infrastructure requirement
for the planned expansion of UG/PG seats at AIIMS. The proposal for consideration
of the EFC has been prepared by AIIMS and submitted to the Ministry in August, 2007
for an estimated cost of Rs.95.29 crores. The Ministry examined the proposals and
asked AIIMS to revise the cost estimate with rescheduling the date of completion. The
proposal is yet to be revived in the Ministry. Upon receipt of approval of the EFC
proposal and approval of the master plan of AIIMS, which is being taken up in parallel,
the project shall be taken up for execution.
• Advance facilities for Pediatrics (Medical) and (Surgery) – The proposal had been
conceived as part of Mother and Child Centre wherein it was planned to integrate the
facilities of the Department of Gynecology and Obstetrics, Pediatrics and Pediatrics
Surgery with the total bed strength of 500 beds. Initially, the proposal was prepared
at an estimated cost (as on August, 2007) of Rs.317.40 crores. The proposal has been
modified as per advice of the Ministry. The modified proposal is yet to be submitted
by AIIMS. Now the project cost has been revised to Rs.285.78 crores and the project
schedule is to be completed by March, 2011. Subject to approval from the CCEA and
revision of Master Plan of AIIMS, project shall be taken up as schedule.
• Advances facilities for Geriatrics and Facility for Family Medicine and Public Health –
The master plan of AIIMS is under revision whereas it is planned to expand the area
by relocating the existing two storied houses in the main Ansari Nagar Campus. The said
work has been entrusted to CPWD who had indicated that the master plan shall be got

20
prepared by October year. In the said master plan it is conceived to establish super
specialty health care center comprising of Advance facility for geriatrics and of Centre
and Family Medicine and Public Health. In the scheduled plan activities in the Eleventh
five year plan the centers and facilities included are, the already ones which have higher
priority with respect to functionalities. The said two projects as above are stated to be
taken up in the next five-year plan. However the concept planning and the type of design
required for the Master plan of the said two centers shall be included in the current year
itself Plan of the said two centres shall be included in the current year itself.
Recommendations/Observations
6.8 The Committee finds that another set of new projects has been listed to be taken during
2008-09. The Committee strongly feels that new initiatives should be taken up with priority being
fixed for the most needed ones. The Committee would appreciate if these initiatives do not continue
to be on paper during 2008-09. The Committee recommends that funds provided for the AIIMS
in the Eleventh Plan show both optimum and judicious utilization as per the action plan with
additional provision as and when required. The Committee is of the view that an effective
monitoring mechanism to be effected by all concerned both at the institute and the Ministry level
needs to be in place for the purpose at the earliest.
Action Taken
The Institute has informed that they have constituted a Committee consisting of different
Heads from different discipline and they are of the opinion that an effective monitoring mechanism
is effected in the AIIMS.
Recommendations/Observations
6.10 The Committee strongly feels that position is bound to aggravate further specially in view
of increasing number of doctors and nurses leaving the Institute in the recent years. The rate of
exodus of doctors and nurses from the Institute is a cause of serious concern. The Department
needs to devise a strategy immediately to arrest this trend. The Committee would like to be
informed of the remedial steps taken in this regard.
Action Taken
There is no exodus of faculty from AIIMS. The trend seen over the last ten years shows
that about 7-8 doctors leave AIIMS every year. The trend may be seen in the background of
coming up of new corporate hospitals in and around Delhi during this period. The faculty members
who had left the institute during the last ten years have either taken voluntary retirement or
resigned from the service of the Institute “ON THEIR PERSONAL GROUNDS”.
It is stated that during the year 2008, only one faculty member has taken voluntary
retirement from the service of the Institute so far. However, in order to stem this tendency,
numbers of facilities/allowances are being extended to the faculty members of the Institute, which
are not available for faculty members working in other institutions. A copy of the details of such
facilities/allowances is at 
Annexure-VII.
VII. POST GRADUATE INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL EDUCATION AND RESEARCH
(PGIMER), CHANDIGARH
Recommendations/Observations
7.4 The Committee takes a serious note of the following projects originally scheduled to be
completed during the Tenth Plan, which are not seeing the light of day and are continuing in the
Eleventh Plan also with their scheduled dates of completion being revised once again:—

21
ANNEXURE-A OF REPORT
The Committee is of the view that there is some inherent problem in the implementation of
projects, namely, the Advanced Cardiac Centre, Setting up of additional 66KV sub-stations and
Advanced Trauma Centre. The Committee in its earlier Report had critically reviewed
implementation of these projects but it appears that no concrete steps have been taken so far. The
projects continue to remain behind schedule with the final expenditure going beyond the sanctioned
cost. The Committee would like to be informed about the remedial measures taken by the Institute
in this regard.
Action Taken
The detailed status alongwith the reasons for delay in respect of Advanced Cardiac Centre,
66KVA Sub Stat ion and Advanced Trauma Centre is as under:—
I. ADV ANCED CARDIAC CENTRE
Brief History – The work was originally approved in 41st SEC held on 22.12.97 and
Expenditure Sanction was obtained vide EFC meeting held on 28.04.2000 for an amount of
Rs.2072.00 lakh. The approval of DNIT and Technical Sanction of the estimate was
obtained in Eleventh PIC held on 01.08.2001 vide agenda item no. 6. Because of the
inadequate provision in the original scope of work and changes/ additions in the scope, the
1st and the 2nd revised cost sanctions were necessitated.
The sanction for 1st Revised cost estimate was obtained by the SFC meeting held on
13.07.2005 and ratified by GB vide no. E3/2005/5985 dated 11.08.2005 for an amount of
Rs.2964.00 lakh and for 2nd Revised cost estimate vide letter no. V .17020/34.2006-ME.II
dated 04.09.06 for and amount of Rs.610.00 lakh.
Reasons for Delay
1. Cost enhanced due to increase in the plinth area from 2,60,000 sq. ft. to 2,85,000 sq.ft.
2. Inadequate provision in the original scope of work i.e. in civil Works, Fire Fighting
and Fire Alarm, Electrical works, air Conditioning etc.
3. Initially, air conditioning was proposed only in main Operation Theatres, Cath labs,
Emergency and ICU’s. Now it has been extended to all the patient care areas, surgical/
Medical OPD, EMGE may, Blood Bank, Admn. block, Private rooms, wards, labs and
diagnostic areas, OPD, Faculty offices etc.
4. EFC approval was given in April, 2000 for Rs.2072.00 lakh first revised amount was
approved on 11.8.2005 and second revised cost was approved on 04.9.2006 which
was necessitated because of in adequate provision initially and changes/addition in
scope. This resulted in the delay in execution.
Present Status – The building shall be completed by August, 2008 as per scope of 2nd
revised cost estimate.
II. 66 KV SUB STATION
Brief History – The Chandigarh administration has been asking to take one point supply for
all electrical load required for existing as well as various new buildings/centre coming in the
institute.
The work was administratively approved amounting to Rs.609.00 lakh in the 47th SEC
committed dated 17.11.2005. This work was awarded to BBMB as a Deposit Work.

22
Reasons for delay
1. The BBMB invited the open tenders through press and the tender opening date had to
be extended 3 times because of the poor response and finally when the tenders were
received the rates of lowest bidder was much higher then the estimated cost.
2. The BBMB referred the matter to PGI, to give revised approval based on the tender
cost. Accordingly the case was taken up for revised administrative approval to various
bodies of the institute such as Standing Estate Committee, Standing Finance
Committee, Governing Body and finally EFC and planning commission sanctioned the
revised cost estimate of Rs.1174.00 crore on 13th March, 2007.
3. Thereafter the BBMB was allowed to take up the execution of this work.
Present status – The work is likely to be completed at site in July, 2008.
III. ADV ANCED TRAUMA CENTER
Brief History - This is Ninth Plan project amounting to Rs.10.00 crore sanctioned in
April, 2000. This work could not start at site due change in scope of work i.e. addition/
deletion of O.T. Block and other various administrative reasons. Revised administrative
approval was obtained on 21st February, 2006 amounting to Rs.1745.00 lakh and
immediately thereafter, the work was awarded to CPWD as a Deposit Work. The actual
construction started at site in August, 2007.
Reason for delay
(a) Changes in the scope of work.
(b) Initially this work was given to CPWD as a deposit work later on this work was put
to tender by Engineering wing of the Institute.
(c) Revised administrative approval was obtained on the 21st February, 2006.
(d) This work was again given to CPWD as a deposit work since the Engineering wing
was occupied with other major construction activities in the campus.
Present status – This work is likely to be completed in December, 2008.
Recommendations/Observations
7.6 The Committee observes that vacancies in a premier medical educational institute like
PGIMER, Chandigarh will affect the quality of medical professionals passing out from there. The
Committee urges the Institute to take all conceivable measures so as to fill up the vacant posts at
the earliest. The Committee also impresses upon the Institute to augment the staff strength by
providing better perks/salary and incentives along with conducive working environment for them.
Action Taken
The category-wise reply in respect of staff is as under:—
Faculty Posts:–Faculty posts includes Professor, Additional Professor, Associate Professor
and Assistant Professors. 65 posts of faculty were filled up during December, 2007/
January, 2008. Further, it is informed that 15 posts of Professor and 63 posts of Assistant
Professor are being notified (including vacancies arise during the period on account of new
creation retirement/resignation etc.). The process for the recruitment will be completed
within next six months.

23
Senior/Junior Residents:–The posts of Senior and Junior Residents are tenure posts and
filled up twice a year by open advertisement. Presently 106 posts of Senior Residents have
been notified and shall be filled up by June, 2008.
Non Faculty Posts:–There are 485 vacant posts but due to ban imposed by the Government
of India, clearance of Screening Committee of Ministry has been sought. 62 posts of non
faculty have been referred to the Government of India for clearance of Screening
Committee. As and when the clearance will be received from Ministry, the vacancies will
be notified accordingly.
Nursing:–99 posts of Nursing staff was advertised and the recruitment is under process
which is likely to be completed within next 2-3 months. Moreover, it is also added that the
vacancies of nursing staff is arising due to high attrition rate i.e. generally 8-10 nurses are
leaving the job every month for lucrative job opportunities abroad on the one pretext or the
other.
Paramedical Staff:–The Departmental Promotion Committee for the promotional posts are
under process. After finishing the process of DPC, the net available vacancies at the feeder
cadre will be notified for direct recruitment.
Teaching (Lecturer/Tutor Technician etc.):–All the 14 posts have been notified, applications
received and likely to be filled up shortly.
VIII. NORTH EASTERN INDIRA GANDHI REGIONAL INSTITUTE OF HEALTH AND
MEDICAL SCIENCES (NEIGRIHMS), SHILLONG
Recommendations/Observations
8.5 The Committee is happy to note that considerable progress has been made in
operationalization of NEIGRIHMS during 2007-08. The Committee is, however, perturbed to note
that no improvement could be made so far as manpower required for the Institute is concerned.
Like 2006-07, position remained unchanged during 2007-08 also.
Action Taken
Efforts to improve the faculty position during the year 2007-08 continued. The Institute has
conducted interviews since April, 2008 for 42 faculty positions. 40 appeared for interview of which
28 were selected, appointment letters issued to 22 candidates and 9 have joined. Incentives like
Assessment Promotion Scheme, Children’s Education Allowance and reservation of 2 MBBS seats
from the Central Pool for the children of NEIGRIHMS faculty, fully furnished quarters have already
been extended. The total faculty position has also improved. A total of 36 faculty posts have been
filled up. Similarly, 745 supporting staff have also been appointed during the period.
Recommendations/Observations
8.6 The Committee would like to point out that efforts made by the Department for getting the
teaching/specialists faculty posted/deputed from Armed Forces Medical Services have not been
successful due to shortage of senior faculty in the AFMS cadre also. The Committee would again
emphasize that it does not have much faith in the collaboration measures reported to be made with
Institutions like NIMHANS and Dr. Cherian’s Frontier Lifeline. The Committee is not aware how
far the efforts for clinching similar agreements with CMC-Vellore, CMC-Ludhiana, Amrita Institute
of Medical Sciences, Kochi, AIIMS and PGIMER would prove to be successful. The Committee
fails to comprehend how the Departments of Neurology, Neuro-Surgery and Cardiology could be
made operational at the Institute located at Shillong by hiring the services of faculty of NIMHANS

24
located at the other comer of the country. Same would be the position with the proposed tie-up
with other Institutes. The Committee would also like to point out that the Department must be fully
aware about the shortage of faculty at these premier Institutes also. The Committee strongly feels
that such stop-gap arrangements, even if made, cannot make the Institute fully functional. Target
of making 500 bedded hospital and teaching Institute at Shillong is thus likely to remain on paper
only for considerable time. The Committee reiterates that the Department has to make all
conceivable efforts for having permanent faculty at the Institute. Special incentives and extra
facilities have to be worked out for attracting the required manpower to the Institute. Collaboration
with leading Institutes/public-private partnership can be feasible for limited number of posts and
period only. The Committee would also like to be apprised about the present status of phase-I of
filling up of 725 posts and phase-II of filling up of 794 posts.
Action Taken
The Undergraduate Medical College with 50 seats intake has already been started from
18.9.2008 and the full 500-bedded hospital is expected to be made functional by the year end. The
out patient load of the hospital has increased fourfold in the past five months and the bed
occupancy risen to about 75%.
With the inception of the Medical College and implementation of the Sixth Central Pay
Commission recommendations, a better response to the Institute’s efforts at recruitment of
permanent faculty is expected.
The collaborations proposed with various Institutions could not be taken up in view of
difficulties in operationalising the arrangements worked out between NIMHANS and Dr. Cherian’s
Foundation. At the moment, none of the collaborations are being further explored. The Institute is
redoubling its efforts to recruit permanent faculty, especially in view of the factors elaborated
above.

Action Taken
The actual plan expenditure during the financial year 2007-08 was Rs.9.79 crore
(Rs.6.79 crore in Revenue and Rs.3.00 crore under Capital) against the Budget Estimate 2007-08
of Rs.10.00 crore (Rs.7.00 crores in Revenue and Rs.3.00 crore in Capital).
The total expenditure during the Tenth Plan period was Rs.42.17 crore (Rs.31.74 crore in
Revenue and Rs.10.44 in Capital) against the total allocation of Rs. 63.90 crores (Rs.31.42 crores
in Revenue and Rs.32.49 crore in Capital).
The Institute had utilized the allocation made under Plan–Revenue budget. The Capital
Budget which was routed through the Ministry of Urban Development could not be utilized despite
requirement of various capital constructions including renovations of old wards and facilities. In
order to overcome this problem, during 2007-08, the Capital allocation was provided directly to the
Institute and therefore, the fund was utilized fully.
