XII. IMPROVEMENT OF NURSING SERVICES
Recommendations/Observations
12.2 However, the Committee is surprised to observe unsatisfactory level of achievement under
the quantifiable deliverables/physical outputs set for 2007-08. The first target set was training of
8000 nursing personnel through 48 identified Nursing Institutions. By the end of the year, no
progress seems to have been made from the details made available to the Committee. Proposal for
conducting 78 courses for training of 2340 nurses was under process. Training of additional 1600
nurses depended upon proposals to be received from the Institutions. The Committee has been
informed that out of 30 institutions, funds could be released for strengthening of 23 institutions.
Status of these institutions is, however, not-known. The Committee would also like to point out
that projects like upgradation of RAK College of Nursing into Centre of Excellence, upgradation of
School of Nursing into College of Nursing at Dr. R.M.L. Hospital, Safdarjung Hospital and Lady
Harding Medical College and establishing a College of Nursing in JIPMER, Puducherry have been
there for the last few years with no evidence of progress being made. Only exception is College
of Nursing at Lady Harding Medical College reported to have started functioning in 2007. The
Committee hopes that required manpower and infrastructure has been made available there.
Action Taken
In fact the conducting of training courses depends on the number of nurses who are
deputed for undergoing training. In view of the exigency of their work in the hospitals, it becomes
difficult sometimes for the authorities to relieve the required number of nurses for undergoing
training. However, efforts will be made to pursue with the hospital authorities to depute the
maximum number of nurses for joining the training programmes in the identified Nursing
Institutions. As regard strengthening of Institutions, funds have been released to 34 Institutions.
Apart from L.H.M.C. the College of Nursing at JIPMER, Puducherry has been established and
started functioning since 2006. It is proposed to start the College of Nursing at Dr. R.M.L. Hospital
and Safdarjung Hospital for B.Sc. (Nursing) course from the current year.
Recommendations/Observations
12.3 The Committee is not very hopeful as upgradation of School of Nursing at Dr. R.M.L.
Hospital has also been listed as one of the targets to be achieved during 2008-09. The Committee
would like to reiterate that all these projects need to be closely monitored so as to ensure their
completion with in the time schedule without any cost escalation in the original estimate. The
Department should not be a mere spectator, it should devise some mechanism for proper utilization
of the funds and its repeated contention that the utilization/grants of funds depends upon receipt

26
of SOE/UCs is not acceptable to the Committee. The Department has to play a more proactive role
in co-ordination with the hospital/college authorities. The problem areas, wherever noticed, need to
be removed at the earliest.
Action Taken
The Dr. R.M.L. Hospital has already initiated action for obtaining recognition from Indian
Nursing Council and getting affiliation from the University. It is hopeful that the colleges start
functioning from the year 2008.
The Department will make all out efforts to have better coordination with the hospital/
college authorities for maximum utilization of grant.
Recommendations/Observations
12.9 While appreciating the initiatives, the Committee would like to point out that out of total
nursing strength of 3015 in Central Government Hospitals in Delhi, only 795 nursing personnel have
been provided with accommodation. If such unsatisfactory conditions are in Delhi, the Committee
can well imagine the conditions in State Government Hospitals. The Committee strongly feels that
residential facilities for Nurses need to be augmented at the earliest. The Committee recommends
to the Department to formulate a comprehensive policy to stop exodus of nurses as also to offer
more incentives so as to make the profession more attractive for arresting this trend.
Action Taken
In order to augment the residential facilities for Nurses, action is being initiated to obtain
proposals from the Central Government Hospitals including State Government of Delhi for taking
necessary action. In addition it is proposed to take up the matter with Ministry of Urban
Development in this regard.
On the basis of representation of cause of nurses, VI Pay Commission has taken special
care of nurses to provide them incentives. A proposal has also been moved for increase in the age
of retirement from 60 to 65 years in respect of Nursing Teaching Faculty. These steps are in the
direction to make the nursing profession more attractive.
XVI. NA TIONAL AIDS CONTROL PROGRAMME (NACP)
Recommendations/Observations
16.6 The Committee is dismayed to note that progress made under several components of the
programme is abysmal and the trend has been continuing irrespective of the issue being raised again
and again. The Committee has been viewing with serious concern the trend of targets remaining
under-achieved in the past few years. Last year, the Committee was given to understand that non-
procurement of blood bank equipment seriously hampered the modernization of District Level Blood
Banks. Non-availability of STD specialists and specialized STD services were held responsible for
target set for STD clinics. The Committee has been given to understand that instead of the
previous emphasis on opening of STD facilities, now targets are being set for the number of STD
cases treated. The Committee would, however, like to point out that readjustments of targets will
not result in having the required STD specialists and services. The Committee reiterates its earlier
recommendation of formulating a comprehensive plan for the training of doctors in handling AIDS
cases. The Committee recommends to the Department to inform it about the major problem areas
in the implementation of National AIDS Control Programme and the corrective measures taken/
proposed by the Department to overcome the same.

27
Action Taken
(i) It is submitted that the physical targets for 39 district level blood banks and one
plasma fractionation unit are the project targets for NACP — III (2007-12) and not
the physical targets for 2007-08.
Out of 39 blood banks, 5 blood banks were to be taken up in 2007-08. This target
has been achieved during 2007-08. Another 4 blood banks will be taken up in 2008-09. The
remaining blood banks are at various stages of construction which is the responsibility of
the respective State Governments. These blood banks will be taken up once the civil
construction is completed and manpower deployed as these are mandatory requirements for
obtaining a license.
Regarding the establishment of one plasma fractionation centre, it is submitted that one
Plasma Fractionation Centre will be set up during Phase III of NACP (2007-12). This will
be a production unit with capacity to process 1.5 lakh liters of plasma annually to prepare
plasma components like Albumin, Gammaglobulin, Thrombin and Anti Haemophilic Factor
etc. The establishment of such a centre requires in depth work on design, construction,
procurement of equipment manpower deployment etc. The EFC cleared the proposal at its
meeting held in March, 2008 for a total outlay of Rs.250.00 crore. As advised by the
Department of Expenditure, the Cost Benefit Analysis of the proposal is being worked out.
On completion of this analysis, matter will be processed to obtain CCEA approval for the
proposal. The process of civil works etc. will be initiated during 2008-09.
The target of 40 blood component separation units could not be achieved in the
year 2007-08 because of delay in procurement of equipment, this was completed only in
March, 2008. Delivery of Cell Counter has been completed for all 40 centres and the
dispatch of the remaining equipments i.e. Refrigerated Centrifuge, -40
0 and -80 0 C Deep
Freezers Platelet Agitator and Laminar Flow Bench will be completed in the month of
June, 2008. These units will be made functional in July, 2008.
(ii) In NACP-III, special efforts have been made to strengthen STD clinics by improving
the infrastructure as well as by training general duty medical officers in Syndromic
STD case Management. In order to ensure optimal utilization of the STD Clinics,
targets have been set for treating STI episodes not only in the designated STI clinics,
but also at sub district health facilities. As 80% of STD patients go to private doctors,
mapping of private doctors having large client load has been taken up in 100 districts.
These doctors will be trained and monitored.
(iii) Regarding the establishment of sentinel surveillance sites, the target of 196 sites has
been kept for the project period of NACP III. The sentinel sites are selected based on
the epidemiological situation. Recently in April, 2008, an international consultation was
organized by WHO to review the existing HIV surveillance systems in the country.
Further expansion will be taken up based on their recommendations.
(iv) The District units have been established in the 31 A&B category districts of Andhra
Pradesh, Karnataka, Maharashtra and Tamil Nadu by nominating a senior medical
officer to coordinate programme activities and monitor implementation. The district
units will be expanded to other States. Operational Guidelines have been prepared to
ensure that the district units are integrated with the district health society.
Medical officers and other health personnel are trained in designated centres before
starting an integrated counseling and testing centre (ICTC) and an anti-retroviral therapy
(ART) centre.

28
Recommendations/Observations
16.7 The Committee would recommend to the Department to continue its consultations with the
states that have either suspended or banned the programme and achieve consensus in implementing
the scheme.
Action Taken
The AEP is a key intervention to build life skills and help adolescents cope with negative
peer pressure, develop positive behavior, improve sexual health and to reduce vulnerability or risk
of HIV infections. Ministry of Human Resource Development scaled up the Adolescence Education
Programme (AEP) in collaboration with NACO as a classroom based co-curricular activity and also
allocated 16 hours for the same. As on 31st March, 2007, 1,14,345 schools were covered out of
1,44,409 Secondary schools in the country and around 2.88 Lakh teachers trained under this
programme.
Under the programme, nodal teachers are trained for AEP instruction for 16 hours per
academic year for students in class IX and XI. These sessions include growing up, adolescence,
STIs/RTIs and HIV/AIDS, life skills for HIV prevention. Question boxes and activity sessions are
also carried out to clarify doubts and dispel myths and misconceptions. Recent activities
undertaken-In response to some concerns expressed on the AEP instruction materials, in
consultation with MHRD, a National Level Toolkit Review Committee comprising of academicians,
educationists, child psychologists, mental health experts, communication experts, representatives
from non-Governmental organizations, National Council of Education Research and Training
(NCERT) and Central Board of secondary Education was constituted to review the education
material. The revised material is under review and will be made available by end June. It is
proposed to send this prototype toolkit to the State Education Department, to hold consultations
with key stakeholders such as parents, teachers, principals, NGOs, faith based organizations,
educationists and child psychologists at regional and State levels to finalize the modules. The
material will also be placed on NACO website and comments invited. It is expected that this
process will be completed by November 2008 and the AEP programme taken up from December
onwards. The states where the programme has been suspended have already been requested to
achieve a consensus by constituting such committees.
Recommendations/Observations
16.8 The Committee appreciates these priorities of the Department and recommends to the
Department to inform it about the specific steps taken by it for achievement of the same.
Action Taken
Under the Blood Safety component of the National AIDS Control Programme, four new
initiatives have been undertaken viz. (a) Establishment of four Metro Blood Banks as Centres of
Excellence in Transfusion Medicine; (b) Establishment of one Plasma Fractionation Centre;
(c) Establishment of National Blood Transfusion Authority and (d) Formulation of a Law to regulate
blood collection and distribution. All these activities require highly skilled technical expertise and
centre specific civil construction. International assistance has been sought from GTZ (Government
of Germany) and WHO. While the activities are being started this year, it will take 3 years or more
to operationalise these initiatives.
The status of implementation of these new initiatives is as follows:
(a) Establishment of Metro Blood Banks –  The Expenditure Finance Committee (EFC)
in its meeting dated 4th March, 2008 approved the proposal for establishment of four Metro

29
Blood Banks. A Note for the Cabinet Committee of Economic Affairs (CCEA) has been
prepared. The CCEA approval is expected to be received by early July, 2008. The process
of identifying a consultant to develop architectural drawings and bid document for selection
of agencies to take up construction is being initiated.
(b) Establishment of one Plasma Fractionation Centre – The Expenditure Finance
Committee (EFC) in its meeting dated 26th March 2008 approved the proposal for
establishment of one Plasma Fractionation Centre in the country subject to developing a cost
benefit analysis. This is under preparation by a lead consultant. The CCEA Note is expected
to be submitted for approval by mid July.
(c) and (d) Establishment of National Blood Transfusion Authority –  To streamline
Blood Transfusion Services in the country, a National Blood Transfusion Authority is
proposed to be established as an independent body under the Ministry of Health and Family
Welfare. Accordingly, a draft Blood Transfusion Act has been prepared by the National
AIDS Control Organisation. The final draft is ready for submitting to the Ministry of Law,
Department of Legal Affairs for examination and approval.
Under the Blood Safety component of the National AIDS Control Programme, four new
initiatives have been undertaken viz. (a) Establishment of four Metro Blood Banks as Centres of
Excellence in Transfusion Medicine; (b) Establishment of one Plasma Fractionation Centre;
(c) Establishment of National Blood Transfusion Authority and (d) Formulation of a Law to regulate
blood collection and distribution. All these activities require highly skilled technical expertise and
centre specific civil construction. International assistance has been sought from GTZ (Government
of Germany) and WHO. While the activities are being started this year, it will take 3 years or more
to operationalise these initiatives.
Recommendations/Observations
16.10 The Committee is happy to note that though delayed, the Department has taken the desired
step towards starting the second line of treatment to lessen the sufferings of drug resistant patients.
The Committee would like to recommend to the Department to put in place an effective mechanism
so that no patient in need of second line ART is deprived of it.
Action Taken
Second line Drugs have been rolled out at two centres on a pilot basis. Scale up of the
second line drugs will be based on the experience gained at these centres. The second-line drugs
are proposed to be initiated only at the 10 Regional Centres of Excellence being developed by
NACO. This is being done to have a regulatory mechanism to monitor the patients on second-line
for 100% adherence as there is no third-line ARV . The steps involved in the roll out includes
training of health-care providers, making available facilities for viral load testing and a monitoring
mechanism as explained above.
Recommendations/Observations
16.12 The Committee is not fully inclined to agree with the contention of the Ministry. Some of
the corrective measures in centralized procurement as mentioned below clearly indicate that besides
deficiencies at the state level, implementation at the Central level was also not upto the mark:—
➢ Procurement responsibility shifted from Procurement Support Agencies to the United
Nations Office for Project Services for Bank supported operations.
➢ Creation of Financial Management Group at the Health Ministry.

30
➢ Development of detailed guidelines and operational manuals for procurement of
NGOs and Public-Private Partnerships.
➢ Introduction of evaluation of NGO performance by Committee having one external
evaluator.
➢ Termination of contracts of about 25% NGOs.
➢ Pre-award validation/certification of WHO GMP made mandatory in all cases.
Action Taken
Following is our submission regarding the observations made above:
➢ The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare has established an Empowered Procurement
Wing (EPW) to professionalize the procurement of health sector goods and services
under guidance of an internationally selected consultancy firm (Crown Agents). Till
such time the procurement and supply management capacities of the EPW, are
developed, all International Competitive Bidding/Limited international Bidding and
National Competitive Bids, estimated to cost more than US$l00,000 for goods and
works under National AIDS Control Organization (NACO) and State Implementation
Societies (SIS) are to be carried out by a qualified procurement agency selected on
basis of Quality and Cost Based Selection (QCBS) or through a UN agency. In view
of the foregoing, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare have entered into an agreement
with UNOPS to act as procurement agency for central health sector projects including
NACP III.
➢ The Financial Management Group was formed at the Ministry of Health and Family
Welfare for better monitoring and control of the available resources and ensure that
funds released to the implementing agencies are optimally utilized.
➢ With the aim of improving, strengthening and standardizing the quality of interventions,
NACO developed detailed technical guidelines on procurement of NGOs/CBOs and
Public Private Partnerships. The guidelines outline key procedures in standards to be
maintained, financial procedures, procurement stock and inventory, budgeting,
reporting, legal and statutory matters. In addition, guidelines on programme
management have also developed and SACS and NGOs trained/oriented on how to
implement them.
The operational guidelines provide for stringent procedures of selection consisting of
Calling for expression of interest are given out on national/regional newspapers,
screening of applications through desk reviews, conducting field appraisals by the JAT
(independent committee consisting of experts from technical and financial background
and members from Government Organisations) and short listing by TAC (Technical
advisory committee) for final selections.
➢ Regarding discontinuation of 25% of NGO’s, the facts of the case are that at the start
of NACP Phase-III, all 952 NGO’s implementing Targeted Interventions were
evaluated. The evaluation done by a team consisting of an external expert
recommended the termination of 163 NGO’s amounting to 17% of the total on various
grounds ranging from coverage of non focus groups; financial and programme
mismanagement inadequacies.
➢ Pre-award validation/certification of WHO GMP made mandatory in all cases Schedule
M (Revised) of Drugs and Cosmetics Act and Rules has been made mandatory for all
pharmaceutical manufacturers in India and is therefore applicable for goods procured

31
through National Competitive Bidding (NCB). Since the Schedule M (Revised) of Drugs
and Cosmetics Act and Rules has not been accepted by the development partners as
equivalent to WHO GMP, a Certificate of Pharmaceutical Products (COPP) i.e., Good
Manufacturing Practices (GMP) as per WHO scheme has been stipulated as an
essential requirement in all procurement of pharmaceuticals products being made by
UNOPS under International Competitive Bidding (ICB).
XVII. NATIONAL CANCER CONTROL PROGRAMME (NCCP)
Recommendations/Observations
17.3 Against a Tenth Plan allocation of Rs.333.00 crores, enhanced provision of Rs.2871.92 crores
including a provision of Rs.471.92 crores for Tobacco Control has been made for the Eleventh
Plan. While welcoming this initiative of the Government, the Committee is constrained to point out
that level of utilization has to keep pace with the enhanced provision. The Committee has been
persistently highlighting the need for better management and co-ordination of all concerned.
However, the position remains unchanged. Against the Tenth Plan allocation of Rs.333.00 crores,
utilization reported at the end of the year was only Rs.252.95 crores. The Committee finds that
year 2007-08, which is the first year of the Eleventh Plan failed to bring any improvement. Plan
allocation of Rs.140.00 crores for 2007-08 for the NCCP was reduced to Rs.70.00 crores at the
RE stage. The expenditure reported upto 29.02.2008 is only Rs.40.31 crores. Similarly, Plan
provision of Rs.40.00 crores for Tobacco Control had to be brought down to Rs.29.00 crores with
utilization of only Rs.7.63 crores by 29.02.2008.
Action Taken
As per the existing guidelines, only one time assistance can be provided to the Institute
irrespective of the cost of the equipment. As per the present condition, Rs.3.00 crore is the
maximum amount that can be provided to the institute for Development of Oncology Wing. Since
the inception of the scheme, most of the institutions had claimed the grant-in-aid, the scheme had
come to a saturated point and this Ministry is receiving very less number of proposals seeking
grant-in-aid under the scheme.
Finalization of the EFC for the Eleventh Five Year Plan is under process and is likely to
be completed shortly.
Recommendations/Observations
17.5 The Committee takes a serious view of total lack of initiative on the part of the Department
so far as implementation of National Cancer Control Programme is concerned. The Committee
strongly feels that its following observations made in the 25th Report have failed to make any
impact on the Department:—
“The Committee is not satisfied with the citing of a number of procedural formalities
as the major factor for undue delay in the release of funds by the Department. It is for
the Department to take a pro-active role by pursuing with State Governments for sending
viable proposals. As admitted by the Department, the proposal for upgradation of nine
existing RCCs into Centre of Excellence was considered only during the last quarter of the
year and five institutions were approved for funding. However, due to non-availability of
required funds, only four institutions could be funded. What is more disturbing is that the
entire allocation of Rs.8.70 crore earmarked for N-E Region remained unutilised for want
of complete proposals. Keeping in view the experience of 2006-07, the Committee
recommends to the Department to put in place an effective monitoring mechanism for
implementation of Cancer Control Programme.”

32
It is a sorry state of affairs that at the end of the year, a large number of proposals
received for different components were at a ripe stage but due to paucity of time and some
technical deficiency found in the proposals, the same could not be materialized. It is high time that
an effective monitoring mechanism in co-ordination with all the implementing agencies is put in
place without any further delay.
Action Taken
Ministry of Finance provided an amount of Rs.25.00 crores to be equally distributed among
the 5 selected RCCs for “upgradation of the existing RCCs into centre of excellence” during the
year 2006-07. However, due to non-availability of required funds, only four institutions (three in
full and one partially) could be funded. During the year 2007-08 an amount of Rs.4.00 crore was
provided to RCC Trivendrum being the balance amount as Rs.l.00 crore was released during
2006-07 and Rs.5.00 crore was released to Dr. B.B. Cancer Institute, Guwahati as the Fifth
Institution.
Status of NE State:
➢ An amount of Rs.5.00 crore was released to Dr. B.B. Cancer Institute, Guwahati for
making the Institute as Centre of Excellence during the year 2007-08.
➢ Rs.22.00 lakhs under District Cancer Control Programme to RCC Guwahati covering
district of Barpeta, Nalbari and Darang during the year 2007-08.
➢ Cancer Hospital, Tripura has been recognized as 27th RCC in the State of Tripura and
an amount of Rs.5.00 crore is likely to be released to the institute.
For settlement of the pending utilization and also timely utilization of grant-in-aid, this
Ministry interacts with the representatives of the RCC, involved Regional Director (Health and FW)
in inspection and monitoring the Institute and also called meeting with the representative of the
State Governments including In-charge of concerned hospitals.
Recommendations/Observations
17.7 The Committee recommends to the Department to take up its case more vigorously and
with sound reasoning with the State Government of West Bengal for early handing over of the
piece of land for early construction of the campus of CNCI, Kolkata.
Action Taken
The matter has already been taken by Hon’ble HFM with the Hon’ble Chief Minister of West
Bengal for early handing over of the land to enable this Ministry to start the survey /construction
activities for the development of second campus of CNCI.
Recently, CNCI, Kolkata had a meeting with HIDCO (land providing agency of West
Bengal). HIDCO has agreed to hand over the possession of 05 acres of land for taking permissive
possession for carrying out pre-construction activities like soil testing, pilling work, construction
of boundary wall etc.
XIX. NA TIONAL MENTAL HEALTH PROGRAMME (NMHP)
Recommendations/Observations
19.4 The Committee is constrained to note that in spite of approved outlay being drastically
reduced to nearly half for the National Mental Health Programme, the expenditure reported upto

33
29th February, 2008 is a meagre 14.13 crores. A proposal for releasing funds to the extent of
Rs.22.165 crores (including Rs.4.71 crores for NE) was stated to be under consideration. The
Committee takes a serious view of the continuing trend of poor utilization of funds earmarked for
the programme.
Action Taken
According to the Tenth Plan Scheme of the National Mental Health Programme, all the
proposals for extending District Mental Health Programme, Upgradation of Psychiatric Wings of
Government Medical Colleges/General Hospitals and Modernisation of Government Mental Hospitals
are to be submitted by the State Government concerned to the Central Government for
consideration. These proposals are first examined by a Working Group and the recommendations
of the Working Group are placed before the Steering Committee under the chairmanship of
Secretary (Health and FW) for approval. Besides, approval of IFD is also obtained in the matter.
The proposals not recommended by the Working Group are got corrected by the State
Governments/Institutions. Non-receipt of adequate number of complete proposals in time from the
State Governments leads to lesser release of grant-in-aid and consequently under utilization of
Budget allocation of the National Mental Health Programme.
For effective and efficient implementation of the programme, periodical interactions with the
State Governments have been held. A review meeting on NMHP with the State Health Secretaries/
Directors of Health Services/Nodal Officers was held in February, 2007. A meeting was also held
with the Health Secretaries of States in July, 2007 in which implementation of NMHP was, inter
alia, discussed. Regional Meetings were held during August-September, 2007 with the State Nodal
Officers for NMHP to review progress of implementation of the Programme. Review meetings on
the implementation of NMHP were also held on 3rd March and 25th March, 2008. All the
representatives of State Governments were requested to submit proposals for assistance under
various components of the Programme and to submit financial year-wise Utilisation Certificates
along with Audited Statements
In order to overcome these problems, it has been envisaged in the EFC Proposal on NMHP
for implementation during the Eleventh  Five Year Plan, to decentralize the programme and also to
integrate it with National Rural Health Mission. Grants under different schemes to a particular State
would be released through the State Health Society along with the schemes and guidelines for
NMHP. S tate Nodal Of ficer representing the programme in the State Health Society would get the
grants released for various districts and institutions. At the district level, the Programme Officer
would represent the programme in the District Health Society and facilitate operationalisation of
DMHP.
A proposal for releasing funds to the extent of Rs.22.165 crore through the State Health
Societies was under consideration but this could not materialize, as the I.F.D./competent authority
did not approve the proposal, pending the approval of the EFC Proposal on NMHP for
implementation during the Eleventh  Five Year Plan.
Recommendations/Observations
19.6 Committee is dismayed at the fact that the expansion of the National Mental Health
programme envisaged for the Eleventh Plan is yet to be approved. The Committee recommends to
the Department to present its case strongly and effectively so as to get necessary approval without
further delay. Further, the Department can not simply shy away from its responsibility by stating
that progress of the events in these scheme depends on the active cooperation of the State
Governments and other stake holders. The Committee, accordingly, recommends the Department to
approach and have regular interactions with the State Governments concerned and identify the

34
problem areas which need to be addressed. The Department should be proactive so as to motivate
the State Governments to participate in the programme actively and furnish proposals with all
requisite information to enable release and utilization of the funds optimally. The Committee would
like to be informed of the remedial measures taken by the Department in this regard.
Action Taken
The EFC Proposal on NMHP for implementation during the Eleventh Five Year Plan has
been circulated among the appraisal agencies i.e.  Department of Expenditure and Planning
Commission. The proposal will be taken up for approval of the Expenditure finance Committee
soon.
For effective and efficient implementation of the programme, periodical interactions with the
State Governments have been held. A review meeting on NMHP with the State Health Secretaries/
Directors of Health Services/Nodal Officers was held in February, 2007. A meeting was also held
with the Health Secretaries of States in July, 2007 in which implementation of NMHP was, inter
alia, discussed. Regional Meetings were held during August-September, 2007 with the State Nodal
Officers for NMHP to review progress of implementation of the Programme. Review meetings on
the implementation of NMHP were also held on 3rd March and 25th March, 2008. All the
representatives of State Governments were requested to submit proposals for assistance under
various components of the Programme and to submit financial year-wise Utilisation Certificates
along with Audited Statements.
It has been envisaged in the EFC Proposal on NMHP for implementation during the Eleventh
Five Year Plan, to decentralize the programme and also to integrate it with National Rural Health
Mission. Grants under different schemes to a particular State would be released through the State
Health Society along with the schemes and guidelines for NMHP for effective implementation of
the programme. State Nodal Officer representing the programme in the State Health Society would
get the grants released for various districts and institutions. At the district level, the Programme
Officer would represent the programme in the District Health Society and facilitate operationalisation
of DMHP.
Recommendations/Observations
19.8 The Committee would like to point out that majority of districts in the country continue to
remain outside the purview of District Mental Health Programme. Only 100 districts have been
covered so far, with another 125 new districts proposed to be brought under the programme during
2008-09.
The Committee would like to emphasize that all the districts need to be covered at the
earliest. All efforts need to be made in this direction.
Action Taken
It has been envisaged in the EFC Proposal on NMHP for implementation during the Eleventh
Five Year Plan, to extend the District Mental Health Programme to all the Districts of the Country
in a phased manner.

Recommendations/Observations
22.3 Now that the Sixth Pay Commission has submitted its recommendations, the Committee
would recommend to the Department to expedite the implementation of the Javed Chaudhary

35
Committee recommendations. The Committee would like to be informed of the progress made in
this regard.
Action Taken
The present status of the implementation of the recommendations of Javed Chaudhary
Committee is at Annexure-XIII.
XXIII. NEW SCHEMES
Recommendations/Observations
23.2 The Committee is dismayed to note that every year the Department announces new
schemes/programmes, many of which are without finalizing the modalities and proper ground work,
thereby resulting in under or no utilization of funds. The Committee recommends to the Department
to enhance its efforts to complete all necessary formalities and launch the programmes at the
earliest so that funds do not get blocked in such schemes.
Action Taken
Most of the Schemes were introduced during 2007-08 It being the first year for
implementation, there was slow progress in appraisal/approval of the schemes which slowed down
the pace of expenditure. The implementation process has gradually gained momentum and the
progress in this regard for certain schemes is given as follows:—
• Pilot project on Prevention and Control of Human Rabies.
• Leptospirosis Control Programme.
The two projects, initially envisaged for five years have been revised in consultation with
Integrated Finance Division and approval of the competent authority has now been obtained to
carry out the projects on pilot basis for two years only.
Project on Prevention and Control of Human Rabies is being carried out on pilot basis in
four cities, namely Delhi, Ahmedabad, Bangalore and Pune, whereas Leptospirosis Control
Programme is to be carried out on pilot basis in three States.
The strategy for implementation of the respective pilot projects has been firmed up and the
action plans have also been drawn for the current year. Nodal Officers in pilot cities/States have
been identified and Core Groups have also been constituted for implementation of the projects.
Draft MoU are under progress. Terms of Reference for appointment of Consultants and Data Entry
Operators under the projects have been formulated. Proforma for collection of base line data and
also for surveillance of respective diseases have been finalized and circulated to the concerned
cities/States. Steps are being taken to strengthen the laboratories and for preparation of IEC
material.
• National Organ Transplant Programme – The National Organ Transplant Programme is
at an advanced stage of formulation. The programme is to be proceeded by
amendments in the THOA Acts and Rules. The amended rules have been notified. The
Cabinet note for amending the THOA is under circulation to concerned Ministries.
• Oral Health – While the Ministry has been in the process of preparing an EFC proposal
for the programme, the Ministry of Finance issued instructions in June, 2008 to the
effect that no new scheme/programme should be launched in the current financial year
even if Budget provision has been made for the same unless it has been mentioned in

36
the Finance Minister’s Budget Speech for 2008-09. As this programme was not
mentioned in the Finance Minister’s Budget Speech, further action in the matter was
held in abeyance.
Nevertheless, in the meeting of Senior Officers under the chairmanship of Secretary
(H&FW) held on 19th August, 2008, it was decided to merge this programme with
National Rural Health Mission. Ministry is in the process of examining the proposal
from this angle, to finalise those parts of the Scheme that can be merged with NRHM.
• National Programme for Health Care for Elderly – The EFC for National Programme
for Health Care for Elderly has been submitted to IFD.
• National Centre for Disease Control – Ministry of Health and Family Welfare, in view
of emerging and re-emerging of infectious diseases and rapid response thereto,
proposed to transform National Institute of Communicable Diseases into National
Centre for Disease Control (NCDC). During discussions with Planning Commission on
the EFC note for the proposal, it was suggested that an Independent appraisal of the
Institution should be undertaken and DPR should be prepared. Accordingly, appraisal
of the institute has been conducted by IIT, Delhi. Further, Consultant for Bid Process
Management has been selected for preparing the concept paper for NCDC. The
process for selection of agency to prepare DPR is likely to be initiated shortly. Once
the DPR is prepared, revised EFC memo with the anticipated expenditure will be
submitted.
• National Programme on Sports Injury – The EFC for the Sports Injury Centre at
Safdarjung Hospital has been approved. Work is held up on account of litigation
involving the State and Centre.
• National Advisory Board of Standards – National Advisory Board for Standards has
already started functioning.
XXIV. MISCELLANEOUS
Recommendations/Observations
24.2 The Committee recommends the Department to work out modalities for establishing
specialized kidney centers immediately and in the mean time approach the Planning Commission for
funds with a proposal to improve dialysis facility in the country. In view of increasing gap in
demand and supply, at least the well known Institutes across India should be provided with the
dialysis machines during the current financial year. The Committee would like to be informed about
the steps taken by the Department in this regard.
Action Taken
There is shortage of Dialysis facilities in the country. Health is state subject and it is for
state Government concerned to provide the health care facilities to the general public. There is
shortage of qualified doctors to run the dialysis machines. There are very few centres which are
providing D.M. (Nephrology) super specialty courses and there are about 500 such specialities in
the country. Curriculum for training of physicians for dialysis is being finalised in collaboration with
IGNOU with a view to start short term training programmes to bridge the gap. Efforts are being
made to initiate the standalone dialysis in the CGHS Delhi. In this regard, some proposals are under
consideration and expert opinion has been taken. Till such time, CGHS patients are reimbursed the
money spent by beneficiaries on Dialysis in the private sector. Similarly other organised sector and

37
Government employees who are covered under similar insurance schemes namely CS (MA) and
ESI are provided the reimbursement.
Central Government institutions e.g. AIIMS, PGI, JIPMER, RML Hospital and Safdarjung
Hospital are already having Dialysis facilities and further upgradation is done from time to time.
There is decentralised decision making in the Health Care Delivery System under National Rural
Health System and State Governments can require equipments themselves.
National Organ Transplant Programme is being finalised and likely to be initiated soon.

38
CHAPTER-III

II. BUDGETARY ALLOCATION
Recommendations/Observations
2.3 The Committee observes that Oversight Committee has been granted only Rs.100.00 crores
against last year’s approved outlay of Rs.600 crores, which, however, could not be utilized. In the
light of the recent Supreme Court’s judgment and the Government’s propensity towards
implementing it from this year itself, the Committee opines that the funds would not be sufficient.
Similarly against proposal of Rs.453.00 crores, only Rs.329.00 crores have been provided
to CGHS; against projected requirement of Rs.87.88 crores, Safdarjung Hospital has received
Rs.50.00 crores; Dr. RML Hospital has been allocated Rs.59.20 crores against projected
Plan requirement of Rs.72.11 crores and Rs.68.95 crores against Non-Plan requirement of
Rs.71.88 crores; AIIMS has been granted Rs.452.00 crores in Grant-in-Aids against projected
requirement of Rs.753.00 crores and against the projected total requirement of JIPMER for
Rs.150.80 crores, it has received only Rs. 98.00 crores. The Committee observes that under
provisioning in many of the Institutes is likely to adversely affect the completion of construction
and developmental activities undertaken by them and in the process the Institutions.
Action Taken
An allocation of Rs.150 crores as proposed by the Ministry had been approved by the
Planning Commission for Oversight Committee for 2008-09. However, at the time of finalizing the
scheme-wise outlays the matter was still sub-judice , as such this allocation was reduced to Rs.100
crores and the balance of Rs.50 crores shown against MIS scheme under NRHM where there was
inadequate resource allocation. This reduction was made in consultation with Planning Commission
with the understanding that in the event of the 27% reservation being operationalized, the amount
will be re-appropriated for this purpose trom out of the budget available. If necessary, additional
allocations will also be sought for the purpose as this activity was not envisaged at the time of
formulation of the Five Year Plan.
In respect of hospitals like Dr. RML, Safdarjung and other medical institutions, Planning
Commission had classified the schemes under broad heads and indicated the overall allocation
against these heads. Thereafter, the Ministry taking into account the trend in expenditure and the
utilization levels as on 31st January, 2008 finalized the scheme-wise allocations. The broad heads
of classification amongst others include strengthening of hospitals and dispensaries and
redevelopment of hospitals/institutions. The details of the proposed outlays and that finally allocated
scheme-wise under these broad heads are given in the tables below:

COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee was given to understand that the overall approved outlays under two
heads- strengthening of hospitals and dispensaries and redevelopment of hospitals/
institutions have been drastically reduced vis-a-vis the proposed outlay. Hospitals like RML
and Safdarjung and other institutions like AIIMS, JIPMER etc. have been allocated lesser
amounts than what had been proposed. The Committee is constrained to observe that time
and again it has been pointing out to the Department to place its case before the Planning
Commission for funds allocation in an effective way, yet it appears that once again the

40
Department has not been able to present its case convincingly. The Committee is of the
view that lesser approved outlay often stems from past trends of sub-optimum utilization of
funds and the issues incidental thereto. The Committee would, therefore, recommend to the
Department to have a right approach in this regard by making a correct assessment of
requirements of funds for the different activities and place the same effectively before the
Planning Commission. The Committee would also like to impress upon the Department to
take effective measures so as to ensure optimum and judicious utilization of allocated
funds.
Recommendations/Observations
2.11 The Committee welcomes the proposal of the Department to introduce a provision in the
Sanction itself regarding audit of all centrally sponsored programmes, PSUs under the Department
and autonomous bodies which are receiving Grant-in-Aid. The Committee recommends to the
Department to expedite the same and intimate the progress made in this regard.
Action Taken
A D.O. letter dated 19th March, 2008 from Chief Controller of Accounts level sent to Joint
Secretaries of Department with request to incorporate the condition in all Grants in aid sanctions/
releases that “The accounts of the grantee institution/organisation shall be open to inspection by the
sanctioning authority and audit both by the Comptroller and Auditor General of India under the
provisions of CAG (DPC) Act, 1971 and Internal Audit by Principal Accounts Office of the
Ministry, whenever the institution or organisation is called upon to do so”. The progress made in
this regard will be intimated on receipt of reply from the Department.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee observes that action regarding audit of all centrally sponsored
programmes, PSUs under the Department and autonomous bodies receiving Grants-in-Aid
has been initiated by the Department. The Committee would like to be apprised about the
latest position in this regard.
Recommendations/Observations
2.12 Besides the names of Bodies/Programmes which would be covered during 2008-09, the
Committee would like to be informed about the centrally sponsored health programmes, PSUs and
autonomous bodies which were audited during 2007-08 along with the adverse audit findings and
the action taken by the Department thereon.
Action Taken
The names of bodies/Institutions/Societies/Programmes/Grantee Institutions which were
covered during 2008-09 is at Annexure-I.
As far as audit during 2007-08 of the centrally sponsored health programmes and
autonomous body is concerned, the list of units alongwith the copy of adverse audit findings
(paras) is at Annexure-II.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee notes that out of the five units where adverse audit findings were
made during 2007-08, compliance in case of only one has been received. The remaining four

41
units have some serious audit paras against them. The Committee would appreciate if the
matter is taken up with these units urgently so that the irregularities reported are
reconciled and a sense of financial accountability prevails. Status Note in this regard may
be furnished to the Committee.
III. CENTRAL GOVERNMENT HEALTH SCHEME (CGHS)
Recommendations/Observations
3.5 The Committee would like to point out that situation is some what different. Problems are
being faced both by the CGHS functionaries and beneficiaries. Technicians, Junior staff manning
counters in dispensaries and attending doctors need to be fully trained. Practical problems like
internet and electricity failure have to be tackled by having an alternative arrangement. These initial
teething problems should not be allowed to be prolonged. At present, beneficiaries which include
senior citizens also are being made to wait for hours at the dispensaries. Their ordeal starts right
from the registration counter and continues upto delivery of medicines. The Committee would
appreciate if all the measures including training, smooth running of computers with full
connectivity, backup for power failure are taken at the earliest and also closely monitored.
Action Taken
The Speed of Internet has improved w.e.f. 1st April 2008. After discussing with MTNL
Internet speed has been enhanced to 2mbps at dispensaries with effect from 1.4.2008.
NIC has also strengthened the Computer servers to improve better connectivity. The
support of Data Entry Operators at Dispensaries is continuing. MTNL has identified Nodal officers
specifically to address the internet related complaints of CGHS dispensaries. NIC has also
earmarked eight Supervisors to take care of software related problems at dispensaries. The project
is closely monitored by Additional Directors, CGHS, Director, CGHS, Sr. Technical Director, NIC
and Senior Officers of Ministry of Health and Family Welfare. In addition regular meetings are held
with officials of MTNL.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee had called for taking measures which includes training of the staff,
connectivity of the computer systems and their backup for power failure. However, the
Department has concentrated on the aspect of internet related issues, viz., enhancing its
speed and software related problems. The issue of training of staff and the power backup
seems to have been ignored at this juncture. The Committee would like to emphasize that
though internet efficiency is a vital aspect of the computerization programme, yet it has to
be seen in totality. The issue of proper training of the staff and the power backup are also
crucial for the success of the computerization exercise. The Committee, therefore, expects
the Department to chalk out a proper strategy in this regard.
Recommendations/Observations
3.6 The Committee has been emphasizing for long for introduction of two shift system in
CGHS. The Committee does not appreciate the contention of the Ministry that a large number of
private hospitals and diagnostic centres have been empanelled under the CGHS and in case of
emergency, beneficiaries can go there for treatment and claim re-imbursement later. The Committee
would like to draw the attention of the Ministry once again to the fact that idea behind having two
shifts in CGGS dispensaries is the inconvenience being faced by the CGHS beneficiaries, majority
of them office goers in going to the dispensaries in the morning hours. The Committee is disturbed

42
by the element of helplessness shown by the Ministry in their candid admission of staff side
vehemently opposing the move. In this era where the service providers like banks etc. are opting
for flexible timings to give their customers maximum possible services, at least the Ministry could
have come forward with a positive and proactive approach. The Committee would reiterate its
recommendation made last year for starting two shifts in a few dispensaries as a test case. One
must not forget that CGHS is a service oriented scheme, supposedly providing health care mainly
to our serving employees and pensioners. It is the responsibility of the Ministry to make the CGHS
employees understand and not to be pressurized by them.
Action Taken
The Ministry of Health and Family Welfare intends to take the matter before the Committee
of Secretaries.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee observes that its recommendation regarding starting of two shifts
system in CGHS dispensaries has ultimately resulted in a positive response from the
Department. The Committee would appreciate if the matter is placed before the Committee
of Secretaries at the earliest.
Recommendations/Observations
3.7 The Committee recommends that construction of dispensary in Dwarka where land is
already available should start at the earliest. All procedural formalities should be expedited and
completion of project strictly monitored.
Action Taken
In view of the increase in the number of CGHS beneficiaries (serving/retired) and their
dependants in the sub city of Dwarka, the competent authority accorded approval for relocation
of CGHS dispensary in Dwarka in lieu  of closure of Moti Nagar dispensary, where the daily
average attendance is low. The beneficiaries now attached to Moti Nagar dispensary will be
adjusted in nearby dispensaries.
DDA has been requested for handing over the possession of three allotted plots of land in
Dwarka at Sectors 6, 8 and 12, which has been followed by reminders. Reply from DDA is still
awaited.
Presently no suitable accommodation is available for housing CGHS Dispensary in Dwarka.
However, efforts are continuing for locating suitable accommodation in Dwarka for the opening of
a dispensary at Dwarka.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee was given to understand that land for CGHS dispensary was available
in Dwarka. The Department has in its recent update had admitted that it had been allotted
three plots of land in Dwarka at Sectors 6, 8 and 12. The problem cited here is that the
construction work of a dispensary could not be started for want of a formal possession of
the plots being given by the DDA to the Department. The Committee has been apprised that
reminders have been sent to the DDA and their reply is still awaited. The Committee is
unhappy to note that in the name of expediting formalities only reminders have been sent.
The Committee fails to understand the reasons for undue delay on the part of DDA in

43
providing possession of the plots in Dwarka when the same had already been allotted. It is
high time that  the Department takes up the matter at the highest level in DDA in the
interest of a large number of CGHS beneficiaries residing in Dwarka. The Committee would
also like to point out that even after possession of land, it will take considerable time in
the opening of CGHS dispensaries in Dwarka. Concerted efforts are, therefore, required to
be made for getting suitable accommodation till then so as to get at least one Dispensary
operational there.
Recommendations/Observations
3.8 The Committee is surprised to note that even after two years, suitable accommodation for
housing the dispensary in Sector 82, Noida and Gurgaon could not be managed. Secretary (Health)
Government of Haryana is now being requested to allot a building for CGHS Dispensary in
Gurgaon. Proposal for housing the CGHS Dispensary in Community Centre, Sector 82, Noida on
rent-basis is under consideration. The Committee strongly feels that the Ministry should pursue the
two proposals in hand so that large number of CGHS beneficiaries residing in these two areas do
not continue to remain deprived of health care any more.
Action Taken
Status of CGHS dispensary at NOIDA
Efforts have been made for housing the dispensary after “In Principle Approval” obtained
for opening extension counter of NOIDA Sector 37 at Greater NOIDA. Efforts have been made
by requesting Chairman (CEO) Greater NOIDA for allotment of suitable accommodation. Recently
Chairman, Greater NOIDA Industrial Development Authority, District Gautam Budh Nagar (U.P.), in
his communication has expressed willingness to provide accommodation at a monthly rental of
Rs.91,473/-. As the rent demanded is considered to be too high for the extension counter, the
Ministry is exploring the possibility of acquiring land for the dispensary.
Status of CGHS dispensary at Gurgaon
Efforts have been made for housing the dispensary after “In Principle Approval” obtained
for opening of a CGHS dispensary at Sector 56 Gurgaon in March 2006. In March, 2007 an
advertisement was released in leading News Paper for hiring accommodation for the dispensary
to which there is no response. Letter was written to the Secretary, Government of Haryana on
1.4.2008 for allotment of a building for CGHS Dispensary. However till date no allotment has been
made.
As soon as accommodation for opening of a dispensary is made available in Sector 82,
NOIDA and Sector 56, Gurgaon, necessary steps will be taken to make them functional.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee is constrained to note that the Department has adopted a lacklustre
approach in the matter. It has been close to three years since the in-principle approval for
opening the new CGHS dispensaries in Sector 82 Noida and Gurgaon was given by the
Planning Commission and in the name of action, only a request to Chairman (CEO) Greater
NOIDA for allotment of suitable accommodation has been made, which however has not
materialized due to high rent cost. Similarly, in case of Gurgaon Dispensary, after a gap of
one year since in-principle approval, an advertisement was issued in March, 2007, which
however didn’t attract any response. Further, it took one more year to write to the
Secretary, Government of Haryana for allotment of CGHS Dispensary. No efforts seem to

44
have been taken since then. The Committee is unhappy with this kind of lackadaisical
approach of the Department. If this is the state of affairs at present, the Committee can
well imagine that it may take even a decade to start the two dispensaries. The Committee
would, therefore, recommend to the Department to make every conceivable effort so that
the two dispensaries are made functional at the earliest and the large number of
beneficiaries residing in these areas get proper CGHS facilities.
Recommendations/Observations
3.9 The Committee recommends to the Department to take the issue on priority with the Ministry
of Finance to complete all the procedural formalities so that necessary posts are created and
subsequently filled up. The Committee would also like to be apprised about the cadre strength created
as a result of implementation of SIU report and the efforts taken so far to fill up the vacant posts.
Action Taken
The SIU study was done in all the 20 CGHS covered cities at that relevant time. SIU study
report has been implemented in 19 CGHS covered cities except Delhi which is under process in
consultation with Finance Division in the Ministry. The department is taking up the issue of
creation of posts in all 20 CGHS covered cities.

COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee notes that SIU study report has been implemented in 19 CGHS
covered cities except Delhi which is under process in consultation with the Finance Division
in the Ministry. The Committee has also been informed that issue of creation of posts in
all the 20 CGHS covered cities is being taken up by the Department. The Committee
expresses its deep concern on undue delay taking place in such a vital area. No progress
seems to have been made since the presentation of 27th Report of the Committee on the
Demands for Grants (2008-09) of the Department. The Committee is also not aware about
the time frame for implementation of SIU Study Report in Delhi. The Committee reiterates
that the entire process of creation of posts in all the 20 CGHS covered cities should be
completed in a time-bound manner with monitoring at all levels.

Recommendations/Observations
3.13 The Committee is happy to note that these ad hoc  arrangements would continue until
further orders. The Committee would, however, like to emphasize that empanelment of these
hospitals should be made on permanent basis, specially in view of cancer patients undergoing
prolonged and costly treatment. The Ministry has to play a proactive role in such matters.
Action Taken
The ad hoc arrangement is further notified to continue till December 31st, 2008 or
empanelment of Cancer Hospitals, whichever is earlier. The cancer hospitals in Delhi are not part
of the empanelled hospitals because the rates that emerged out of tendering process was not
acceptable to them. CGHS has constituted a committee headed by the Additional Director General
of Health Services. As cancer treatment involves a complex procedure of surgical intervention and/
or chemotherapy and/or Radiotherapy, the committee has already initiated action for fixation of
various packages and its rates for treatment of cancer patients under CGHS which may be
acceptable to all stake holders. After the exercise is completed, the rates could be offered to the
hospitals and those hospitals which accept the rates would be empanelled under CGHS.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee expresses its strong reservation on the continuance of ad hoc
arrangements with regard to empanelment of cancer hospitals for CGHS beneficiaries. The
Committee is not aware about the progress made by the CGHS Committee with regard to
fixation of various packages/rates for treatment of cancer patients. The Committee can well
imagine the plight of cancer patients till a final decision is arrived at, which would
ultimately depend on the willingness of hospitals for accepting the rates offered leading to
their empanelment. It seems that CGHS beneficiaries, specially the cancer patients, would
continue to suffer. There is an urgent need for review of empanelment procedure so that
a situation does not arise where patients needing extensive medical care continue to be
deprived of the same.

VI. ALL INDIA INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL SCIENCES (AIIMS)
Recommendations/Observations
6.11 The Committee is deeply concerned about the matter as the Institute is one of the best
training grounds for the doctors and that too at highly subsidized rates involving precious tax
payers’ money. The Committee is of the view that this brain drain is on two counts – higher
studies and lucrative assignments. At the same time, the Committee is also of the opinion that some
policy or enactment should be made so that tendency of leaving the Institute by doctors/nurses can
be minimized. This could be in the form of a monetary bond or post qualification compulsory
service. The Committee strongly feels that urgent steps are required to be taken so as to ensure
an ideal working environment for our young talented doctors along with all the incentives
comparable to those enjoyed by private sector doctors to the extent possible. The Committee would
like to be apprised of the steps taken in this regard which should not be only AIIMS specific but
should also cover all the Government medical institutions.
Action Taken
Presently, there is no proposal to consider an enactment of Law requiring trained medical
professional to leave the country only after serving for a requisite number of years in the country.
Presently, there is no proposal to consider an enactment of Law requiring trained medical
professional to leave the country only after serving for a requisite number of years in the country.

48
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
Nobody can deny the fact that our country is facing shortage of medical
professionals. Not only this, precious public money is being used to pay for the subsidized
education in the Government institutions, some of which are renowned ones. The
Committee, therefore, strongly feels that some policy guidelines are  urgently required to be
framed so that trend of our doctors and nurses leaving the country is checked. The
Department can not shy away from its responsibility to frame such a policy which is
required in public interest.

6.12 The Committee is given to understand that the Valiathan Committee had submitted its report
on the functioning of AIIMS more than a year ago. The Committee is surprised to note that only

Family Welfare) with Director, AIIMS on the 1st March, 2008. The Committee was informed that
the matter was now under Government’s consideration. The Committee is not happy with the
undue delay in the implementation of Valiathan Committee recommendations. Any further delay
would simply negate the very purpose of setting up such a Committee, that too at the directive
of the Prime Minister. The Committee, therefore, urges upon the Department to expedite the
implementation of the V aliathan Committee Report.
Action Taken
V aliathan Committee has made a number of recommendations for overall development of
AIIMS with reference to its aims and objectives and improvement in its functioning. The report
is under active consideration of the Government.

14th November, 2008, the representatives of the Department inter alia  stated that action on
some of its recommendations had taken place. It was also stated that the recommendations
pertain to some short-term and some long-term goals. Action in case of short-term goals
would take place within 5-6 months and in other cases it would take a longer period. While
recommending speedy action on the recommendations of the Valiathan Committee, the

scrutiny of the proposals received from the State Governments from the beginning of financial year

Recommendations/Observations
14.3 The Committee observes that even after more than a decade of setting up of the Fund with
50% of the contribution made by the State/UT to be provided by Central Government, there are
still several States/UTs which have not set up the State Illness Assistance Fund. Every State/UTs
should have such a fund. The Committee would appreciate if this matter is taken up at the highest
level in all such States/UTs.
Action Taken
Almost all the States have established this RAN Scheme. This Ministry is regularly
reminding the States of Uttar Pradesh, Assam, Orissa, Manipur, Arunachal Pradesh, Meghalaya and

53
Nagaland for setting up of State Illness Fund. Recently, Secretary, Health and Family Welfare, has
again reminded the States/UTs through D.O. letters addressed to all Chief Secretary/Resident
Commissioners on 14th November, 2007. Further, the Department is thinking of D.O. letters to the
States/UTs Chief Ministers/Health Ministers for expediting the work of setting up of State Illness
Fund.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee notes that the Department has approached the State Governments of
U.P., Assam, Orissa, Manipur, Arunachal Pradesh, Meghalaya and Nagaland for setting up
of State Illness Assistance Fund. The Committee strongly feels that there is an urgent need
for having such a fund in these States. The Committee once again urges upon the
Department to take up this vital matter of public interest with the concerned State
Governments at the highest level.
Recommendations/Observations
14.4 The Committee feels that in order to facilitate easy access to quality health care to the poor
and down trodden strata of our society, the limit of Rs. 50,000 under immediate financial assistance
fund, per case, for which funds are placed at the disposal of Medical Superintendents of above
hospitals, need to be enhanced realistically for treatment of dreaded diseases like AIDS, Cancer etc.
Some of the major states like U.P., Assam, Karnataka, Kerala and Jammu and Kashmir do not have
such a fund. With only a very few major hospitals located in metros having extended facility under
the fund, people of such States/UTs are being deprived of medical care for no fault of theirs. From
the details of funds released to States/UTs during 2005-08, it is very clear that such states are
having negligible funds at their disposal. The Committee wonders about the adequacy of such State
Funds. The Committee is of the view that with the increasing rate of inflation and also increasing
number of patients suffering from major diseases, an assessment of the Rashtriya Arogya Nidhi
should be made at the earliest. Necessary changes may be made thereafter. The facility of financial
assistance should be extended to more hospitals so as to cover the geographical limits of the
country. The Committee also recommends to the Department to give wide publicity to the scheme
specially in the hospital campuses to generate awareness so that the same can be utilized by the
needy and poor patients.
Action Taken
Regarding providing of immediate financial assistance to the extent of Rs.50,000/- per case
to critically ill, poor patients, living below the poverty line and undergoing treatment, the Medical
Superintendent of Government Hospitals/Institutes have been provided with a revolving fund of
Rs.10 to 20 lakhs. The proposal of financial assistance has been increased to less than or
equal to Rs.1 lakh per case and the proposal has been accepted by Managing Committee of RAN/
HFM.
Regarding the North East States, the NEIGRIHMS, Shillong and Regional Institute of
Medical Sciences Hospital (RIMS), Imphal has requested that the RAN Scheme may also be
extended to this Institute on the lines of AIIMS, PGI, Chandigarh, JIPMER., Puducherry etc.. The
proposal has been accepted by the Managing Committee of RAN.
Regarding the facility of financial assistance extended to more super specialty hospitals/
Institutes, in State/UTs level, the Ministry will write to State/UTs Governments to set up revolving
funds from the State Illness Funds.

54
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee welcomes the Department’s move regarding increase in financial
assistance to less than or equal to Rs.1 lakh per case and extension of the facility to the
NEIGRIHMS, Shillong and Regional Institute of Medical Sciences Hospital (RIMS), Imphal.
The Committee would appreciate if steps are taken for a realistic increase in the revolving
fund to cater to the demands of poor patients and some concrete action is taken for
extension of the scheme to other hospitals. The Committee would also like to point out that
the Department’s reply is silent on the issue of giving wide publicity to the scheme
especially in the hospital campuses to generate awareness so that the same can be utilized
