ASHA
Recommendations/Observations
3.12 Capacity building of ASHA is critical in enhancing her effectiveness as a link between the
community and the health facility. The initial orientation training is for a period of 23 days spread
in 5 rounds over a period of 12 months and followed by periodic re-training for about 2 days once
every alternate month, apart from the on-the-job training she would be getting. Training modules
for this purpose have been developed based on a thematic approach and States have also

90
constituted district and block training teams for this purpose. The Department has informed the
Committee, during its discussion on demand for grants, that selection of 5,87,512 ASHA has been
completed, 4,90,000 ASHA have completed their first round training and 2,18,339 ASHA have been
provided with drug kits.
The Committee is happy to note the progress made by the Department on the ASHA
initiative under NRHM. It is also happy to note that 86.41 % ASHA have been trained in their first
module post induction training till 12th March, 2008. The Committee observes that ASHAs have to
undergo three more rounds of training. So far, only 27% ASHAs have undergone 2nd round of
training. The Committee is of the view that every effort needs to be made to ensure that second
round of training of all the ASHAs is completed at the earliest.
The Committee has its apprehensions regarding the effectivity of ASHA as a multipurpose
social health activist, as envisaged under the NRHM. The Committee observes that job
responsibilities of ASHA are manifold as indicated below:—
– Awareness generation - Health, RCH, HIV/AIDS.
– Advising newly married couples for their special needs.
– Guiding pregnant women for availing of institutional facilities for Anti-Natal, Natal and
Post-Natal needs.
– Counselling on nutrition, pregnancy, breast-feeding, immunization and family planning.
– Establishing  link with PHCs, private providers and others for service requirement of
beneficiaries.
– Mobilizing parents for child survival interventions.
– Promoting basic sanitization.
– Provision of non-clinical spacing contraceptives.
– Surveillance of communicable diseases.
Action Taken
Training under various modules is at different stages in the States. The second module
training is complete in Rajasthan, Jharkhand, Madhya Pradesh and Uttarakhand. In Orissa and Uttar
Pradesh 2nd, 3rd and 4th modules have been combined and the training is on. It will be complete
by September, 2008 in Orissa. In Bihar also 2nd, 3rd and 4th modules are being combined and
training is to commence soon as training of Trainers is complete.
State facilitators have been deputed in Jharkhand, Madhya Pradesh, Orissa, Uttarakhand and
NE States to facilitate the training programme.
ASHAs are performing various tasks. National Health System Resource Centre (NHSRC) is
reviewing the ‘job responsib ilities’ and “Role clarity of ASHA”.
Recommendations/Observations
3.13 ASHA is also the primary provider for National Health Programme. She is the Depot Holder
for health commodities like Vitamin A. Impregnated bed nets. ORS etc. She is also supposed to
handle treatment of minor ailments. Another major task assigned to ASHA is to provide escort
services for women seeking delivery services at institutions, requiring emergency care and MTP
services, women and men for clinical contraception services and VCTC. The Committee has been

91
given to understand that States have also taken up the training of ASHAs on diagnosis of malaria
cases. For being eligible for ASHA. a woman has to be 25 to 35 years of age, married, resident
of the village, acceptable to the village and community and proficient in reading and writing. The
Committee fails to understand as to how a single woman, who is a simple villager, can take care
of one thousand population by performing such wide-ranging tasks. Situation becomes more
complex and well away from ground realities when one finds that only remuneration payable to
ASHA is for providing Escort Services. Instances of Escort Services may not be very frequent
some times. The Committee strongly feels that ASHA is supposed to play a very challenging role.
Action Taken
ASHA is already performing wide ranging tasks for which incentives are being given. The
list of remuneration and activities is at Annexure-V.
Recommendations/Observations
3.14 The Committee is of the view that with the passage of time, the number of responsibilities
of ASHA will itself become a challenge for her to cope up with. This will not only reflect in the
quality of her work and performance but ultimately fail in fulfilling the very objectives with which
the initiative was initially conceptualized. As the initiative is still in the early stages, the Committee
suggests that the Department should review the responsibilities entrusted to the ASHA. Committee’s
attention has been drawn by the findings of Common Review Mission which conducted appraisal
of NRHM in November, 2007, 16 months after NRHM got final Cabinet approval in July, 2006.
Appraisal was based on one-two districts in AP, Assam, Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Orissa, M.P., Gujarat,
J&K, Rajasthan, Tamil Nadu, Tripura, U.P. and West Bengal. The Committee notes that
performance assessment of ASHA has led to mixed findings in different States. Non-payment
against activities/conflicts regarding compensation money, and lack of role clarity between ASHAs
and AWWs are two major problem areas identified. The Committee apprehends that situation may
worsen with more complexities arising in future. The Committee, therefore, is of the view that role
of ASHA needs to be reassessed and reformulated.
Action Taken
We agree with the observations. Accordingly, NHSRC has agreed to review the
responsibilities of ASHA, AWW and ANM.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee notes with a sense of relief that the Department has at last come to
concur with the observations of the Committee regarding its apprehensions on the role clarity
and feasibility of discharging the duties assigned to ASHA. The Committee would like to state
here that these apprehensions were voiced by it in earlier reports so that the objective with
which the concept of ASHA was initiated under the NRHM doesn’t get diluted. The
Department has stated in its Action Taken Note that National Health System Resource Centre
(NHSRC) is reviewing the ‘job r esponsibilities’ and “Role clarity of ASHA” as well as that of
AWW and ANM. The Committee observes that the Department has not specified any
timeframe, within which NHSRC would be completing its review. In the meantime, these
Social Health Activists would continue discharging their duties originally mandated with the
likelihood of new ones adding to their responsibilities. The NHSRC has to complete its review
in a time bound manner so that such an important health initiative does not become
redundant. The Committee, therefore, urges upon the Department to ensure the timely
submission of the Report by NHSRC, followed by expeditious implementation of the same.

92
MOBILE MEDICAL UNITS (MMUs)
Recommendations/Observations
3.16.1 During 2006-07, though the target set was for providing MMUs to all 600 districts in the
country, only 318 districts could be provided with such units. As a result, operationalisation of
those 318 MMUs sanctioned in 2006-07, was set as the target to be achieved during 2007-08 under
the scheme. However, as informed by the Department, only 319 districts have been funded for
MMUs and operationalisation of only 198 MMVs could be achieved. The Committee had pointed
out to the Department last year that the names of bigger States like Bihar, Jharkhand, M.P,
Maharashtra, U.P. did not register among those that had disbursed funds for providing MMUs in
the States. The Committee had also pointed out that these 318 districts are distributed among 18
States only. The Department, in its action taken note, had replied that these states had incorporated
and provided funds for MMUs in their Programme Implementation Plan (PIP-2007-08) under
NRHM. It seems that incorporation and provision of funds for MMUs in the Programme
Implementation Plan (2007-08) of Bihar, Maharashtra and U.P. has not been followed by
procurement of MMUs in these States. The Committee notes that MMUs were to be operationalised
in 400 districts by March, 2008. However, as per the latest figures there is no increase in the
number of 198 functional MMUs.
Against such a backdrop, chances of improvement do not seem to be possible in the near
future. The Committee understands that many States are having their own set-up of ambulances
which seem to be functioning well. There are also States where NGOs are involved for this
service. The Committee feels that based on the findings of Common Review Mission in respect of
some States, steps may be taken for strengthening the facility of MMUs.
Action Taken
The operationalisation of the MMV has increased to 200 districts of the country. The States
of Bihar, Maharashtra and U.P. have been urged in the meeting to expedite the procurement of
MMUs and the States in the NPCC meeting have reported progress in the procurement process.
Besides MMUs, arrangements for emergency ambulance services are also being made in States on
a very large scale. It is likely to improve access to health services even in remote areas.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee observes with constraint that no significant progress has been made
in the operationalisation of Mobile Medical Units (MMUs). As per the latest status furnished
by the Department, only 200 districts in the country are having this facility. Committee’s
apprehensions about states like Bihar, Maharashtra and U.P. failing to procure MMUs
inspite of provision of funds therefor continue to persist as confirmed in the response of
the Department. Notwithstanding availability of emergency ambulance services on a very
large scale in some states, the Committee would like to emphasize that effective steps need
to be taken for strengthening the facility of MMUs in the entire country. This task can be
accomplished if follow-up action on the constraints highlighted in this regard in the
1st Common Review Mission (November, 2007) and second Common Review Mission
(November, 2008) is taken by the Department in co-ordination with concerned States.
Recommendations/Observations
3.16.2 The Committee had the opportunity to apprise itself about the key findings of the appraisal
conducted by the Common Review Mission in November, 2007. The study is based on team visits
made to one or two districts in Andhra Pradesh., Assam, Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Orissa, M.P., Gujarat,

93
J&K, Rajasthan, Tamil Nadu, Tripura, U.P. and West Bengal. The Committee finds that assessment
of NRHM on 24 parameters has been made through this appraisal. The Committee takes note of
the constraints being faced by these States in the implementation of NRHM and the
recommendations on the areas that need strengthening and course correction. While appreciating
this initiative, the Committee is of the view that such an appraisal needs to be followed by intensive
interaction with all the implementing agencies in the States at the earliest. Otherwise the very
purpose of undertaking such an exercise will be defeated. The Committee also strongly feels that
such Common Review Missions should be conducted in all the States, with one region being taken
at a time. It would also be a good idea if two-three performing and non-performing districts in
a State are taken together for making a comparative analysis. Incorporation of surprise visits will
make the assessment more realistic.
Action Taken
The 1st Common Review Mission (CRM) was held in the month of November, 2007 as part
of the Mission Steering Group’s mandate of review and concurrent evaluation. The CRM had
identified areas of achievement and constraint and made recommendations for corrective action.
The Report was also shared with all States/UTs and corrective actions were reported by States
during the NPCC meetings. The 2nd CRM of NRHM has been scheduled in the month of
November, 2008 for 13 States. It would be visiting these States for assessing the improvements
and constraints of NRHM at various levels.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee hopes that the Second Common Review Mission of NRHM of 13
States scheduled for November, 2008 must have been reviewed and corrective steps initiated
by the concerned States. The Committee is, however, not aware as to whether its suggestion
for including both performing and non-performing districts in the States selected for CRM
has been implemented. If not, the Committee would emphasize that in the absence of such
a framework, the very purpose of the Review would remain incomplete.
NATIONAL VECTOR BORNE DISEASE CONTROL  PROGRAMME
Recommendations/Observations
4.7 The Committee notes that increased supervision and monitoring is one of the main strategies
adopted for effective control of malaria. The latest figures made available to the Committee reveal
continuance of shortage of surveillance and supervisory staff in NE States. In the biggest State of
NE Region, Assam against sanctioned posts of 694 Health Supervisor (Male) only 570 were in
position, against 343 Health Supervisors (Female), there were 316 and only 1647 MPW (M) against
sanctioned posts of 3080. What is more disturbing is that while Tripura failed to furnish the data,
Meghalaya and Nagaland have provided incomplete information. The primary role of MPW (M) and
MPW (F) is door to door surveillance and detection of communicable diseases and it’s reporting.
The fact that these functionaries play a vital role in disease detection and surveillance cannot be
ignored. By not appointing these functionaries, the States leave a big gap at the field-level disease
surveillance, detection and treatment. This vital issue needs to be pursued at the highest level in
the concerned States. The other strategy adopted is use of Rapid Diagnostic Kits by ASHAs. The
Committee does not have high expectation from this initiative as firstly, required numbers of ASHAs
have to be in place in malaria endemic States and even if they are there, they would be over-
burdened with the various tasks already assigned to them. The Committee fails to comprehend the
continuance of such a discouraging situation inspite of special dispensation for NE States. The
Committee can only reiterate that there is need for drastic measures and extensive monitoring under
the supervision of State Nodal Officers.

94
Action Taken
The extensive monitoring and supervision by the State Nodal Officers through the visits are
being done. The States are continuously being reminded to take remedial measures, fill up the
vacant positions and appoint the contractual technical manpower provided under the GFATM
project. Assam has recruited 461 lab technicians under NRHM whereas Arunachal Pradesh,
Mizoram and Nagaland have filled their allotted technical manpower like MTS and LTs under
GFATM. Other States have also initiated the recruitment process. Further, 5057 contractual MPWs
(male) have been sanctioned to 14 high malaria endemic States under NVBDCP during 2007-08 out
ohvhich Nagaland has recruited 100 MPWs as per allotment, and other States like Tripura, Manipur,
Arunachal Pradesh and Assam have initiated the process of recruitment. States have geared up and
actions have been initiated by them to fill up the vacancies.
The monitoring has also been strengthened by the State level officers as well as through
NRHM. The NRHM review meeting held at Imphal, Manipur on 13.05.08 under the Chairmanship
of Hon’b1e HFM has reflected that various processes for malaria control have been intensified
with. the involvement of district level administration.
ASHA is being involved in the community based Malaria control activities in the high malaria
transmission areas by training them in the RDT use and administration of anti-malarials.
Considering the workload of the ASHA, initially 61 Districts of 11 States have been identified for
involvement of ASHAs.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
Apprehensions of the Committee with regard to shortage of surveillance and
supervisory staff in Malaria-endemic N-E States continue to persist. Out of 5057 contractual
MPWs (male) sanctioned in all the 14 high malaria-endemic States under  NVBDCP during
2007-08, with the exception of Nagaland, remaining N-E States like Tripura, Manipur,
Arunachal Pradesh and Assam have only initiated the process of recruitment. No mention
being made about other health functionaries like Health Supervisor (Male) and (Female) by
the Department is self- revealing. The Committee has been persistently drawing the
attention of the Department towards the problem of sanctioned posts of Health workers
remaining vacant. But it seems no viable solution has emerged so far . Involving ASHAs in
malaria-control activities can only be considered a stop-gap arrangement, that too not viable
due to their being already over-burdened with manifold tasks. The Committee can only
reiterate that the matter of filling up of sanctioned posts of Health workers in malaria-
endemic N-E States needs to the taken up at the highest level by the Department.
JAPANESE ENCEPHALITIS
Recommendations/Observations
4.10 Japanese Encephalitis (JE) is a viral disease with a tendency of seasonal outbreaks. JE has
been reported repeatedly from 15 States out of which, Andhra Pradesh, Bihar, Haryana, Karnataka,
Kerala, Maharashtra, Manipur, Tamil Nadu, Uttar Pradesh and West Bengal have been reporting
repeated outbreaks. There is no specific anti viral drug for JE and cases are managed
symptomatically. Efforts have been directed to provide technical support and strengthen capabilities
of States through training to tackle this disease. A vaccination programme for children between 1
and 15 years of age under the Universal Immunization Programme, using single dose live attenuated
SA-14-14-2 vaccine has been launched in 2006. In 2006, 11 districts in 4 States were covered
which was expanded to 28 districts in 10 States during 2007. A total of 93.08 lakh children were
vaccinated against the target of 105.31 lakh children achieving 88.39% coverage. As per the Annual

95
Report, 2007-08, the vaccination campaign has so far been completed in Assam (89%), Haryana
(86%), West Bengal (61 %) and in 7 districts of Uttar Pradesh (99.7%).
The Committee is dismayed to note that despite various methods adopted by the Department
over the past year, to contain the disease, the number of cases reported to be suffering from JE/
AES has shown an increase over the previous year figures. The fact is even more disturbing that
though the Department claims to have completed 99.97% immunization in the past year in 7 worst
affected districts in Uttar Pradesh, the situation there has worsened with 2675 cases and 577
deaths in 2007 (till December) over the 2006 figures of 2320 cases and 528 deaths. The Committee
fails to comprehend as to why inspite of a number of preventive measures taken by the Central
Government in co-ordination with the State Government of Uttar Pradesh, the JE/AES related cases
and deaths continue to be high in the State. The Committee apprehends that the actual figures
would increase the tally much further. The Committee would like to take the opportunity to State
that though the data for Assam has not been made available by the Department, going by the fact
sheet of 2006, it is apprehensive that the situation in Assam would also show a similar trend as
that of U.P.
Action Taken
About 88% was covered during 2007 in 27 districts. 4022 cases and 963 deaths were
reported.
Infection with JE virus may be asymptomatic or may cause febrile illness, meningitis,
myelitis or encephalitis. Encephalitis is the most commonly recognized presentation of JE and is
clinically indistinguishable from other causes of acute encephalitis syndrome (AES) which may
include meningococcal meningitis, TB meningo encephalitis, viral encephalitis, JE, Cerebrospinal
meningitis and even cerebral malaria, hence since 2006 Dte. of NVBDCP issued guidelines to report
JE under AES. The CSF/sera samples collected for AES cases are subjected to serological testing
and for the past three years, JE positivity has fallen from 39% in 2005 to 14.2% and 13% in 2006
and 2007 respectively. Though 51 Sentinel sites with manpower and equipment for serological
testing have been identified in 12 JE endemic States by Dte. NVBDCP, all the samples are not
tested due to constraints in laboratory testing. Secondly, in Eastern Uttar Pradesh, apart from JE
other viruses like enterovirus (EV76&89 strains) are circulating in the community which was
established by NIV, Pune during 2006 as out of 306 CSF and 179 sera samples, only 36% and
18% JE positivity was reported from these samples. In addition, 21.56% (EV76&89 strains) and
1.96% Coxsackie ‘B’ was detected in 6 CSF samples. It is pertinent to note that vaccination is
given against JE only.
Recommendations/Observations
4.11 The Committee would like to draw the attention of the Department to the fact that the
year 2006 had reported a visible impact of preventive measures initiated by registering a declining
trend when compared with 2005 figures. Quantum jump in the first three quarter of 2007 calls for
a serious review with special focus on Assam and U.P. the two main endemic States. The
Committee would impress upon the Department that analysis of epidemiological and entomological
data for epidemic outbreak prediction and timely remedial measures is required to be carried out
on an urgent basis.
The Committee feels that perhaps the precautionary methods adopted by the Department
have not percolated properly to the affected population because of which the disease has once
again registered an increase. The Committee adds that lax monitoring of the disease by the Central
as well as the State Governments could aggravate the situation further unless it is dealt with a
sense of urgency. It suggests the Department to undertake on-the-spot review of the situation as

96
to the possible causes for upsurge in the reported cases. The Committee urges the Department to
step up the ongoing mc activities in the endemic States so that the desired objective is reached
soon.
Action Taken
The JE situation in Eastern UP is reviewed at regular intervals by Additional DG and
Director, NICD. The last meeting was held on 12.05.08. Apart from above, Dte. of NVBDCP set
up JE sub-office and Vector Borne Disease Control unit (VBDCU) in Gorakhpur in February and
April, 2007 respectively. The main task assigned to the sub-office of VBDCU is to closely monitor
JE situation by making field visits, collecting entomological/epidemiological data, analyzing and
interpreting the same in consultation with Dte. of NVBDCP and sharing it with district authorities
for immediate remedial measures. During 2007, two multidisciplinary Central Teams were deputed
to Gorakhpur and Saharanpur districts (30.7.07 to 6.8.07) and 25-31 October respectively.
Recommendations of both these teams were discussed with concerned State/district officials for
immediate corrective measures. The States are also supported by providing funds for various JE
components. JE kits are also supplied to all JE endemic States by Dte. NVBDCP. During 2008, all
the States were sent advisories for preparing Action Plan on Prevention and Control of AES/JE on
5.02.08. It was followed by sending a check list indicating month wise activities on 25.02.08. On
19.03.08 a one day workshop was organized at BRD Medical College, Gorakhpur on
clinical manifestations and case management. Experts from NVBDCP helped the States/district
authorities to draw up a field oriented Action Plan in a meeting held under the Chairpersonship of
Principal Secretary (Health) and F.W., Government of Uttar Pradesh on 20.03.08. A central multi
disciplinary team of 6 experts also visited the State and BRD, Medical College, Gorakhpur w.e.f.
13-15 May, 2008 for making an on the spot assessment on the requirement of manpower, civil
works and equipment for establishing AES treatment unit as desired by HFM, Government of India.
Dte. NVBDCP organized 2 days workshop cum training at Lucknow on surveillance and
case management on 30-31 July, 2008 which was attended by 40 participants including 35 officials
from 11 endemic districts and 5 State officials.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee observes that a number of measures have been initiated by the
Department in co-ordination with the State authorities with regard to control of Japanese
Encephalitis (JE) in eastern U.P., the most endemic area. The Committee also takes note
of two problem areas - one of constraints in lab-testing and prevalence of other viruses,
apart from JE with vaccination being available only against JE. The Committee fails to
understand why, inspite of adequate funds being provided under the Programme, proper
sample testing facilities have been lacking. The Committee would like to State here that
lack of proper serological testing facilities would result in improper detection of infected
cases and could aggravate the situation further. The Committee suggests that mobile labs
for conducting the required test may be introduced so as to supplement the existing
facilities and to overcome the constraints faced in lab testing. The Committee would
appreciate if such an initiative is implemented at the earliest by the Directorate of NVBDCP
after due consultation with the State and district authorities in the JE infested Areas in
Assam and U.P.
The Committee would like to point out that problem of other viruses, apart from JE
in eastern UP was deteted by NIV , Pune in 2006. By now, strategy for tackling these viruses
including vaccination should have been in place and being implemented. The Committee,
therefore, impresses upon the Department to take the required action on an urgent basis.

97
CHIKUNGUNYA
Recommendations/Observations
4.16 State-wise analysis of suspected Chikungunya cases and number of confirmed cases during
2006 and 2007 reveals a very disturbing trend about the spread of this disease. In 2006, maximum
number of confirmed cases were reported from Maharashtra (804) followed by Karnataka (298)
and Gujarat (225). In contrast, Kerala reported the highest number of confirmed cases of 909 while
there are 347 confirmed cases occurring in West Bengal in 2007 up till November. Another cause
of serious concern is overall increase in total number of confirmed Chikungunya cases from 2001
in 2006 to 1876 in 2007 (upto November).
Recommendations/Observations
4.17 Incidence of Chikungunya cases in the country during 2006 and 2007 has led the
Committee to the conclusion that various initiatives taken by the Department have so far failed to
yield any desired results. The Committee strongly feels that with the enhanced allocation of
Rs.14.59 crore in 2008-09 for Dengue and Chikungunya, effective measures are required to be
undertaken in the affected States. Mere circulation of detailed guidelines and advisories for
prevention and control of these diseases to all affected States would not serve any purpose. What
is required is to identify the affected districts and go for regular fever surveillance in such endemic
areas to detect an unusual trend. The Committee is of the view that if this analysis of ground
reality is followed by IEC activities and strengthening of apex Referral Institutions for diagnosis and
treatment, number of Dengue/Chikungunya cases would show a declining trend. The Committee
apprehends that unless adequate remedial measures are taken, sporadic outburst of these diseases
would continue to be reported from different States every year.
Action Taken
During 2007,59535 cases for Chikungunya were reported. Total 7850 samples tested out of
which 1826 was confirmed for Chikungunya virus.
During 2006-07, Rs.21.60 crores was released as emergency package to the affected States
for items like Fogging Machines, Pyrethrum Extract, Temephos and Insecticide-treated bed nets
and IEC activity. Subsequently in 2007-08, Rs.8.75 crores was released as emergency package to
the affected States for items like Pyrethrum Extract, Temephos and IEC activities including social
mobilization of the community.
During 2006, Chikungunya disease appeared in the country after acquiescence of almost
three decades. The community did not have immunity against this disease and 1390322 suspected
cases of Chikungunya were reported by 16 States/UTs respectively. Only 15961 samples (1.15%
of suspected cases) were tested, of which 2001 were confirmed for Chikungunya virus (12.5%
of the samples tested).
In 2007, due to the intensification of implementation of the strategic action plan for
prevention and control of Dengue and Chikungunya in the country including community based
vector control and intensification of focused IEC activities, the number of suspected cases were
only 59535 i.e. 95.72% less than 2006 from 14 States. Besides, in 2007 Government of India had
established 110 Sentinel Surveillance hospitals in the affected States for proactive surveillance and
diagnosis. In addition, 13 Apex Referral Laboratories were also identified and linked with these 110
Sentinel Surveillance Hospitals for providing advanced diagnostic facility and back up support.
Therefore, due to better facility in 2007 more samples could be tested and confirmed, total 7850
samples tested (13.2% of suspected cases) and 1826 confirmed for Chikungunya virus (23.3% of
the samples tested).

98
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee appreciates the efforts made by the Department in intensifying multi
pronged approaches to contain the incidence of Dengue and Chikungunya cases. The
Committee feels that results of such measures would surely reflect in the numbers of cases
detected for the year 2008. The Committee would like to be apprised about the IEC
interventions undertaken in this regard.
Committee’s attention is, however, drawn to the number of samples tested against
the suspected cases. The Committee notes that out of 59,535 suspected cases in 2007 only
13.2% were tested and of these 13.2% cases (7,850 samples tested), 23.3% of the samples
turned out to be confirmed cases. The Committee feels that 13.2% samples tested is a
fairly low number compared to the suspected cases figures and given the fact that more
than 23% turned out to be confirmed cases of the 7,850 tested samples, the figures could
be much more if the number of samples tested were increased.
The Committee apprehends that in such a situation, there are chances of there being
a large number of confirmed yet undetected cases. The Committee, therefore, suggests that
the Department should concentrate its efforts towards strengthening the testing lab
facilities so that maximum number of samples are tested.
V. NATIONAL T.B. CONTROL PROGRAMME
Recommendations/Observations
5.7 The Department has informed the Committee that over 2400 NGOs, 17,000 Private
practitioners, and 120 corporate houses have been involved in the provision of RNTCP services in
the Programme during 2007-08 under various components of the Programme. The Committee, in
its earlier report, had suggested that a forum can be evolved where participating NGOs/PPs get the
opportunity to share their experiences with similar workers from other States. At the same time,
the Committee would also recommend that the evaluation of the work being done by NGOs/PPs
be made under the Routine Programme Evaluations carried out by the States (2 districts per
quarter) and the Centre (2 districts per month) in order to review the progress made under the
mc initiatives and suggest proper utilisation of the funds allocated under the scheme. In reply to
the above recommendation, the Department had stated that in all the Central Evaluations conducted
in two districts per quarter, a representative from CTD goes to review the PPM activities both at
the district and State level. Similarly, the State evaluations also carry out the PPM review with
respect to utilization of funds and activities undertaken by the NGOs.
The Committee would like the Department to furnish details of the evaluation reports as
stated above with respect to utilization of funds and activities undertaken by the NGOs. The
Committee would also like to suggest here some measures for increasing advocacy, communication
and social mobilization. It recommends that the Department should sit with the Private Print and
Media sector and should work out modalities as to how they can be involved in the IEC activities
for the programme.
Action Taken
Under the Revised National TB Control Programme (RNTCP), involvement of NGOs has
been decentralized to the District level and MOUs are signed with the District TB Societies. Funds
to NGOs are released by the concerned District TB Society in accordance with the terms and
conditions of the MOUs. Many NGOs also participate without any financial assistance. Evaluation
of performance of NGOs/PPs is an integral part of Routine Programme Evaluations carried out by
the States and the Centre. Few examples of different schemes being implemented by the NGOs in

99
the districts and reviewed during internal evaluation of the districts are placed at Annexure-VI. In
addition, an independent evaluation of selected NGOs will be conducted and the report is likely to
be available by the third quarter of current year.
There was a Consultation on NGO/PP guidelines on 29-31st Jananuary, 2008 wherein 60-
70 NGO/Private practitioners were invited from all over the country to share their experiences in
Public private partnership in RNTCP implementation. The successful models as well as constraints
were discussed in detail and recommendations were made to increase the scope and scale of
current NGO/PP Guidelines.
ACSM strategy in RNTCP II focuses on strengthening advocacy and social mobilization
activities. Strong advocacy is required with the professional bodies, medical colleges for
popularizing DOTS and rational use of second line drugs.
Programme has plans to address this through electronic and print media, and also working
closely with the professional bodies with the support and collaboration of media agency.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee is happy to note that the Department had acted on its suggestion
for evolving a national forum where participating NGOs/PPs  share their experiences of
providing services under RNTCP. The Committee hopes that 60-70 NGOs/PPs participating
in the conference held on 29-31st January, 2008 must have given some concrete
suggestions for successful implementation of RNTCP. The Committee would like to be
apprised about the report on an independent evaluation of selected NGOs conducted by the
Department.
The Department has also informed that in order to strengthen advocacy and social
mobilization activities under the programme, it is closely working with professional bodies
with the support and collaboration of media agency. The Committee would like to be
apprised in detail about its plan under the proposed initiative and the outcome of such
enterprise.
Recommendations/Observations
5.8 Reacting to a query on the status of research by the Tuberculosis Research Centre, Chennai
that is currently involved in the designing stage of a new TB drug the Department has informed
that Johnson & Johnson pharmaceuticals approached Tuberculosis Research Centre, Chennai (TRC)
with the protocol for the multi-centric trial for a Phase II, placebo-controlled, double blind,
randomized trial to evaluate the anti-bacterial activity, safety and tolerability of TMC 207 in subjects
with sputum smear positive pulmonary infection with multi-drug resistant Mycobacterium
tuberculosis (MDR-TB). After extensive discussions with senior researchers at TRC followed by
the members of 35th Scientific Advisory Committee (SAC) of TRC in November, 2007 as well as
by a Sub-Committee constituted by the SAC, the protocol now is ready to be presented to the
Institutional Ethics Committee (1EC) TRC in the month of March, 2008. In the meantime, the Drug
Controller of GoI has given clearance for the trial. It is likely to start in the month of May, 2008.
The Committee is happy to note that the pharma company Johnson & Johnson has
undertaken the responsibility for developing a drug for treatment of patients with Multi-Drug
Resistant TB. The Committee has been given to understand that the cost of treatment of MDR-
TB patients is very high. The Committee feels that the issue of cost effectiveness of the drug needs
to be examined simply because patients in India are generally poor and lack means to afford the
drugs. Under the circumstances, the affordability of the drug could be construed as a blessing for
the poor and downtrodden.

100
Action Taken
The Programme Division is conscious of the aspect of affordability of the drug for the
patients as well as by the government. Before any new Drug is adopted by the programme, it will
have to meet the criteria of not only safety and efficacy but also of cost, affordability and evidence
based acceptability of the drug under programmatic conditions. Further, adoption of any new drug
in the programme will be based upon recommendations/ consensus arising out of consultative
process involving all stakeholders.
In view of the rolling of management of MDR-TB cases, the programme plans to lay a lot
of emphasis on preventive aspects.
The programme emphasizes on the prevention of MDR-TB through sustained high quality
DOTS implementation by all health care providers in the public and private sector.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee appreciates the Department’s concerns and efforts on the prevention
of MDR- TB through the administration of high quality DOTS and the Programme Division’s
concerns regarding the availability of effective affordable drugs. However, the Committee
feels that perhaps the Department has missed the import of the Committee’s comment
seeking further information on the status regarding the trials of the affordable drug for
treatment of patients with Multi-Drug Resistant TB, developed by the pharma company
Johnson & Johnson. The Committee would like to be apprised of the recent developments
in the matter in detail-and the preliminary findings of the trial reports, if any, of the drug.
VI. NATIONAL LEPROSY ERADICATION PROGRAMME
Recommendations/Observations
6.5 Another initiative proposed by the Department is provision of an amount of Rs.5000/- for
reconstructive surgery to leprosy disabled persons from BPL families. 19 medical colleges/hospitals
in Bihar, Orissa, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Tamil Nadu, Chhattisgarh, U.P and New Delhi have been
identified therefor. The Committee has been informed that reconstructive surgeries are being
conducted for medical rehabilitation of such leprosy affected persons in four Central Institutions
located at Chengalpattu, Agra, Raipur (Chhattisgarh) and Aska (Orissa). The Committee has been
given to understand that efforts are being made to start services for medical rehabilitation in seven
Medical Colleges and nine Physical Medicine Rehabilitation Institutions spread across the country.
The Committee notes that few NGOs have also been given recognition for conducting
reconstructive surgeries.
The Committee welcomes the initiatives taken by the Department to provide reconstruction
treatment to leprosy disabled persons. The Committee would appreciate if modalities for
implementing this new scheme are finalized at the earliest. The Committee would appreciate if this
scheme takes off at the earliest. The Committee would, however, emphasize that strict monitoring
should be ensured so that really needy persons are benefited. Adequate publicity also needs to be
given. All the stake-holders, both implementing agencies and targeted beneficiaries, need to be made
aware of this new initiative proposed under the programme.
Action Taken
The new initiative of providing incentive of Rs.5000/- for Reconstructive Surgery (RCS) to
leprosy disabled persons from BPL families and to 20 institutions for conducting RCS has been
proposed in the EFC for continuation of NLEP in the 11th plan period. The EFC has already been

101
approved by Secretary, MoH and FW and the matter has been processed for obtaining suitable date
from EFC for presentation of the programme.
Adequate publicity is being given to this scheme and it will be ensured that needy persons
are benefited by the new initiatives.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee observes that the proposal for providing incentive of Rs. 5000/- for
Reconstructive Surgery to leprosy disabled persons from BPL families and to 20 institutions
for conducting RCS is yet to be approved by EFC, though more than half of the Eleventh
Plan period is over. Such an undue delay, that too on a proposal already going on a pilot
basis in four Central institutions, is uncalled for. The Committee, therefore, urges upon the
Department to expedite the completion of all the procedural formalities.
VII. NA TIONAL PROGRAMME FOR CONTROL OF BLINDNESS
Recommendations/Observations
7.4 The Committee, is, however, surprised to find that implementation of new initiatives
depends on the approval of EFC Memo by the Ministry of Finance and CCEA. Till then, the funds
allocated for 2008-09 will continue to be utilized as per the existing scheme approved for the Tenth
Plan. The Committee is not happy with this State of affairs. Second year of Eleventh Plan has
already started and by the time approval of the competent authority is finally received, the year
2008-09 would be nearing its end. The Committee would like to point out that some of the new
initiatives like construction of dedicated Eye Wings/Eye OTs in District Hospitals, appointment of
manpower, development of mobile Ophthalmic Units in NE States will have to undergo a number
of procedural formalities before their actually taking off. The Committee accordingly recommends
that concerted efforts need to be made for getting the formal approval at the earliest. The
Committee would appreciate if simultaneously preparatory activities are also started.
Action Taken
The EFC Memo for Eleventh Five Year Plan for NPCB is under active consideration in
consultation with the Integrated Finance Division, MoH and FW and Planning Commission. In view
of certain observations of Planning Commission, the proposal has been revised and submitted to
the Integrated Finance Division, MoH and FW for seeking a suitable date from Department of
Expenditure, Ministry of Finance for consideration of the proposal.
Simultaneously, preparatory activities for implementation of new initiatives like requirement
of manpower, construction of dedicated Eye Wings/Eye OT in District Hospitals and development
of Mobile Ophthalmic Units have been started on provisional basis in consultation with the
concerned States.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
Last year, while welcoming new initatives under the National Programme for Control
of Blindness for the Eleventh Plan with focused attention on NE States and also in Bihar,
Jharkhand, J&K, Himachal Pradesh and Uttarakhand, the Committee had voiced its
apprehensions about undue delay in their actual initiation. More than one year has passed
since then and third year of the Eleventh Plan period is also reaching its end. However, the
EFC Memo for the proposed projects remains under active consideration in consultation with
the Integrated Finance Division. The Committee has been drawing the attention of the

102
Department to the trend of undue delay in getting the formal approval for the proposed
projects from all the authorities. However, no serious introspection on such a vital aspect
seems to have been done by the Department. As a result, funds continue to get allocated
for proposed projects year after year with their status remaining unchanged and funds
remaining unutilized. Preparatory activities for implementation of new proposals on
provisional basis may also prove to be counter-productive at the end simply because there
is always a possibility of the proposed projects undergoing drastic changes. The Committee,
therefore, once again emphasizes that the Department has to evolve a mechanism
whereunder all procedural formalities for a proposed project are expeditiously completed and
the project takes off in the real sense.
Recommendations/Observations
7.7 The Department has informed that in order to encourage eye donation among masses, IEC
activities are being intensified under the programme. The major focus towards this direction is
organization of National Eye Donation Fortnight from 25th August to 7th September every year.
During the fortnight, intensified publicity through all available media is given to step up eye
donation campaign and persuade people to donate eyes after death. The focus of the fortnight has
been shifted from “Pledging of Eyes” to “Actual Collection of Eyes” by launching “Hospital
Retrievable Programme” in major hospitals with various specialties, teaching hospitals attached to
medical colleges and large multi-specialty hospitals in Government and Non-Government Sector,
Army, Railways, ESI and Industrial Establishments of the country. In addition to this, the message
for eye donation is being propagated through various print and electronic media.
The Committee is not convinced that the IEC activities are being carried out as intensely
as is stated by the Department. The Committee has been given to understand that out of the total
allocated budget under National Programme for Control of Blindness, around 7% has been spent
on IEC, which according to the Committee, is quite a sizable amount. The Committee strongly feels
that public awareness about the noble cause of eye donation for blind persons continues to be very
low. The Committee is of the view that a review of IEC activities on this aspect needs to be made
in consultation with all the stakeholders, specially reputed Eye Hospitals/NGOs doing commendable
work in this area and also both government and private advertising agencies. The Committee would
also like to emphasize that the functioning of Eye Banks in the country requires to be reviewed
in view of reported lack of logistic support for timely collection of eyes by the Eye Banks. Steps
also need to be taken for increasing the number of Eye Banks in the country.
Action Taken
An independent evaluation of IEC activities under NPCB has already been initiated with the
following objective:—-
1. To assess the level of awareness created by the Govt. of India IEC materials/activities
among target population.
2. To assess the media habits and effectiveness of media tools used among the different
target groups.
3. To recommend cost effective IEC strategies for future, based on this assessment
including media tools – both conventional and non-conventional.
As regards observation of the Committee on review of functioning of Eye Banks, it is stated
that review of Eye Banks is a part of overall Comprehensive Evaluation NPCB, which is under
process.

103
Further, in order to increase number of Eye Banks in the country to encourage eye donation,
it has been proposed to strengthen 50 Eye Banks during Eleventh Five Year Plan with enhanced
assistance from Rs.l0 lakh to Rs.15 lakh as non-recurring grant to Eye Banks in Government/
voluntary organisations. In addition, recurring assistance of Rs.l000 has been proposed to be
enhanced to Rs.1500 per pair of eyes to Eye Banks towards honorarium to Eye Bank staff,
consumables including preservation material and media, transportation/POL and contingencies. It has
also been proposed to appoint 150 Eye Donation Counselors in Eye Banks in Govt. and NGO Sector.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee appreciates the efforts of the Department for monitoring and
evaluation of IEC activities, the functioning of the Eye Banks in the country and other
initiatives to boost eye donations. However, the Department has not mentioned clearly the
time lines of initiation or the conclusion of the evaluation studies for IEC activities and also
the comprehensive evaluation of NPCB on review of Eye Banks. The Committee would like
to be informed about the outcomes of the evaluation studies at the earliest and furnish the
relevant reports for its perusal.
VIII. NA TIONAL IODINE DEFICIENCY DISORDERS PROGRAMME
Recommendations/Observations
8.2 The Committee observes that out of a Plan allocation of Rs.15 crores in 2006-07,
expenditure reported is only Rs.11.79 crores while against an allocation of Rs.25 crores during
2007-08, expenditure reported upto 28.02.2007 is Rs.13.13 crores only. During 2008-09, the
allocation has been further enhanced to Rs.32 crores. The target achievement figures for 2007-08
are given below:—
Processes Tar gets for 2007-08 Achievement
upto 28.2.2008

The Committee notes with dismay that not even one of the targets set under the Programme
could be achieved during the past year, by the time line given. Moreover, unti1l 28.2.2008, only
Rs.13.13 crores of the funds released, could be spent. The Committee considers such a trend
highly unsatisfactory. The Committee remarking on the under-utilisation of funds during 2006-07,
had expressed its apprehension on proper utilisation of funds allocated under the Programme for

104
2007-08 in its 22nd Report. The Committee was given to understand that a provision of Rs.4.30
crores has been made for States and UTs to meet the expenditure on the following components
for the same year.
1. Establishment of an IDD Control Cell
2. Establishment of an IDD Monitoring Lab
3. DD Surveys/resurveys of districts
4. Health Education and Publicity
To the utter dismay of the Committee, the States could not set up even one IDD Control
Cell or IDD Monitoring Lab. Not only that, of the 20,000 salt samples the Department had planned
to analyse from 40 districts at the rate of 500 samples per district, not even one sample had been
analyzed. The Committee takes a serious view of this.
The Committee’s attention has also been drawn by enhanced allocation of Rs.32 crores in
2008-09. The Committee would like to emphasize once again that all conceivable efforts may be
made so as to ensure optimum and judicious utilization of allocated funds.
Action Taken
For the year 2006-07, expenditure under National Iodine Deficiency Disorders Control
Programme (NIDDCP) was Rs.13.86 crores from the allocated amount of Rs.15 crores. This is
an expenditure of 92.4%. The shortfall was due to non-filling up of posts of IDD cell and IDD
Monitoring laboratories by the States/UTs.
For the year 2007-08, the expenditure was Rs.20.04 crores against the allocation of Rs.25
crores, which is about 80.2%. The shortfall is due to Non-filling up of vacant posts under IDD
cell and IDD lab at the State/UT level as well as a delay in procurement of Salt Testing Kits. The
IEC component, a major strategy of NIDDCP, has full utilization of funds.
In terms of physical achievements, the production & distribution of Iodated salt was 41.76
MT upto January, 2008 and most likely the target of 50 lakh MT would be achieved. There are
already 31 State IDD Control cells present. From the 5 States that have been mentioned namely
Jharkhand, Bihar, West Bengal, Puducherry and M.P, the IDD cells are present in Bihar, West
Bengal and M.P. For Establishment of State IDD Monitoring laboratories, a laboratory has been
established in Uttar Pradesh.
The target of 20,000 salt samples @ 500 samples per district from 40 districts could not
be undertaken due to non-availability of programme vehicle by administration.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee finds the Status Report on the achievement level of physical targets
set under the National Iodine Deficiency Disorders Control Programme for 2007-08 very
discouraging. Out of 6 States, i.e. Jharkhand, Bihar, West Bengal, Puducherry, Madhya
Pradesh and U.P. targeted for setting up State IDD Control Cells, Jharkhand, UP and
Puducherry have failed to do so. Similarly, only U.P. could set up IDD Monitoring lab although
all the aforesaid 6 States were targeted to do so. What is more disturbing is that the target
of analysis of 20,000 salt samples @ 500 samples per district from 40 districts could not be
undertaken due to non-availability of programme vechicle by administration. The Committee
refuses to accept such flimsy justifications. The Committee would like to point out that proper
planning means achievement of targeted goals within the prescribed time-schedule. Problems
like non-availability of programme vehicle could have been easily handled. The Committee,

105
therefore, would like to impress upon the Department once again to make all conceivable
efforts in active co-ordination with State authorities for timely achievement of goals.
IX. JANANI SURAKSHA YOJANA
Recommendations/Observations
9.13 The Committee feels that monitoring mechanism for such Schemes needs to be very
effective so that no grey areas are there to be misused by unscrupulous persons. The Committee
would like to have a Status Note on the procedure of verification of claims and release of
compensation package. The Committee also strongly feels that community participation is
encouraged so as to keep a watchful eye on the activities of implementing agencies under the
Scheme. The Committee would also appreciate if an assessment is made about the impact of JSY
on the lowering of Maternal Mortality Rate in the crucial States.
Action Taken
The Ministry fully shares the views of the Committee to have a fool-proof Monitoring
mechanism to avoid misuse of funds. A Status Note on the lines desired by the Committee will be
prepared as early as possible after obtaining specific inputs from the States.
The Department, in collaboration with UNICEF, UNFPA and GTZ, has commissioned
surveys regarding the implementation of the scheme in eight States. The reports, however, do not
give any specific information regarding impact of the scheme on the lowering of Maternal Mortality
Rate. The Department is in the process of finalizing modalities for concurrent evaluation of the
scheme in six major States. During the course of survey, an assessment about the impact of the
scheme on the reduction of Maternal Mortality will be made.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee notes with satisfaction that the Department also shares a similar
view point with the Committee regarding putting a fool-proof monitoring mechanism in
place so that misuse of funds intended for the beneficiaries under the programme are
avoided. The Committee observes that nearly a year has passed since the Department has
stated in its ATN regarding preparing a S tatus Note on the pr ocedure of verification of
claims and release of compensation package under Janani Suraksha Yojana as desired by
the Committee. The Committee hopes that the Department must have obtained specific
inputs from the States for the Status Note. The Committee would like the Department to
furnish the said Status Note for its perusal and also to be apprised about the follow-up
action, if any, taken so far.
The Committee would also like to point out that the Department must have finalized
the modalities for concurrent evaluation of the scheme in six major States regarding the
impact of the scheme on the lowering of Maternal Mortality Rate. However, the Department
has not given any timelines for commencing or concluding the assessment. It, therefore,
urges upon the Department to draw the plan of action for making the assessment within
a specified timeline and apprise the Committee about its findings.
ROUTINE IMMUNISATION PROGRAMME
Recommendations/Observations
9.15 The Committee has been observing that State specific problems especially in the low
performing States continue to persist. Shortage of Health Workers, lack of awareness and laxity
in monitoring and supervision of the programme are some of the problems being noticed in such

106
States. Reported coverage of different vaccines under the Routine Immunization Programme
confirms Committee’s apprehensions that in some of the States like Arunachal Pradesh.. Bihar.
Jharkhand, Manipur, position is very unsatisfactory. The Committee would like to point out that the
steps taken for strengthening the Routine Immunization Programme like recruitment of health
workers, regular reviews at all levels and catch up rounds in poor performing States do not seem
to have yielded the desired results. The Committee can only say that catch-up rounds in poor
performing States with the districts identified beforehand is the most important step for effective
reduction in the number of un-immunized and partially immunized children. Entire focus of the
Implementing agencies should be on this alone.
Action Taken
The catch-up rounds/special immunization weeks have been observed in the low performing
States viz. EAG and NE States. As per the observations of the Committee, the poor performing
districts will be identified from the District Level Household Survey III (DLHS-III), which is under
process and the report is awaited soon. The States will be asked to focus on these districts and
conduct catch-up rounds in these select districts.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
The Committee hopes that by this time the District Level Household Survey III (DLHS-
III) must have been completed and the Report received. The Committee would like the
Department to furnish a copy of the Survey Report for its perusal as also the progress made
on the proposed course of action in the EAG and NE States indicated in the Action Taken Note.
Recommendations/Observations
9.17 Committee understands that the three pioneer Institutes namely Central Research Institute
(CRI), Kasauli in Himachal Pradesh, Pasteur Institute of India (PII), Koonoor, Tamil Nadu and the
BCG Vaccine Laboratory (BCGVL) in Chennai, Tamil Nadu have been directed to stop vaccine
production. The Committee strongly feels that these vaccine production units should not be
converted to only testing laboratories. The Committee, accordingly, recommends that the Ministry
of Health and Family Welfare should withdraw the order without delay.
Action Taken
The three Institutes were inspected and due to non-compliance of Schedule M requirements,
the Drugs Controller General of India suspended their licenses w.e.f. 15th January, 2008. The
Ministry constituted a Committee under the Chairmanship of Drugs Controller General of India to
(i) assess feasibility of reviving these units by making them Schedule M compliant: (ii) if found
feasible and viable, what would be the time frame and the costs involved in the revival; and
(iii) if the revival is not found feasible/viable, alternate use of the existing facilities.
COMMENTS OF THE COMMITTEE
In the light of shortage of vaccines in the country, the Committee observes that the
reply of the Ministry that the units are not schedule M compliant is unacceptable. It also
notes that since there have been no reckonable reports of adverse impact of the vaccines
produced by the units over the years, though they have not been schedule M compliant
during the said period, the Committee feels that suspension of vaccine producing license
and putting the existing facilities to alternative uses would not be a viable move. Therefore,
the Committee reiterates its earlier stand and recommends that the suspension of the
license of these units be revoked and the units be allowed to produce vaccines.
