OBSERV ATIONS/RECOMMENDATIONS — AT A GLANCE
Part A
BUDGETARY ALLOCATION
The Committee was given to understand that the overall approved outlays under two
heads – strengthening of hospitals and dispensaries and redevelopment of hospitals/
institutions have been drastically reduced vis-a-vis the proposed outlay. Hospitals like RML
and Safdarjung and other institutions like AIIMS, JIPMER etc. have been allocated lesser
amounts than what had been proposed. The Committee is constrained to observe that time
and again it has been pointing out to the Department to place its case before the Planning
Commission for funds allocation in an effective way, yet it appears that once again the
Department has not been able to present its case convincingly. The Committee is of the
view that lesser approved outlay often stems from past trends of sub-optimum utilization of
funds and the issues incidental thereto. The Committee would, therefore, recommend to the
Department to have a right approach in this regard by making a correct assessment of
requirements of funds for the different activities and place the same effectively before the
Planning Commission. The Committee would also like to impress upon the Department to
take effective measures so as to ensure optimum and judicious utilization of allocated
funds. (Para 2.3)
The Committee observes that action regarding audit of all centrally sponsored
programmes, PSUs under the Department and autonomous bodies receiving Grants-in-Aid
has been initiated by the Department. The Committee would like to be apprised about the
latest position in this regard. (Para 2.11)
The Committee notes that out of the five units where adverse audit findings were
made during 2007-08, compliance in case of only one has been received. The remaining four
units have some serious audit paras against them. The Committee would appreciate if the
matter is taken up with these units urgently so that the irregularities reported are
reconciled and a sense of financial accountability prevails. Status Note in this regard may
be furnished to the Committee. (Para 2.12)
CENTRAL GOVERNMENT HEALTH SCHEME (CGHS)
The Committee had called for taking measures which includes training of the staff,
connectivity of the computer systems and their backup for power failure. However, the
Department has concentrated on the aspect of internet related issues, viz., enhancing its
speed and software related problems. The issue of training of staff and the power backup
seems to have been ignored at this juncture. The Committee would like to emphasize that
though internet efficiency is a vital aspect of the computerization programme, yet it has to
be seen in totality. The issue of proper training of the staff and the power backup are also
crucial for the success of the computerization exercise. The Committee, therefore, expects
the Department to chalk out a proper strategy in this regard. (Para 3.5)
The Committee observes that its recommendation regarding starting of two shifts
system in CGHS dispensaries has ultimately resulted in a positive response from the
108

109
Department. The Committee would appreciate if the matter is placed before the Committee
of Secretaries at the earliest. (Para 3.6)
The Committee was given to understand that land for CGHS dispensary was available
in Dwarka. The Department has in its recent update had admitted that it had been allotted
three plots of land in Dwarka at Sectors 6, 8 and 12. The problem cited here is that the
construction work of a dispensary could not be started for want of a formal possession of
the plots being given by the DDA to the Department. The Committee has been apprised that
reminders have been sent to the DDA and their reply is still awaited. The Committee is
unhappy to note that in the name of expediting formalities only reminders have been sent.
The Committee fails to understand the reasons for undue delay on the part of DDA in
providing possession of the plots in Dwarka when the same had already been allotted. It is
high time that the Department takes up the matter at the highest level in DDA in the
interest of a large number of CGHS beneficiaries residing in Dwarka. The Committee would
also like to point out that even after possession of land, it will take considerable time in
the opening of CGHS dispensaries in Dwarka. Concerted efforts are, therefore, required to
be made for getting suitable accommodation till then so as to get at least one Dispensary
operational there. (Para 3.7)
The Committee is constrained to note that the Department has adopted a lacklustre
approach in the matter. It has been close to three years since the in-principle approval for
opening the new CGHS dispensaries in Sector 82 Noida and Gurgaon was given by the
Planning Commission and in the name of action, only a request to Chairman (CEO) Greater
NOIDA for allotment of suitable accommodation has been made, which however has not
materialized due to high rent cost. Similarly, in case of Gurgaon Dispensary, after a gap of
one year since in-principle approval, an advertisement was issued in March 2007, which
however didn’t attract any response. Further, it took one more year to write to the
Secretary, Government of Haryana for allotment of CGHS Dispensary. No efforts seem to
have been taken since then. The Committee is unhappy with this kind of lackadaisical
approach of the Department. If this is the state of affairs at present, the Committee can
well imagine that it may take even a decade to start the two dispensaries. The Committee
would, therefore, recommend to the Department to make every conceivable effort so that
the two dispensaries are made functional at the earliest and the large number of
beneficiaries residing in these areas get proper CGHS facilities. (Para 3.8)
The Committee notes that SIU study report has been implemented in 19 CGHS
covered cities except Delhi which is under process in consultation with the Finance Division
in the Ministry. The Committee has also been informed that issue of creation of posts in
all the 20 CGHS covered cities is being taken up by the Department. The Committee
expresses its deep concern on undue delay taking place in such a vital area. No progress
seems to have been made since the presentation of 27th Report of the Committee on the
Demands for Grants (2008-09) of the Department. The Committee is also not aware about
the time frame for implementation of SIU Study Report in Delhi. The Committee reiterates
that the entire process of creation of posts in all the 20 CGHS covered cities should be
completed in a time-bound manner with monitoring at all levels. (Para 3.9)
The Committee hopes that the proposal for direct procurement of proprietary/
branded medicines as per the rates approved by MSO through rate contract system has been
approved by the competent authority leading to savings on heavy consultancy fees being
paid to HSCC. (Para 3.12)
The Committee expresses its strong reservation on the continuance of adhoc
arrangements with regard to empanelment of cancer hospitals for CGHS beneficiaries. The

110
Committee is not aware about the progress made by the CGHS Committee with regard to
fixation of various packages/rates for treatment of cancer patients. The Committee can well
imagine the plight of cancer patients till a final decision is arrived at, which would
ultimately depend on the willingness of hospitals for accepting the rates offered leading to
their empanelment. It seems that CGHS beneficiaries, specially the cancer patients, would
continue to suffer. There is an urgent need for review of empanelment procedure so that
a situation does not arise where patients needing extensive medical care continue to be
deprived of the same. (Para 3.13)
The Committee has been provided with the revised rates for various investigation
and treatment procedures under CGHS in the four metro cities. The Committee is surprised
to note substantial variation in the approved rates for many investigation/treatment
procedures. In many cases there has been a variation of 50%. Further, there are several
cases where the rates vary upto two to three times in the metro cities which the Committee
is not able to reconcile itself with. If such is the level of variance in rates in different
metro cities, the Committee can well imagine about the difference in CGHS rates prevalent
in other cities. The Committee places on record its deep concern in this regard. Either there
is some inherent problem in the procedure or there may be some mismanagement. The
Committee would like to be apprised in detail about the procedure along with criteria for
approval of CGHS rates for the various cities. (Para 3.14)
SAFDARJUNG HOSPITAL AND V ARDHMAN MAHAVEER MEDICAL COLLEGE, NEW DELHI
The Committee in its Report had recommended to the Department to provide
adequate funds to the Safdarjung Hospital to meet its functional requirements. The
Committee is anguished to note that in the name of the Action Taken in this regard, the
Department appears to have just forwarded the status given by the Hospital administration.
The Department has not come up with any assurance or plan of action in this regard. The
Committee notes that inadequate provision is likely to result in non procurement of
Medicines and Disposables thereby adversely affecting the patient care and the various
Major Works/Maintenance undertaken by this Hospital & CPWD and the construction of
staff quarters would also be put on hold. The Committee therefore recommends that urgent
steps be taken to provide additional funds to the Hospital, which caters to the needs of the
poorer sections of the people, not only from Delhi but also from the adjoining and far off
areas. (Para 4.3)
ALL INDIA INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL SCIENCES (AIIMS)
Nobody can deny the fact that our country is facing shortage of medical
professionals. Not only this, precious public money is being used to pay for the subsidized
education in the Government institutions, some of which are renowned ones. The
Committee, therefore, strongly feels that some policy guidelines are urgently required to be
framed so that trend of our doctors and nurses leaving the country is checked. The
Department can not shy away from its responsibility to frame such a policy which is
required in public interest. (Para 6.11)
The Committee notes that it has been about two years since the Valiathan
Committee submitted its report on the functioning of AIIMS. In a meeting held on the 14th
November, 2008, the representatives of the Department inter alia  stated that action on some
of its recommendations had taken place. It was also stated that the recommendations
pertain to some short-term and some long-term goals. Action in case of short-term goals
would take place within 5-6 months and in other cases it would take a longer period. While

111
recommending speedy action on the recommendations of the Valiathan Committee, the
Committee would like to have a status note in the matter especially about the
recommendations which have been implemented and the time-frame within which the
remaining would be implemented. (Para 6.12)
The Committee has been pursuing the matter of setting up of a Burns Unit in
AIIMS. The Committee notes that the proposal for setting up a Burns Unit in the JPNA
Trauma Centre, AIIMS has been approved by the Standing Finance Committee of the
Institute on the 7th May, 2008. The Committee finds that the minutes of the Standing
Finance Committee have now to be approved by the Institute Body/Governing Body. The
Committee once again emphasizes that this matter needs to be pursued vigorously by both
the Department and the Institute authorities so that the proposed Burns Unit starts
functioning at the earliest. Present pace of progress has to be expedited in a time-bound
manner otherwise the proposal will continue to remain on paper for years. (Para 6.13)
POST GRADUATE INSTITUTE OF MEDICAL EDUCATION AND RESEARCH (PGIMER),
CHANDIGARH
The Committee had sought information on the initiatives taken by the Institute for
the timely completion of the new projects, especially in the light of under-utilization of
allocated funds in the past and the subsequent delay in the ongoing projects. The Action
Taken Note submitted by the Department in this regard is a mere statement of the present
status of the ongoing projects. The Committee is constrained to observe that there is no
mention of the initiatives, if any, being taken by the Department for timely completion of
the ongoing and new projects. In the absence of any concrete steps, the Committee
apprehends no improvement in the scheme of things. The Committee would again
emphasize on the fact that the Department needs to devise a proper strategy urgently to
back the targets set for the Institute. (Para 7.5)
CENTRAL INSTITUTE OF PSYCHIATRY, RANCHI
The Committee is anguished to note that the Department has not supplied the
requisite information sought in respect of the Central Institute of Psychiatry, Ranchi. The
Department has not furnished any update regarding the progress made w.r.t. the proposed
re-development plan of the Institute. It has only stated the previous position that the EFC
proposal has been circulated and is under consideration. From this, the Committee can only
infer that there has not been any progress on this front since quite some time and no
sincere efforts seem to have been taken by the Department to expedite the same. The
Committee is also not happy with the Department for not sharing the circumstances leading
to the monitoring of the development of the Institute by the Jharkhand High Court despite
being asked to do so specifically. The Committee would like to be furnished the requisite
information without further delay. (Para 9.3)
V ALLABHBHAI PATEL CHEST INSTITUTE, DELHI
The Committee is anguished to note that despite its observation regarding the details
of the short-term training courses/workshops in respiratory diseases for faculty members
and medical practitioners conducted by the Institute during 2007-08 not being specific, the
Department has not provided the same again. In Annexure-IX th e contents of Annexure-
X, which is about the details w.r.t.  workshops, courses etc. in 2008-09, have been
reproduced. The Committee views this with serious concern. Such an approach is neither
acceptable nor expected from the Department. The Committee, therefore, directs the

112
Department to be careful in future and provide the requisite information at the
earliest. (Para 10.3)
PRADHAN MANTRI SWASTHYA SURAKSHA YOJANA (PMSSY)
The Committee, during the presentation by the Secretary before it, in connection
with the Demands-for-Grants of the Ministry, had conveyed to him its deep concern over the
delay in the PMSSY Project. Subsequently, the Committee had directed the Department to
furnish a status report regarding the implementation of the Project after every two months,
which was agreed to by the Secretary. In spite of a categorical assurance by the Department
that such a report would invariably be furnished, till date no such report has been furnished
to the Committee. Non-compliance of an assurance given to a Parliamentary Committee is
a serious matter. The Committee cautions the Department in this regard and expects that
it would be kept informed of the progress made in the implementation of the project, as
desired. (Para 11.8)
UPGRADATION OF DIST RICT HOSPITALS
The Committee is constrained to observe that the Action Taken Note furnished by
the Department is silent on the basic issues raised by the Committee. Though merging the
activity with Mission Flexible Pool (NRHM), as an administrative decision, may be aimed at
tackling the problems afflicting the scheme, the Department has not categorically stated
about any action w.r.t.  Committee’s recommendations regarding giving priority to the
remote and rural area hospitals and those States where MMR and IMR are high and where
maternity facilities are not up to the mark formulating a mechanism so as to facilitate
early selection, granting and overseeing proper utilization of funds and expediting scrutiny
of the proposals received from the State Governments from the beginning of financial year
2008-09 itself. The Committee would appreciate if the Department takes some pro-active
steps keeping in view its specific recommendations on a very crucial issue of public health.
(Para 13.2)
RASHTRIYA AROGYA NIDHI
The Committee notes that the Department has approached the State Governments of
U.P., Assam, Orissa, Manipur, Arunachal Pradesh, Meghalaya and Nagaland for setting up
of State Illness Assistance Fund. The Committee strongly feels that there is an urgent need
for having such a fund in these States. The Committee once again urges upon the
Department to take up this vital matter of public interest with the concerned State
Governments at the highest level. (Para 14.3)
The Committee welcomes the Department’s move regarding increase in financial
assistance to less than or equal to Rs.l lakh per case and extension of the facility to the
NEIGRIHMS, Shillong and Regional Institute of Medical Sciences Hospital (RIMS), Imphal.
The Committee would appreciate if steps are taken for a realistic increase in the revolving
fund to cater to the demands of poor patients and some concrete action is taken for
extension of the scheme to other hospitals. The Committee would also like to point out that
the Department’s reply is silent on the issue of giving wide publicity to the scheme
especially in the hospital campuses to generate awareness so that the same can be utilized
by the needy and poor patients. The Committee would like to be apprised of the steps taken
by the Department in this regard. The Committee would also appreciate if an assessment
of the Rashtriya Arogya Nidhi is made at the earliest. The Committee strongly feels that
such an exercise would result in identification of problem areas and necessary remedial
steps. (Para 14.4)

113
NATIONAL AIDS CONTROL PROGRAMME (NACP)
The Committee is given to understand that steps are being taken to ensure full
utilization of funds during 2008-09. Y et no specific steps have been stated. In the opinion
of the Committee merely stating that steps are being taken to ensure full utilization
without specifying the steps being taken/to be taken is not adequate. The Committee,
therefore, would like to be apprised of the concrete steps being taken/contemplated to be
taken to obviate the possibility of under-utilization of funds sanctioned by Parliament.
(Para 16.5)
The Committee places on record its anguish that the Department has not shared with
it the requisite information categorically asked for in respect of unspent balances available
with the blacklisted NGOs and the action taken for recovery of the same, details of all
similar cases/discrepancies related to NACO and the action taken by the Department/CBI
in that regard. This coupled with contradictory figures regarding the NGOs under paras
16.13 and 16.12 clearly shows the perfunctory and careless approach of the Department
towards the Committee. The Committee takes this seriously and advises the Department to
be careful in future. Accordingly, the Committee would like to be provided with the
requisite information without further delay. (Para 16.13)
NATIONAL CANCER CONTROL PROGRAMME (NCCP)
The Committee is once again unhappy to note that the requisite information
regarding the details of the new component – Public Private Partnership has not been
furnished. The Committee, accordingly, would like to be provided with complete details
regarding this new component. (Para 17.6)
The Committee appreciates the steps taken by the Department regarding submission
of UCs by the State Governments. However, the Committee is constrained to note that a
Status Note on pending UCs (State/NGO-wise), as asked for by the Committee has not been
furnished by the Department. The Committee would appreciate if the same is furnished to
it. (Para 17.8)
MEDICAL EDUCATION
The Committee observes that the issue at hand is too urgent to be allowed to linger
on, as it concerns the quality and standards of medical education and any compromise in
the matter has the potential to put the lives of the people in peril. Therefore, mere
assurance of keeping in view the observations of the Committee at the time of finalizing
any mechanism does not suffice. In view of the gravity of the issue, the Committee would
recommend to the Department to deal with it with a sense of promptitude and bring some
sort of policy or legislation urgently. (Para 21.6)
Last year, Committee’s attention had been drawn to a very disturbing situation
whereunder as many as 27 medical colleges continued to function in the country whose first
batch of MBBS students had appeared for final examination but they were yet to be
recognized. The Committee had impressed upon the Department to resolve this matter
involving the fate of our students without any further delay. The Committee is deeply
disturbed to note that the position remains unchanged. No action seems to have been taken
in the case of 9 medical colleges where recommendations of MCI have reached the
Department. With respect to remaining 18 medical colleges, recommendations of MCI are
still awaited. The Committee is well aware of the statutory provisions regarding grant of
recognition to a medical college. The Committee views with serious concern this state of

114
affairs, that too in the critical area of medical education. The Committee is of the firm
opinion that this entire matter along with the existing procedure of recognition of medical
colleges needs to be reviewed by all concerned. The Department has to take a pro-active
role being the nodal authority for medical education in the country. (Para 21.7)
V ACANCIES IN CBS CADRE
The Committee takes note of the fact that in the General Duty Medical Officers
sub-cadre of CHS recently 179 posts have been abolished in various CGHS units. The
Committee welcomes the Department’s initiative to get a clearance from the Cabinet for
appointment of retired GDMOs on contractual basis for a period of two years on a
remuneration of Rs. 25,000/-. But the Committee cautions the Department that if agreed
to by the Cabinet this should only be a stop gap arrangement and there should be no laxity
on its part to make all out efforts for having the vacancies filled up on a full time basis,
at the earliest. The Committee is dismayed to note the contradictory figures of vacancies
in the Non-Teaching, Teaching and Public Health sub-cadre supplied to it at the time of
Demands for Grants and in the present Action Taken Note of the Department. The
Committee cautions the Department to be careful in future in presenting facts and figures
to it. (Para 22.2)

115
Part-B
BUDGETARY ALLOCATION
The Committee has been drawing the attention of the Department to the uneven
pace of utilisation of funds allocated under NRHM. Year 2007-08 was also no exception. As
on the 29th February, 2008, utilisation figures stood at Rs. 8800.88 crore which reached to
Rs. 10,310.16 crore by the 31st March, 2008, clearly indicating release of Rs. 1509.28 crore
during the last month of the financial year. The Department has not come up with any
clarification on this pertinent issue. The Committee is also not happy with the attempt
made by the Department in projecting a satisfactory utilization status of allocated funds by
drawing a comparison between RE allocation and utilization thereof. The fact remains that
inspite of increased allocation at the beginning of the year, the same had to be brought
down at the Revised Estimate Stage. Crucial programmes like Routine Immunisation, Pulse
Polio Immunisation, Intergrated Disease Survelliance Project showing this trend is a cause
of serious concern for the Committee.
The Committee is not prepared to accept the argument put forth by the Department
that balance remaining at the end of the year is mainly due to curtailed releases owing to
low absorptive capacity in the NE region. The Committee is constrained to observe that the
Department, instead of devising means to break through the obstacles to ensure proper
utilization of funds in the region, seems to pose a picture of resignation in the matter. The
Committee is of the firm belief that under the above circumstances, major healthcare
interventions in the NE region would not be reaching the targeted beneficiaries, thereby
depriving them of the healthcare initiatives. The Committee strongly feels that the
Department, being the apex nodal body in the country for healthcare initiatives, has to
strive harder so as to ensure their reach to the NE region in the real sense. The
Committee, therefore, recommends to the Department to take up this issue with the Chief
Secretaries of all the NE States as well as the Department of Development of NE Region.
(Para 2.5)
The Committee is disappointed to note that the Department has chosen to remain
silent on the reasons of steadily decreasing allocation of funds to such an important
healthcare programme like the Routine Immunisation Programme. The Committee is also
not convinced by the justification given by the Department with regard to Integrated
Diseases Surveillance Project. The Department has also, not indicated in its reply as to what
extent the number of labs was reduced resulting in less expenditure on the procurement of
equipments. Similarly, it is also not clear as to what is the exact number of units that were
planned to be linked by the NIC network. The Department has informed the Committee
that funds for networking could not be released to NIC since UCs and SOEs could not be
timely obtained from it. The Committee is aware that National Informatics Centre (NIC) is
a government set up. Therefore, it wonders as to what could be the reasons for which there
were delays in submission of UCs and SOEs by the latter. The Committee, under the
circumstances, can only conclude that the Department’s attempts in the matter have been
lackadaisical, and as a result of which, achieving targeted landmarks in fully
operationalising critical initiatives like that of the IDSP have been put on the backfoot. The
Committee observes that the Department should actively pursue the project of making IDSP
fully operational and should be extra vigilant towards achieving the targets set for such
critical health initiatives. (Para 2.6)
115

116
INFRASTRUCTURE STRENGTHENING
The Committee is happy to note that Punjab has been able to furnish all 20
Integrated District Health Action Plans (IDHAPs). However, its concern stands unabated for
the progress reported from the State of Himachal Pradesh and Bihar. It fails to understand
the reasons as to why the matter of formulation and submission of IDHAPs would go into
litigation. Under such a dismal scenario, the situation in a State like Bihar, where the need
for basic health facilities is urgent, could only worsen if timely action on the part of both
Central and State Government is not taken. The Committee, therefore, urges upon the
Department to take up this matter at the highest level in the State. The Committee also
recommends that the slow progress made by Haryana in respect of IDHAP may also be
monitored by the Department. (Para 3.6)
The Committee is disappointed with the Department’s reply to the Committee’s
observation regarding pending civil construction work for 66,382 Sub-Centres (SCs). The
Committee had also impressed upon the Department to undertake regular monitoring of
such SCs. However, the Department, instead of informing the Committee of the progress
made so far, and the monitoring mechanisms, if any, put in place, has chosen to take note
of the issue as a “matter of record”. Taking a serious note of the approach of the
Department, the Committee would appreciate if the Department furnishes the details of the
progress made in the civil construction work of the Sub-Centres in all the States and also
indicate the monitoring mechanism in place for the purpose. The data so furnished should
also contain details of the funds released to the States, the date of commencement of work,
a list of the number of units that were proposed to be built and those that have completed
construction, till date. (Para 3.7)
ASHA
The Committee notes with a sense of relief that the Department has at last come
to concur with the observations of the Committee regarding its apprehensions on the role
clarity and feasibility of discharging the duties assigned to ASHA. The Committee would like
to state here that these apprehensions were voiced by it in earlier reports so that the
objective with which the concept of ASHA was initiated under the NRHM doesn’t get diluted.
The Department has stated in its Action Taken Note that National Health System Resource
Centre (NHSRC) is reviewing the ‘job r esponsibilities’ and “Role clarity of ASHA” as well
as that of AWW and ANM. The Committee observes that the Department has not specified
any timeframe, within which NHSRC would be completing its review. In the meantime,
these Social Health Activists would continue discharging their duties originally mandated
with the likelihood of new ones adding to their responsibilities. The NHSRC has to complete
its review in a time bound manner so that such an important health initiative does not
become redundant. The Committee, therefore, urges upon the Department to ensure the
timely submission of the Report by NHSRC, followed by expeditious implementation of the
same. (Para 3.14)
MOBILE MEDICAL UNITS (MMUs)
The Committee observes with constraint that no significant progress has been made
in the operationalisation of Mobile Medical Units (MMUs). As per the latest status furnished
by the Department, only 200 districts in the country are having this facility. Committee’s
apprehensions about States like Bihar, Maharashtra and U.P. failing to procure MMUs
inspite of provision of funds therefor continue to persist as confirmed in the response of
the Department. Notwithstanding availability of emergency ambulance services on a very

117
large scale in some States, the Committee would like to emphasize that effective steps need
to be taken for strengthening the facility of MMUs in the entire country. This task can be
accomplished if follow-up action on the constraints highlighted in this regard in the 1st
Common Review Mission (November, 2007) and second Common Review Mission (November,
2008) is taken by the Department in co-ordination with concerned States. (Para 3.16.1)
The Committee hopes that the Second Common Review Mission of NRHM of 13
States scheduled for November, 2008 must have been reviewed and corrective steps initiated
by the concerned States. The Committee is, however, not aware as to whether its suggestion
for including both performing and non-performing districts in the States selected for CRM
has been implemented. If not, the Committee would emphasize that in the absence of such
a framework, the very purpose of the Review would remain incomplete. (Para 3.16.2)
NATIONAL VECTOR BORNE DISEASE CONTROL  PROGRAMME
Apprehensions of the Committee with regard to shortage of surveillance and
supervisory staff in Malaria-endemic N-E States continue to persist. Out of 5057 contractual
MPWs (male) sanctioned in all the 14 high malaria-endemic States under  NVBDCP during
2007-08, with the exception of Nagaland, remaining N-E States like Tripura, Manipur,
Arunachal Pradesh and Assam have only initiated the process of recruitment. No mention
being made about other health functionaries like Health Supervisor (Male) and (Female) by
the Department is self- revealing. The Committee has been persistently drawing the
attention of the Department towards the problem of sanctioned posts of Health workers
remaining vacant. But it seems no viable solution has emerged so far . Involving ASHAs in
malaria-control activities can only be considered a stop-gap arrangement, that too not viable
due to their being already over-burdened with manifold tasks. The Committee can only
reiterate that the matter of filling up of sanctioned posts of Health workers in malaria-
endemic N-E States needs to the taken up at the highest level by the Department.
(Para 4.7)
JAPANESE ENCEPHALITIS
The Committee observes that a number of measures have been initiated by the
Department in co-ordination with the State authorities with regard to control of Japanese
Encephalitis (JE) in eastern UP, the most endemic area. The Committee also takes note of
two problem areas - one of constraints in lab-testing and prevalence of other viruses, apart
from JE with vaccination being available only against JE. The Committee fails to understand
why, inspite of adequate funds being provided under the Programme, proper sample testing
facilities have been lacking. The Committee would like to state here that lack of proper
serological testing facilities would result in improper detection of infected cases and could
aggravate the situation further. The Committee suggests that mobile labs for conducting the
required test may be introduced so as to supplement the existing facilities and to overcome
the constraints faced in lab testing. The Committee would appreciate if such an initiative
is implemented at the earliest by the Directorate of NVBDCP after due consultation with
the State and district authorities in the JE infested Areas in Assam and U.P.
The Committee would like to point out that problem of other viruses, apart from JE
in eastern U.P. was deteted by NIV, Pune in 2006. By now, strategy for tackling these
viruses including vaccination should have been in place and being implemented. The
Committee, therefore, impresses upon the Department to take the required action on an
urgent basis. (Para 4.11)

118
CHIKUNGUNYA
The Committee appreciates the efforts made by the Department in intensifying multi
pronged approaches to contain the incidence of Dengue and Chikungunya cases. The
Committee feels that results of such measures would surely reflect in the numbers of cases
detected for the year 2008. The Committee would like to be apprised about the IEC
interventions undertaken in this regard.
The Committee’s attention is, however, drawn to the number of samples tested
against the suspected cases. The Committee notes that out of 59,535 suspected cases in 2007
only 13.2% were tested and of these 13.2% cases (7,850 samples tested), 23.3% of the
samples turned out to be confirmed cases. The Committee feels that 13.2% samples tested
is a fairly low number compared to the suspected cases figures and given the fact that more
than 23% turned out to be confirmed cases of the 7,850 tested samples, the figures could
be much more if the number of samples tested were increased.
The Committee apprehends that in such a situation, there are chances of there being
a large number of confirmed yet undetected cases. The Committee, therefore, suggests that
the Department should concentrate its efforts towards strengthening the testing lab
facilities so that maximum number of samples are tested. (Para 4.17)
NATIONAL T.B. CONTROL PROGRAMME
The Committee is happy to note that the Department had acted on its suggestion for
evolving a national forum where participating NGOs/PPs share their experiences of
providing services under RNTCP. The Committee hopes that 60-70 NGOs/PPs  participating
in the conference held on 29-31st January, 2008 must have given some concrete suggestions
for successful implementation of RNTCP. The Committee would like to be apprised about
the report on an independent evaluation of selected NGOs conducted by the Department.
The Department has also informed that in order to strengthen advocacy and social
mobilization activities under the programme, it is closely working with professional bodies
with the support and collaboration of media agency. The Committee would like to be
apprised in detail about its plan under the proposed initiative and the outcome of such
enterprise. (Para 5.7)
The Committee appreciates the Department’s concerns and efforts on the prevention
of MDR-TB through the administration of high quality DOTS and the Programme Division’s
concerns regarding the availability of effective affordable drugs. However, the Committee
feels that perhaps the Department has missed the import of the Committee’s comment
seeking further information on the status regarding the trials of the affordable drug for
treatment of patients with Multi-Drug Resistant TB, developed by the pharma company
Johnson & Johnson. The Committee would like to be apprised of the recent developments
in the matter in detail and the preliminary findings of the trial reports, if any, of the drug.
(Para 5.8)
NATIONAL LEPROSY ERADICATION PROGRAMME
The Committee observes that the proposal for providing incentive of Rs.5000/-  for
Reconstructive Surgery to leprosy disabled persons from BPL families and to 20 institutions
for conducting RCS is yet to be approved by EFC, though more than half of the Eleventh
Plan period is over. Such an undue delay, that too on a proposal already going on a pilot
basis in four Central institutions, is uncalled for. The Committee, therefore, urges upon the
Department to expedite the completion of all the procedural formalities. (Para 6.5)

119
NATIONAL PROGRAMME FOR CONTROL OF BLINDNESS
Last year, while welcoming new initatives under the National Programme for Control
of Blindness for the Eleventh Plan with focused attention on NE States and also in Bihar,
Jharkhand, J&K, Himachal Pradesh and Uttarakhand, the Committee had voiced its
apprehensions about undue delay in their actual initation. More than one year has passed
since then and third year of the Eleventh Plan period is also reaching its end. However, the
EFC Memo for the proposed projects remains under active consideration in consultation with
the Integrated Finance Division. The Committee has been drawing the attention of the
Department to the trend of undue delay in getting the formal approval for the proposed
projects from all the authorities. However, no serious introspection on such a vital aspect
seems to have been done by the Department. As a result, funds continue to get allocated
for proposed projects year after year with their status remaining unchanged and funds
remaining unutilized. Preparatory activities for implementation of new proposals on
provisional basis may also prove to be counter-productive at the end simply because there
is always a possibility of the proposed projects undergoing drastic changes. The Committee,
therefore, once again emphasizes that the Department has to evolve a mechanism
whereunder all procedural formalities for a proposed project are expeditiously completed and
the project takes off in the real sense. (Para 7.4)
The Committee appreciates the efforts of the Department for monitoring and
evaluation of IEC activities, the functioning of the Eye Banks in the country and other
initiatives to boost eye donations. However, the Department has not mentioned clearly the
time lines of initiation or the conclusion of the evaluation studies for IEC activities and also
the comprehensive evaluation of NPCB on review of Eye Banks. The Committee would like
to be informed about the outcomes of the evaluation studies at the earliest and furnish the
relevant reports for its perusal. (Para 7.7)
NATIONAL IODINE DEFICIENCY DISORDERS PROGRAMME
The Committee finds the Status Report on the achievement level of physical targets
set under the National Iodine Deficiency Disorders Control Programme for 2007-08 very
discouraging. Out of 6 States, i.e. Jharkhand, Bihar, West Bengal, Puducherry, Madhya
Pradesh and U.P. targeted for setting up State IDD Control Cells, Jharkhand, U.P. and
Puducherry have failed to do so. Similarly, only U.P. could set up IDD Monitoring lab
although all the aforesaid 6 States were targeted to do so. What is more disturbing is that
the target of analysis of 20,000 salt samples @ 500 samples per district from 40 districts
could not be undertaken due to non-availability of programme vechicle by administration.
The Committee refuses to accept such flimsy justifications. The Committee would like to
point out that proper planning means achievement of targeted goals within the prescribed
apprehensions about undue delay in their actual initation. More than one year has passed
since then and third year of the Eleventh Plan period is also reaching its end. However, the
EFC Memo for the proposed projects remains under active consideration in consultation with
the Integrated Finance Division. The Committee has been drawing the attention of the
Department to the trend of undue delay in getting the formal approval for the proposed
projects from all the authorities. However, no serious introspection on such a vital aspect
seems to have been done by the Department. As a result, funds continue to get allocated
for proposed projects year after year with their status remaining unchanged and funds
remaining unutilized. Preparatory activities for implementation of new proposals on
provisional basis may also prove to be counter-productive at the end simply because there
is always a possibility of the proposed projects undergoing drastic changes. The Committee,
therefore, once again emphasizes that the Department has to evolve a mechanism

120
whereunder all procedural formalities for a proposed project are expeditiously completed and
the project takes off in the real sense. (Para 7.4)
The Committee appreciates the efforts of the Department for monitoring and
evaluation of IEC activities, the functioning of the Eye Banks in the country and other
initiatives to boost eye donations. However, the Department has not mentioned clearly the
time lines of initiation or the conclusion of the evaluation studies for IEC activities and also
the comprehensive evaluation of NPCB on review of Eye Banks. The Committee would like
to be informed about the outcomes of the evaluation studies at the earliest and furnish the
relevant reports for its perusal. (Para 7.7)
NATIONAL IODINE DEFICIENCIES DISORDERS PROGRAMME
The Committee finds the Status Report on the achievement level of physical targets
set under the National Iodine Deficiency Disorders Control Programme for 2007-08 very
discouraging. Out of 6 States, i.e. Jharkhand, Bihar, West Bengal, Puducherry, Madhya
Pradesh and U.P. targeted for setting up State IDD Control Cells, Jharkhand, U.P. and
Puducherry have failed to do so. Similarly, only U.P. could set up IDD Monitoring lab
although all the aforesaid 6 States were targeted to do so. What is more disturbing is that
the target of analysis of 20,000 salt samples @ 500 samples per district from 40 districts
could not be undertaken due to non-availability of programme vechicle by administration.
The Committee refuses to accept such flimsy justifications. The Committee would like to
point out that proper planning means achievement of targeted goals within the prescribed
time-schedule. Problems like non-availability of programme vehicle could have been easily
handled. The Committee, therefore, would like to impress upon the Department once again
to make all conceivable efforts in active co-ordination with State authorities for timely
achievement of goals. (Para 8.2)
JANANI SURAKSHA YOJANA
The Committee notes with satisfaction that the Department also shares a similar
view point with the Committee regarding putting a fool-proof monitoring mechanism in
place so that misuse of funds intended for the beneficiaries under the programme are
avoided. The Committee observes that nearly a year has passed since the Department has
stated in its ATN regarding preparing a S tatus Note on the pr ocedure of verification of
claims and release of compensation package under Janani Suraksha Yojana as desired by
the Committee. The Committee hopes that the Department must have obtained specific
inputs from the States for the Status Note. The Committee would like the Department to
furnish the said Status Note for its perusal and also to be apprised about the follow-up
action, if any, taken so far.
The Committee would also like to point out that the Department must have finalized
the modalities for concurrent evaluation of the scheme in six major States regarding the
impact of the scheme on the lowering of Maternal Mortality Rate. However, the Department
has not given any timelines for commencing or concluding the assessment. It, therefore,
urges upon the Department to draw the plan of action for making the assessment within
a specified timeline and apprise the Committee about its findings. (Para 9.13)
ROUTINE IMMUNISATION PROGRAMME
The Committee hopes that by this time the District Level Household Survey III
(DLHS-III) must have been completed and the Report received. The Committee would like
the Department to furnish a copy of the Survey Report for its perusal as also the progress

121
made on the proposed course of action in the EAG and NE states indicated in the Action
Taken Note. (Para 9.15)
In the light of shortage of vaccines in the country, the Committee observes that
the reply of the Ministry that the units are not schedule M compliant is unacceptable. It
also notes that since there have been no reckonable reports of adverse impact of the
vaccines produced by the units over the years, though they have not been schedule M
compliant during the said period, the Committee feels that suspension of vaccine
producing license and putting the existing facilities to alternative uses would not be a
viable move. Therefore, the Committee reiterates its earlier stand and recommends that
the suspension of the license of these units be revoked and the units be allowed to
produce vaccines. (Para 9.17)

Para No. 02 : Irregularities in the procurement of Mosquito Bednets by procuring Agencies
Bids were invited for a total of 39 lacs Nos. of Bednets under 13 schedules through
Interntional Competitive Bidding (ICB) by RITES (Procurement Agency) on behalf of Directorate
of National Vector Borne Disease Control Programme (NVBDCP) Ministry of Health and Family
Welfare. Approval of the bidding document of the Bank was received on 20.9.20.0.4 and bids were
opened on 17th November, 2004 receiving bids from the 19 bidders.
1.1 Bid evaluation was conducted in the office of RITES in association with the representatives
from NVBDCP and recommendations were sent to NVBDCP on 16.12.20.0.4 seeking their
approval, subsequently Purchase Advisory Committee (PAC) held on 24.12.2004 and
NOAs were released in March, 2005 after approval of WorId Bank.
1.2 The NOAs were issued to the following five firms:

dated 17.9.2005 that they have supplied the imported bednets, instead of
manufactured in India by doing value addition, deliberately violating condition of the
contract.
5.0 Based on above, following actions were proposed to be taken by Director, NVBDCP:—
(1) M/s Vestersgaard Frandsen:
On receipt of letter dated 17.9.05 from M/s V estergaard that they have supplied the Bednets
which were not of Indian Origin, but imported one, following actions were taken
immediately by RITES:
(a) The firm was advised on 22.9.05 to take back the supplied quantity of 2,20,000
imported Mosquito Bednets from consignees with copy of the letter endorsed to
Director, NVBDCP and consignee.
(b) To avoid any payment against above supply, necessary direction was given to the
bank where LC was opened Bank has confirmed that no payment has been released.
(c) These quantities were inspected against three inspection certificates. The
firm surrendered the two inspection certificates issued to them and the third
certificate was not released by RITES to them to avoid claim of any payment.
After taking legal advise, a show cause notice was issued to them as to why action should
not be taken for cancellation of contract for violation of terms and condition.
Firm replied vide their letter dated 17.11.2005. As their reply was not found to be
satisfactory, following recommendations were sent to Director, NVBDCP on 25.11.2005.
(I) To initiate action for cancellation of contract.
(II) T o initiate action to declare firm ineligible for further contracts as per [TB clause 36.1(c)].
(2) M/s Chemical International:—
M/s Chemical International replied to final notice dated 2.9.2005 vide their letter
dated 19.9.2005. Legal advice was taken on their reply. As per the legal advice
dated 24.10.2005 the firm’s statements are contradictory. Therefore, it has been concluded
in the legal opinion that the information furnished by the firm is incorrect/doubtful in certain
situations and definitely required explanation. It was further advised to issue Show Cause
Notice as to why their contract should not be cancelled in view of incorrect/false/doubtful
information. Accordingly show cause notice was issued by Procurement Agency. On receipt
of reply from M/s Chemical International the Department not initiate any further action for
cancellation of the contract.

Audit Review and Findings
The regular receipts of complaints is a serious matter which apparently indicate a serious
lapses in the entire procurement process. M/s  RITES and Directorate NVBDCP has examined these
complaints, but these should have been investigated by other agencies like specia1 Audit of
Ministry/Vigilance. This has also been learnt that Government has ordered a CBI inquiry into the
charges of fraud and corruption on the malaria project supported by the World Bank.
The procurement scams were uncovered in the detailed implementation. Review launched by
the World Bank supported by the Government of India. As some of the procurement are under
scanner the purchase policy require to be reviewed/take steps to streamline procurement procedure
of the Ministry. This need to make procurement procedure foolproof at all leve1. All these projects
now also require to go through more stringent audit and performance review.
2(c) : Procurement of sub-standard bed nets worth Rs.12.91 crore
Directorate General of Health Services, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare, New Delhi
has given contract for procurement of 10 lakh bed nets (2 lakh double size+8 lakh single size) to
Hospital Services Consultancy Corporation (1) Limited (HSCC) for distribution in North Eastern
States under the National Anti Malaria Programme (renamed National Vector Disease Control
Programme in 2003). The Ministry accorded expenditure sanction for Rs.18.52 crore in
December, 2000 and an advance of Rs.4.32 crare was released to the HSCC during the year 2001.
HSCC placed the supply order in October, 2000 to three suppliers i.e. M/s R.S. Industries,
M/s ISSA Industries and Superior Industries. The supply of bed nets was to be completed within
90 days from the date of issue of order. The suppliers supplied 7,17,500 bed nets (double size
74,500 and single size 6,43,000) during February–April, 2001.
On receipt of a complaint of the supply of sub-standard bed nets, the Ministry issued
instructions in April, 2001 to the Government of Assam to withheld the distribution of bed
nets and deputed an officer of the rank of Deputy Secretary to investigate the matter.  By
the time, 1,63,000 bed nets (double size; 60,000 and single size 1,03,000) out of the above supply
had already been distributed by the concerned Stat Government. The samples lifted by the Deputy
Secretary from the State Government stores in Assam were tested at Indian Institute of
Technology, Delhi (IIT)  that showed that 3 out of 4 samples were inferior. Subsequently, more
samples were lifted in January, 2003 by an inspection team constituted with representatives of
various departments and IIT from the sealed stores in the presence of the representatives of HSCC
and supplier. These samples were also tested (March, 2003) at Indian Institute of Technology, Delhi
(IIT). The report received from the IIT revealed that the bed nets have failed to meet the Bureau
of Indian Standards (BIS) specifications, which was required as per the contract, in a number of
areas like bursting strength, GSM, number of holes etc. In addition, these bed nets did not carry
any label or marking to indicate the manufactures nor was the marking  “NAMP” supply not
for sale’ found on bed nets. An Expert Committee  constituted by the Ministry, further,
observed (June, 2003) that test results of the sample indicate a huge variation from the BIS
standards and large size of holes made these nets almost unfit for use.
Based on the above report, notices were issued (March, 2003) by HSCC to above firms to
lift the sub-standard bed nets lying in the godown at Guwahati. However, the concerned firms have
refused to lift the material stating that they should have been informed within four months of
supply. 14,500 double size and 5,20,000 single size bed nets totally 5,34,500 valuing at
Rs.9.45 crores were lying in the godown which was hired by the Department at Rs.89,000/- per
month. The avoidable expenditure on rent of godown alone amounted to Rs.51.67 lakh as on
November, 2005.

143
Audit Review Findings
Failure on the part of the Dte. of NVBDCP to ensure quality of bed nets as per BID
specification not only defeated the very objective of providing relief from severe malaria in
high risk and remote area but also resulted in wasteful and avoidable expenditure of Rs.4.32
more and Rs.51.67 lakh, respectively.
Para No. 03 : Failure of procurement system (Badly delayed system)
Under the provision of scheme Director, NVBDCP, Delhi not only provides financial
assistance but also provide assistance in the form of commodity namely DDT, bed nets, RD Test
kits, Malarias Fenthion, Tempephos Malathion/20 Malathion Tec Principhas, synthetic Pyrethroids
etc. These articles are procured by NVBDCP through PSU like HIL, HLL, RITES, HSCC for supply
to State Health Society from there goes to the user s. Director, NVBDCP  gives advances to these
procurement agencies as per the requirement with the approval of M/o Health FW. The IA has
since collected information in r/o 12 items supplied by the procuring agencies to the State health
Society. The information contained the procurement from action plan to the end of user. The
statement is annexed at Annexure-IV .
In view of the above it can be glanced that there are 35 to 40 stages from the date of
sanction of Action Plan to the end of user. Time involved in the present processing system is more
than 16 months. It may be seen from the Annexure-IV that the Action Plan approved in the
financial year 2005-06 and procurement action is being taken in next financial year defeated the
purpose of approval of Annual Action Plan. The urgent effective steps are required to be taken to
minimize the period from Annual Action Plan to the end of user. In the whole process of supplies
there is no internal check in the form of receipt and use by the official at the village level. There
is no proper system which can prove that the articles reaches up to lower level as per guidelines
given by the Government of India.
As such procurement system is not foolproof, this requires to be streamlined. This need to
make procurement procedure at all level foolproof and more stringent audit and performance
review.
In view of the non maintenance of record the audit is of the view whether these material
actually reaches to the user or not.

Para No. 07 : Status of V ector Borne Disease under NVBDCP.
(A) Status of Malaria and death cases during 2005 and 2006 (NE States).
Among the vector borne diseases, malaria stands apart as the leading killer disease. As per
the data given below its appeared that high percent age of malaria cases and deaths were reported
from the North East States—Arunachal Pradesh, Assam, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Tripura, Orissa,
West Bengal etc. Incidence of malaria cases and deaths in N.E. States, Orissa, West Bengal
continued to remain unabated during 2005-06.

(B) Status of Ka1a-Azar and death cases during 2001–06
Kala-Azar is a slow progressing indigenous disease caused by protozoan parasite Leishmania
donovani and spread by its vector sand fly. The National Health Policy has set the goal for
eliminating of Kala-Azar from the country by 2010. In last few years there is increasing trend as
indicated below:

the States/UTs.
The Dte. of NVBDCP have to recover 2% incidental charges for handling supplies of
material and equipments from the various state and UT Government. During scrutiny of records

146
its revealed that from 1991-92 to March, 2007 Rs.3,90,23,005 (Annexure-VIII) is lying outstanding
on account of 2% incidential charges from various States and UTs.

and UTs Government and this amount may be recovered from further grants released to States by
M/o Health and Family Welfare so that the outstanding amount could be liquidated at the earliest.

Para No.14 : 0utstanding earnest money/security depos it amount to the tune of
Rs.15,44,925/- as on 31.3.2007.
Government of India Note 2 below role 273 of GFR cleatly states that the earnest money
of all the successful tenderers shall be returned as early as possible after expiry of the bid validity
period but not later than 30 days of the award of contract.
It is the duty of the competent authority to ensure that the earnest money deposit is
refunded as early as possible to avoid any litigation by the unsuccessful bidders.
The earnest money of the successful bidders shall also be refunded after the security
deposit by way of FDR, DDs or Bank Guarantee etc. are furnished by the Tenderer as required
under Rule No. 273 of GIR.

Para : 1 : Loss due to abandoned work and Blockade funds Rs.71.57 lakh.
Test check of records of Hospital Engineer (Civil-I), PGIMER, Chandigarh revealed that the
work “Construction of 36 Nos Type II houses in sector 12, PGI. Chandigarh” was Administratively
approved by the Standing State Committee in its 41st meeting held on 22.11.97 at a cost of Rs.1.20
crore vide SHE, PGI letter No. PGI/Engg/SHE/98/8852 dated 7.9.98. The work was awarded to
M/S A.P. Construction Co., Mohali for Rs.91,44,157.66 vide H.E. (Civil-I) PGI letter No. 5236
dated 5.9.02. The date of commencement of this work was to be reckoned from the seventh day
of issue of the award letter and time allowed to complete the work was 15 months i.e. up to
11.12.03 stipulated date of completion but the agency could not complete the work within the
stipulated, time period and as such provisional, extension in time was granted by H.E (Civil-I) vide
letter No. 616-20 dated 27.3.04 up to 30.6.04 up to 30.6.04, but even in the extended period the
agency had failed to complete the work.
The last opportunity was given to the contractor vide H.E (Civil-I) PGI letter No. 2002
dated 9.7.05 to explain his position and submit the completion schedule within 7 days failing which
Institute will be constrained to initiate action under Clause 2 and 3 of the contract agreement. Till
date neither reply was received from contractor nor was work completed by him. An expenditure
of Rs.71,56,534/- had been incurred on this work as per “Works Register for 2005-06” and last
payment was made to the contractor vide Bill No. 201 dated 29.12.04 through 7th Running bill
for Rs.4,33,790/-  against the gross amount of the bill Rs.73,66,118.53/-. As such the work was
lying stand still since December, 2004 and no action under Clause 2 and 3 of the agreement had
been initiated so far. The penalty leviable under clause 2 of agreement comes to Rs.914415/- and
work is to be got completed at the risk and cost of the contractor. As with the passage of time

148
there is every possibility of damages to the structure and escalation in the cost of construction.
Besides this, unnecessary payment of the avoidable house rent to the eligible employees who would
have been allotted the said houses and loans of standard licence fee recoverable from employees

As the work remained stand still since 22.11.04 as per entry under on 7th running bill and
certificate recorded by the concerned J.E. thereon, an expenditure of Rs.71.57 lakh incurred on this
work was loss to the institution as this abandoned work does not  serve any purpose. Necessary
clarification may be given to internal audit.
PGIMER, Chandigarh
Para:- 3 Non initiation of action against faculty member Dr. R.S. Dhaliwal
Dr. R.S. Dhaliwal was Additional Professor, Cardiovascular and Thoracic Surgery
department. He carried out a number of open heart operations at PGI during the period 1994 to
2001. He used to purchase interior quality heart valves and accessories at very high rates on behalf
of patients under his treatment. He was asking them to deposit cash with unathorized agent who
supplied the material directly to Dr. Dhaliwal thereby ensuring that the patients could not check the
quality and quantity of  material supplied. The agent who was supplying the material was
Shri Surinder Singh Uppal owner of M/s Hitech Surgical Company 9/60, Rajouri Garden, New
Delhi allegedly having office in Sector 47, Chandigarh. M/s Northern Remedias (P) Ltd., SCO
No. 98, Sector 47, Chandigarh and M/s Paul Medical Hall. Sector 11, Chandigarh. Shri Uppal issued
bills for the sale of their heart valves in the these two firms which were found to be non existent
at the given address at Delhi.
The CBI registered Preliminary enquiry on 12.6.01 against Dr. R.S. Dhaliwal and others. After
investigation the CBI registered an FIR/RC on against Dr. R.S. Dhaliwal and others.
Dr. Dhaliwal performed 324 Tharacic Sargery during the period January, 1994 to July, 2001 of which
39 cases investigated by the CBI. The method adopted by Dr. Dhaliwala as by CBI was that he used
to personally take cash or drafts from patients for the valves at higher rates and passed on the
payments to Shri Uppal and Shri Singla. He also referred certain patients to Shri Uppal for supplying
valves who used to receive payments and supply the valves directly is Dr. R.S. Dhaliwal charging
higher rates. In certain cases the payments were taken for different brands of valves at a higher rate
and wrongful implantation was done. The investigation of CBI also mentions the personal diary of
Shri Uppal indicating the details of such cases, patients, profit and share given to Dr. Dhaliwal, which
is corroborated by GEQD and confessional statement of Shri Uppal. In the report it is mentioned that
Shri Uppal admitted before the  Magistrate that he has paid Rs.15 lakh to Dr. Dhaliwal for 160 such
patients out of the profits in addition to his own profits up to Rs.11 lakh.
The copy of the investigation report dated 22.3.2005 was sent the Secretary (Health) to
sanction prosecution under section 120B (Criminal conspiracy), 420 (cheating), 467, 468, 471
(forgery) of IPC and Section 13 of Prevention of Corruption Act. The Ministry asked the PGIMER
to send its comments on the case and to place the matter before the Governing Body for action.
The Governing Body discussed the matter in its meeting held on 31.8.05 vide Agenda Item
No. 14 and it was decided that the matter of sanction of prosecution against Dr. R.S. Dhaliwal
may be referred to the Ministry for taking decision in consultation with the Law Department. As
per decision of the Governing Body on the issue, the matter was referred to the Secretary, Health
and Family Welfare, Government of India, New Delhi vide letter Vig cell/PGI/F-545 dated 2.9.05.
The Government of India intimated that the Governing Body of the Institute is competent to grant
prosecution sanction against Dr. R.S. Dhaliwal now Professor and Head, Department of Cardic
vasscular and Thoracic Surgery, PGIMER, Chandigarh. Finally the matter was placed before the
Governing Body in its meeting no. 110 dated vide agenda item No. 17 where the Governing Body
authorized Director the PGIMER, Chandigarh who is a Member Secretary of the Governing sign
and convey the prosecution sanction on behalf of the Governing Body..

149
Final outcome of the case may be intimated to internal Audit.

other Income and Expenditure shown in the consolidated Annual Report/Annual Accounts. The

Vallabh Bahi Patel Chest Institute, Delhi
Para No.  22. : Non obtaining of performances security from suppliers firms Rs.11,50,000/-
In order to ensure due performance up the contract/agreement entered into with suppliers/
firms for purchase of Government Stores, it is necessary that performance security should be
obtained from every successful bidder. The performance security should be obtained for an amount
of five to ten percent of  the value of the contact. The VPCI, new Delhi have not been obtaining
the performance security from the suppliers. But only EMD amount is being treated as such. As
per rule 158(III) of GFR, the bid security of the successful bidder should be refunded immediately
and performances security (which normally higher amount) should be obtained. In one particular
case M/s. Shimadzu Medical Ltd. an amount of Rs.11,50,000/- as performances security which is
10% of the amount of value of stores purchased could have been obtained as the performance
security is to be obtained @5% to 10% of the value of the contract. In this case the EMD amount
of Rs.300000/- which was not refunded after bidding ceremony has been treated as security. Even
this securit:y has been refunded to  the firm before completion of five years stipulated period of
guarantee/warranty etc. Institute may elucidate the reasons for non obtaining of performances
security at specified rates from the suppliers and early release of security to the said firm.
Para No. 24:- Non charging of Building rent from SBI Ext. Counter.
During the course of audit it was noticed that the State Bank of India is opened one extension
counter in the office complex of the VPCI, Delhi. This portion of the building had been offered by
the Institute earlier to the Council of Scientific and Industrial Research (CSIE). Now the CSIR had
ready vacated their part of the building including the building of SBI extension counter.
But the extension counter is being free of rent ever since. The Institute is not taken the
congnigence of this reality. It is requested that this matter may be taken up with the SBI and rent
may be obtained from them including the arrear of rent. Such rent should be got determined in
consultation with PWD in terms of prevalent market rate.
Para No. 27:- Non maintenance of Liability Register and Outstanding Liabilities up to
Rs. 10,95,6,263/-.
On scrutiny of relevant records it was noticed that at the close of financial year 2006-07
Rs.10956263/- was outstanding liabilities. Proper accounting records for as many as 20 liabilities
has not been maintained by the institute for proper scrutiny and examination of liabilities. It is
necessary that the liabilities Register should be duly maintained. These liabilities may be cleared and
compliance may be shown to next audit.
Para No. 28:- Non Settlement of outstanding Interest amount of Rs.11,12,447/- under
scheme account.
The VPCI, Delhi has been receiving large number of funds from other source the
M/o Health and Family Welfare lunched nearly 100 of Projects/Scheme over the years. The element
of interest earned by the institute on such funds has been increased to Rs.11,12,447/- as on
31.03.07 and likely to be increased in the coming years. The item wise details of interest earned
on over the years in not identifiable. As such the amount may be taken as receipt of the institute
with the approval of competent authority.

ANNEXURE-VII
DETAILS OF PERKS AND OTHER F ACILITIES PROVIDED TO FACULTY
MEMBERS AT THE AIIMS, NEW DELHI
1. Housing Facility
The faculty members of the Institute have been categorized under essential category for
allotment of residential accommodation and accordingly; they are being allotted accommodation.
2. Salary
The pay scales of the faculty members of the AIIMS are as under:—
(i) Assistant Professor - Rs.11625-325-15200
(ii) Associate Professor - Rs.14300-400-18300
(iii) Additional Professor - Rs.16400-450-20900
(iv) Professor - Rs.18400-500-22400
– 25% of the sanctioned posts of Professor (sanctioned posts-110) will be placed in the
higher scale of Rs.22400-525-24500.
– The Non Practising Allowance is also being paid to the medical faculty @25% of basic
pay subject to basic pay + N.P.A. does not exceed Rs.29,500/-)
– Rs.2500/- per month is being paid to faculty members as Clinical Research Allowance.
– Two advance increments are being provided to the existing Additional Professors.
– All the faculty posts under reference above, carry usual allowances as admissible to
Central Government servants of similar status stationed in Delhi/New Delhi.
3. Conveyance Allowance
A sum of Rs.1650/- per month is being paid to the faculty members as Conveyance
Allowance.
4. Assessment Promotion Scheme
– 100% Assistant Professors who have completed 4 years of service as on 30th June
every year being promoted to the post of Associate Professor subject to their Fitness.
– 75% Additional Professors who have completed 4 years of service as on 30th June
every year being promoted to the post of Additional Professor subject to their Fitness.
– 50% Additional Professors who have completed 7 years of service as on 30th June
every year being promoted to the post of Professor subject to their Fitness.

206
5. Attendance of various Conferences/meetings etc. in each financial year
(i) Abroad
A maximum amounts of the tune of Rs.l,00,000/- is being granted to the faculty members
once in two years to the faculty members, subject to availability of funds, for attending
conference/Symposium/Workshop/meeting etc.
(ii) In India
Faculty members of the Institute were being permitted to attend two Conferences/
Meetings/Symposia/Workshop etc. in India/SAARC Countries with T A/DA The Registration Fee for
upto 3 Conferences. However, they are being permitted to attend 8 Conferences/Meetings/
Symposia/Workshop etc. on duty terms in each financial year. In addition to the above, they are
also being permitted to conduct examination, to deliver lectures in various institutions on duty
terms. 42 days duty leave is being granted to them for each financial year for the above purpose.
6. Learning Resource Allowance
A sum of Rs.20,000/- is being reimbursed to faculty members in each financial year for
purpose of books, journals, CDs, video films, floppies, colour films, mounting charges and
membership fee for national or international societies.
7. Newspaper Allowance
Newspaper allowance is being paid to the faculty members in accordance with their
entitlements as per orders of the Government of India.
8. Re-imbursement of Telephone Bills
The Director, Dean, Medical Superintendent, Chief of Centres and the Head of the
Departments are being allowed to reimburse the charge of telephone calls at par with the Central
Government Departments. All other faculty members are being allowed to reimburse the charges
of the telephone calls upto 1000 calls bi-monthly.

I. Augmentation of Patient Care Facilities
Cardio Pulmonary Rehabilitation
This is a new Unit which has been approved by the Governing Body of this Institute and is to
be developed during Eleventh Plan to provide comprehensive medical rehabilitation to patients
suffering from chronic respiratory diseases. A medical doctor and a physiotherapist have already
been trained in Cardio Pulmonary rehabilitation from Canada and they have initiated the work.
Expansion of 24 hours Emergency Facility
The existing 24 hours respiratory emergency section requires expansion due to increase in load of
emergency patients attending the hospital. The existing services that are provided to the patients
are insufficient to provide state-of-the-art facilities. Additionally, life-saving equipments have to be
upgraded in this section.
National Allergy, Asthma AND Clinical Immunology Centre
V .P Chest Institute is a well known for its patient care and research activities in the field of Allergy
Asthma and Clinical Immunology in the last 50 years. As the specialty of Allergy and Immunology
has made tremendous advancement during the last decade, Governing Body has approved the
establishment of a National Allergy, Asthma and Clinical Immunology Centre with subsidiary units
located in various parts of the Country.
Clinical Pharmacology
This is a new unit which has been approved by the Governing Body of this Institute to study the
respiratory pharmacology in relation to patient management.
Radio-diagnosis
This is an expansion to the existing facilities, which require upgradation and replacement of
obsolete radio imaging equipment. In addition, the new equipment is also proposed to be added for
more advanced diagnostics facilities for patient care.
Augmentation of diagnostic facilities
Due to the increase in patient care and related increase in the diagnostic investigations, the existing
facilities are inadequate and require up gradation and replacement, which is essential for patient care
management.
Running cost
This forms an important component to maintain optimal patient care services along with
management of the various diagnostic facilities provided. It inter alia  includes annual maintenance
of equipment, consumables, chemicals and related materials, testing kits etc. as required for
efficient patient care.
II. Renovation/Infrastructure development
Clinical Research Centre (Renamed as Viswanathan Chest Hospital)
This is the hospital wing of the Institute, which has been renamed as “Viswanathan Chest Hospital”
by the Governing Body of this Institute. The current hospital facilities housed in this wing had been

209
in place since last 50 years and there is an urgent need to upgrade the hospital building for
enhancement of patient bed strength and handling increasing patient load.
Resident hostel for Postgraduate students
The existing residential hostel for postgraduate (PG) students is insufficient to accommodate all the
resident doctors admitted to various postgraduate courses. In addition, due to the implementation
of the Oversight Committee Report with regard to enhanced seats in the reservation category in
postgraduate admission, the number of postgraduate resident will increase considerably (54%) and
there is an urgent need to increase the hotel facilities.
III. Augmentation of facilities of the Laboratories
The following laboratories of the Institute (Biochemistry, Physiology, Pharmacology, Microbiology,
Pathology, Mycology and Respiratory Virology) are in a dilapidated condition. These laboratories are
in existence since last 50 years without modernization and are not maintained as per the standards
required for running the facilities efficiently. These laboratories require adequate up-gradation and
renovation including life saving diagnostic facilities.
IV. Implementation of Oversight Committee Report
As per the implementation of the Oversight Committee Report, the number of Post Graduate seats
has to be increased by 54% from the existing level of seats. This requires creation of additional
infrastructure in terms of patient beds, other diagnostic and patient care management facilities,
augmentation of manpower especially in area of resident doctors (junior and senior), nursing and
other technical staff and augmentation of library and information sciences. Residential quarters are
to be provided to the additional nursing and other technical staff.
V. Introduction of New Courses and Induction of Junior/Senior Resident Students
V .P Chest Institute being a post graduate medical educational Institute, it is proposed to introduce
new post graduate medical courses in order to strengthen the specialty of Respiratory Medicine and
related sub-specialties.
VI Others
In order to augment the research facilities of this Institute, the existing Animal House has to be
upgraded to improve the research capabilities of the Institute.

ANNEXURE-IX
Proposed Training Courses/Workshops during 2008-09
I. Proposed Workshops
1. Workshop on “Yogic management of cardio-respiratory disorders” in collaboration with
Morarji Desai National Institute of Yoga on 5-6th April, 2008.
2. Workshop on “Diagnostic bronchology” to general medical practitioners on 29th June, 2008.
We have approached the Delhi Medical Council to accredit the workshop for
postgraduate training.
3. Workshop on “Recent Advances in Pulmonary Pathology” on 6-7 December, 2008.
Pulmonary pathology is a neglected area in our country and there are not many
pulmonary pathologists. In order to upgrade the skills of general pathologists, this
workshop is planned.
4. Workshop on “Respiratory Allergy: diagnosis and management” from 10-14 February, 2009.
This workshop is intended to train Pulmonologists, Pediatricians and ENT specialists
in scientific principles of immunotherapy in allergic rhinitis and bronchial asthma.
II. Proposed Orations
1. 10th “Professor Raman Viswanathan-VPCI Oration” on 6 April, 2008.
2. 4th “Professor Autar Singh Paintal Memorial Oration” on 24 September, 2008.

ANNEXURE-XIII
STATUS OF THE RECOMMENDATIONS OF THE JA VED CHOUDHARY
COMMITTEE AND CADRE REVIEW
Sl. Recommendations
No.
12
1 Removal of stagnation at SAG and HAG
levels
(i) Upgradation of 458 posts at SAG
level
(ii) One time In-situ promotion to SAG
to those completed five years
service on 31.3.2006 in NFSG grade
2 Enhancement of retirement age from 60
to 62 years
3 Terminal benefits
Action Taken
3
The proposal has been referred to the Committee
of Secretaries. Meeting fixed on 8.7.2008 has
since been postponed. No date for fresh Meeting
has been intimated.
The proposal for enhancement of retirement age
of Teaching, Non-Teaching and Public-Health
sub-cadre of CHS has been approved by Cabinet
and orders issued on 16.11.2006. Proposal in
respect of General Duty Medical Officers has
been referred to 6th Pay Commission. 6
th CPC is
not in favour of recommending a blanket increase
in the age of superannuation for all General Duty
Medical Officers belonging to CHS. The
Commission had recommended that the current
age of superannuation should be maintained.
Further except in the case of Scientists and
Medical Specialists, no extension should be given
in any other case. Tenure based posts should be
filled by incumbents who have sufficient period
of service left before the stipulated age of retirement.
The proposal for grant of benefit of added years
of service over and above that is admissible under
Rule 30 of CCS (Pension) Rules, 1972 was sent to
Department of Pensions for obtaining their approval
but was not agreed to. The matter was again
referred to D/o Pensions for re-consideration.
They have agreed to the proposal and had advised
to approach D/o Economic Affairs which is
administering New pension Scheme w.e.f. 1.1.2004
to explore possibility as to whether any special
dispensation could be given under the New
Pension Scheme. The matter was referred to D/o
P&PW for re-consideration but the same has not
been agreed to.

12
4 Leave/Deputation/Training Reserves
5 Improvement of Infrastructure
6 Enhancement of Non-Practising
Allowance
7 Enhancement of Book/research/Academic
allowance
8 Deterrant to unplanned departure of CHS
doctors from service
